# Verification log

This file tracks everything in the project that is not yet checked against a primary source or a
running system: rule IDs and tool flags that came from memory, behaviours that were read in
documentation but never run, open design questions, and corrections to the original plan.

How to use it:

- Add a row when you rely on something you have not verified. Do not state it as fact elsewhere in
  the repository until the row is closed.
- Status is one of `unverified`, `verified` or `corrected`.
- When you verify or correct an item, fill in the evidence (the command run, the page read, the
  version tested) and the resolution (what changed in the repository as a result), then change the
  status. Keep closed rows; they are the history.
- Shipping rule: no detector rule, tool flag or archetype row goes into a runtime reference while
  its row here is `unverified`, unless the reference marks it as unverified too.

## Plan corrections and inconsistencies

| Item | Status | Evidence | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| The plan says the workspace has "eight crates" but its layout lists ten: core, stats, probe, census, workload, sample, backends, parsers, report, cli | corrected | Counting the layout in the plan gives ten names | Ten crates were created. The seven that milestone M0 does not use are library stubs holding only a crate-level doc comment that states their future responsibility |
| The archetype data example uses the holders `thread` and `executor`, which are not in the holder vocabulary (static/global, registration-table, container-field, thread-local, blocked-stack, closure/continuation, native-handle/global-ref, cycle-only) | corrected (M6) | The example is inconsistent with the closed holder enum in `dla-core` | The enum stays closed. `data/archetypes.toml` maps `thread` and `executor` to `blocked-stack` and `registration-table` (L-WORKER); see the M6 section below |
| Plan correction (M6): the plan says a `Stop` hook in `worker.md` becomes `SubagentStop`. Plugin agents ignore the `hooks` frontmatter field, so this cannot work for a plugin | corrected | Verified empirically: a plugin agent with `Stop` and `PreToolUse` frontmatter hooks ran Bash and neither marker file was created (run with the Claude Code CLI in print mode against a throwaway plugin). The sub-agent docs say the same for plugin subagents (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents) | Decide in M6 between a `SubagentStop` entry in `hooks/hooks.json` (conflicts with the rule that plugin hooks hold only `SessionStart`) and a hook in the SKILL.md frontmatter (documented to fire for plugin skills; the subagent case is unverified). Agents carry no hooks until then |

## Research facts that are not verified by running them

These came from documentation or a changelog and could not be exercised in the build environment.
The remaining research facts were checked by running the Claude Code CLI.

| Plugin agent frontmatter: `permissionMode`, `hooks`, `mcpServers` and `initialPrompt` are ignored for plugin agents, and the other listed fields are honoured | unverified | Read in the Claude Code plugin and sub-agent docs. The `hooks` part is verified: hooks in a plugin agent do not fire (see the M6 plan correction above). The other fields were not exercised | Agents carry no hooks. Recheck the supported field list when raising the minimum Claude Code version |
| Hooks on Windows: shell-form hooks run in Git Bash when installed and in PowerShell otherwise; exec form needs a real executable | unverified | Read in the hooks docs; the build environment is Linux | The README now says Windows needs Git for Windows. CI runs `tests/shim/run.sh` under Git Bash on a Windows runner. Still untested: a machine without Git Bash, where `bash "..."` fails as a non-blocking error; consider a Node-based hook or two entries with `shell` set |
| Minimum Claude Code versions per feature (plugin options 2.1.83, plugin data 2.1.78, plugin `bin/` 2.1.91, plugin skill hooks 2.1.94, exec-form hook args 2.1.139, Stop-hook continuation cap 2.1.143, `${user_config.*}` shell-form rejection 2.1.207, `options` enum 2.1.271, validating both manifests 2.1.289) | unverified | Mapped from changelog lines, not tested on old versions | README states 2.1.94 as the floor. Install the floor version in CI once and run the plugin checks against it |
| Minimum Node for memlab: 18 in practice (its browser driver requires 18; the core package declares 12.6) | unverified | Package `engines` fields; no memlab run | README recommends 20 or 22. Run a memlab scenario on the floor version before M-series work that depends on it |

The smoke subset is run by hand from a session |
| `onFailure: "block"` has no effect on Stop-type hooks, so Stop hooks always fail open | unverified | Docs statement; fail-open behaviour for crashes and non-zero exits was verified | Recheck on the version raised to in the future |

Non-sensitive values only | SKILL.md stage 1 runs `dla run init --budget ${user_config.time_budget} --session-id ${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}` |

## Open design questions

| Item | Status | Evidence | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| Privilege tiers 1 to 4 (user decision: escalating by intrusiveness) | corrected | Defined in `skills/detect-leak-anywhere/references/safety-and-effects.md`, the `consent.rs` module doc and the `privilege_ceiling` description in `plugin.json`: 1 builds and runs project code in a scratch copy, 2 observes a process DLA did not start, 3 debugger or kernel level, 4 captures memory contents. Tiers 3 and 4 have no command yet (later adapters) | Revisit the `privilege_ceiling` default (still 4; a lower default may be safer) when the first tier 3 or 4 adapter lands |
| Source of the session ID for `dla run init`: `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` or `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` | verified | The Bash tool receives no such variable, but the skill body substitutes `${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}` (observed: a skill body containing `${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}` rendered the session ID, while `env \| grep SESSION` in the Bash tool printed nothing). The CLI still reads both variables and falls back to `unknown` | Resolved: SKILL.md passes `--session-id ${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}` explicitly |
| Checklist answers accept any evidence ID (M6) | unverified | `dla ledger resolve intake.* --status answered --evidence E<n>` is accepted with any evidence ID, whatever kind of evidence it names | Bind each intake item to the evidence kinds that may answer it and refuse the rest |
| No file locking for concurrent ledger writers (M4) | unverified | Ledger appends from two processes at once can interleave | Per-worker ledger slices plus `dla ledger merge` (M4) remove the concurrent writers |

## Benchmark and evaluation (M1)

| Item | Status | Evidence | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| Runs without dla (the `no-skill` and `prose` ablations) have no ledger, so graders read `findings.json` instead | verified | `bench/GRADING.md` ("`findings.json` (runs without dla)"); both graders ran on the fixture results in `bench/runner/testdata/` and produced the same metrics | Grading reads `ledger.jsonl` when it exists and `findings.json` otherwise. |
| Grading contract clarified after the two graders (`dla-bench grade` and `bench/runner/grade.py`) disagreed on the same results directory | corrected | The two graders produced different `metrics.json` on the same results; the contract rule is in `bench/GRADING.md` | The contract was fixed first, then both graders were changed to match; CI runs both on `bench/runner/testdata/` and compares them |
| Benchmark isolation: each child runs under Claude Code's Bash sandbox with Bash allowed inside it (`autoAllowBashIfSandboxed`), no network, writes confined to the working copy, `cache` and `tmp`; the file tools and the sandbox are denied the repository, the answer keys, the results directory and the parent configuration. Sibling working copies under the work root remain readable but hold no answers | verified | Settings in `bench/runner/src/run.rs` (`sandbox_settings`); `bench/README.md`, section "Isolation and cost"; the runner's `--probe-prompt` option exists to check the sandbox with a custom prompt | Documented, including the known gaps (parallel runs can read each other's working copies; the sandbox does not confine plugin hooks) |
| JVM attach tools (`jcmd`, `jmap`, `jcmd -l`) do not work inside the benchmark sandbox: the JVM writes its attach socket and `hsperfdata` files to `/tmp`, which the sandbox keeps read-only | verified | A sandboxed session built `jvm-order-cache`, started its driver in the background and ran `jcmd -l`: no JVM was listed, and `jcmd <pid> GC.class_histogram` found no process. Maven in `jvm-billing-private-repo` likewise failed to write its lock file to `/tmp` | The M3 harness does not attach: its JVM agent reads NMT, the class histogram and the native trim in-process through the DiagnosticCommand MBean (see the M3 section). `dla sample --pid` on a JVM still needs a writable `/tmp` for the attach socket: decide in M4 how sandboxed sessions get one while keeping the orchestrating session's files and the answer keys unreadable, then re-probe. No M1 baseline run used these tools |
| The first M1 no-skill baseline was discarded: the orchestrator had denied the child sessions Claude Code's whole shared temporary directory (`/tmp/claude-<uid>`), so every Bash call failed to record its shell state and `TMPDIR`-based tools such as valgrind could not start, while every run still finished normally | corrected | Every transcript of the discarded run showed `/tmp/claude-0/cwd-...: Read-only file system` after each Bash call; a probe denying only the orchestrating session's own subdirectory showed none, a writable `TMPDIR` and a working valgrind | The baseline was rerun in full; `dla-bench run` now refuses a deny path that covers the shared temporary directory |
| Plugin eval constraints: a plugin directory containing hard-linked files is refused, so cargo's `target/` must live outside the plugin directory | verified | `claude plugin eval` refused the repository while `target/` held hard links; it ran after the build moved to another directory | A by-hand eval run builds with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` outside the plugin directory and copies only the binary to `target/debug/dla`. CI has no eval job |
| Plugin eval constraints: running as root, the eval's working directory was read-only here | unverified | Observed in this build environment (root user); not tried as a non-root user | Run the eval smoke subset by hand as a non-root user and note the result here. CI has no eval job |
| Plugin eval constraints: the plugin's `bin/` was not on `PATH` inside the eval | unverified | Observed in this environment's eval run; the README's 2.1.91 floor assumes `bin/` is on `PATH` in normal sessions | The skill and fixtures call `dla` through the shim; recheck on a normal session and in a by-hand eval run |
| Plugin eval constraints: the model benchmark does not run in CI | decided | The `plugin-eval` job was removed in M7 by the user's decision: no model benchmark and no credential in CI. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` keeps the mechanical jobs | Model gates are run by hand from a session and committed as results (`bench/GRADING.md`). The eval smoke subset is run by hand the same way |
| Privilege tier definitions (1 to 4); the benchmark's headless consent grants `1-4` | corrected | The tiers are now defined (see "Open design questions"). `bench/runner/src/run.rs` still sets `DLA_CONSENT_TIERS=1-4` for plugin ablations; the M3 commands the fixtures need run at tier 1 | Narrow what the benchmark grants when a fixture policy needs less than the full set |
| Fixture caveat: the sawtooth period of `python-graph-churn` and the 70/30 split of `python-ingest-two-leaks` depend on CPython object sizes | unverified | Object sizes differ between CPython versions and platforms | Re-measure on the Python version the benchmark runs under, and loosen the key if the numbers move |
| Fixture caveat: the JVM fixture drivers print heap-after-GC checkpoints, which are the growth signal the driver gives a run | unverified | The checkpoints depend on the JVM's collector and flags | Check that the growth is still visible when the JDK or collector changes |

| ruff `B019` precision | verified | `B019` is on by default and also fires on an instance-method cache that is bounded (`lru_cache(maxsize=N)`), so a hit is not proof of an unbounded cache | Detector `precision_prior` is low; the census and writers/removers review decide |

Retry where extraction is allowed |
| Sonar `S5164` and `S2095` | unverified | Sonar needs a server; none is available here, so neither rule was seen | Both rows stay `verified = false`; the backend is `blocked` as server-only since the Kotlin/Gradle static scope was limited to server-free analyzers (see the M7 row on Sonar) |
| Rules verified to exist but never seen firing: golangci-lint `contextcheck` and `sqlclosecheck`, `spancheck`, staticcheck `SA1015` and `SA5005`, SpotBugs `ODR_*` and `SIC_*` | unverified | Each ID is in the installed tool's rule list or documentation; no M1 fixture triggers it | Every row carries `verified = false` (the ID exists, but a rule never seen firing is not counted as verified); flip each flag once it fires on a real sample |
| DLA-owned rules are Rust over tree-sitter syntax trees, not Semgrep rules | corrected | The plan assumed Semgrep for rules no tool covers; `crates/dla-backends/src/dla_rules` implements them with tree-sitter and unit tests with positive and negative snippets | Backend `dla` is always available and needs no install |
| `dla ledger annotate` | corrected | The plan names no command for recording a holder's writers and removers; the model needs one | `dla ledger annotate F<n> --writer file:line --remover file:line --note` was added; it appends a `finding_annotated` record and changes no state |
| JVM class-file census reader | corrected | `crates/dla-census` contains a new class-file reader written for this project; no external code was ported | Used when class files exist; tree-sitter otherwise |
| Chain-head-growth rules suggest no archetype | corrected | An object chain linked through ordinary fields from a global head is not a captured scope, so `L-CAPTURE` does not describe it | `suggested_archetype` is empty for `dla/jvm-chain-head-growth`, `dla/node-chain-head-growth` and `dla/python-chain-head-growth`, in the detector rows and the rule table |
| Detector `verified` flags | corrected | The flags in `data/detectors/*.toml` had been `true` for rules this file lists as unverified | `verified = true` now means the rule ID was seen in the installed tool version; every rule this file lists as unverified (CodeQL, `SuspendFunInFinallySection`, `contextcheck`, `sqlclosecheck`, `spancheck`, `SA1015`, `SA5005`, `deferInLoop`, `ODR_*`, `SIC_*`, `OS_OPEN_STREAM*`, `ThreadLocalUsage`, `ClassCanBeStatic`, `SIM115`) and both Sonar rows are `false`. `crates/dla-workload/tests/detector_flags.rs` checks this |
| Recall of the DLA-owned rules and the census | unverified | They were developed and tested with the 20 M1 fixtures available, so the static recall measured on those fixtures is optimistic | M7's new fixtures, written after the rules, are the real test of generality; do not quote M1 recall as a general figure |

## Dynamic core (M3)

Thresholds first: every number below that decides a verdict is provisional until the M7
calibration, because no calibration data exists yet.

| Item | Status | Evidence | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| All dynamic thresholds are provisional: the slope gate (8 points, alpha 0.05, half-split ratio 0.5, rise above 3 noise units), alloc-test (extra slope at least 0.5 of the main slope, purge release at least 0.5 and retention at most 0.2 of the growth, 1% of the level as the smallest material rise; the noise of the extra window is the larger of its own MAD and the main window's scatter around its trend; a byte-column rise under 32 KiB is a plateau, `MIN_BYTE_RISE`, a placeholder) and the residual threshold (max of 0.25 of the total and 3 noise per x-span) | unverified | Taken from the plan; no fixture sweep has tuned them. Every trend, alloc-test and residual result carries `calibrated: false` or a limitation saying so | Calibrate on the M7 fixtures; do not quote a gate verdict as more precise than that. Counters use the every-delta rule, which has no threshold |
| The every-delta rule for counters | verified | CPython's refleak checker is `all(delta >= 1 for delta in deltas)` (`check_rc_deltas` in `Lib/test/libregrtest/refleak.py`, read in the 3.11, 3.12 and 3.13 installs here). `dla trend` applies it to the deltas between consecutive post-warm-up points and adds the delta from the last warm-up point | The rule is strict: one non-growing step, including after a collection, fails a counter column. Whether a share of zero deltas should be tolerated is an M7 question |
| The plan's shape classification order and plateau test | corrected | Checking `flat` before `spike` makes `spike` unreachable (a spike that returns has a Sen slope near zero; one 500 among 100 with noise reads flat). The plateau alternative "first-half Mann-Kendall p below alpha" let flat pseudo-noise through (xorshift seed 7, n 30, S 101, p 0.037, rise 0.98 against noise 0.91 was classified plateau, then stair) | `classify` checks `spike` first, `plateau` needs a first-half rise above the noise tolerance, and `stair` and `steady` need a total rise above it. Test: `crates/dla-stats/tests/shapes.rs::shapes_and_gate_table` |
| Sen confidence interval indexing: "slope at index floor(M1) and ceil(M2)" is ambiguous between 0-based and 1-based | corrected | Implemented as Gilbert's rank form: 0-based floor(M1)-1 and ceil(M2), clamped, which makes the interval symmetric. Hand-worked check in `crates/dla-stats/src/trend.rs` (x 1..5, y 1 3 2 5 4: slope 0.875, 95% interval minus 1 to 3) | Closed |
| Exact Mann-Kendall p-values and the normal CDF | verified | The exact one-sided p for n up to 10 without ties was checked against the histogram of S over all n! permutations for n 4 to 8 (monotone n 8 gives 1/40320, n 10 gives 1/3628800). The CDF (no tables) matches Phi(1.96), Phi(3) and the tails to 1e-8 relative | Closed |
| A late-onset gauge (flat first half, rising second half) passes the gate: the half ratio is undefined when the first-half slope is not positive, and the half passes if the second-half slope is positive. The counter gate counts only post-warm-up points, plus one delta from the warm-up tail | unverified | Design choices of `dla-stats` where the plan was silent; unit tests pin them | Revisit with the M7 fixtures |
| `dla trend --against` and N-SCALE: the plan does not say how "plateaus in the extra-cycle test" is judged when only two series are known | unverified | Read as the fresh column's own shape being `plateau`. The gauge gate needs a non-decaying second half, so a plateau-shaped column almost never passes: growth in fresh keys that stays quiet in repeated keys is a `leak` verdict in practice, and N-WARM plus N-SCALE support appears only for a plateau-shaped column that still passes the gate. A cache that fills and stops (plateau in fresh, flat in repeated) lands in "neither grows" and supports N-WARM only | Pass the extra-cycle series to `dla trend` explicitly if N-SCALE is wanted for those; threshold decision for M7 |
| The `GrowthSummary.gate` doc comment says the gate is absent on live samples | corrected | The ledger rule (confirm needs `gate: pass` among test, live or dump evidence) needs it on live evidence, and `dla trend` always writes it; `dla sample` itself writes none | The sentence is gone from the doc comment in `crates/dla-core/src/trend.rs`. Closed |
| Workspace lint `unsafe_code = forbid` is incompatible with the libproc and Win32 calls that `dla sample` needs | corrected | `dla-sample` sets its own lints (`unsafe_code = deny`, `missing_docs` warn) and allows unsafe only in `os/linux.rs`, `os/macos.rs`, `os/windows.rs`. `cargo clippy -p dla-sample --all-targets -- -D warnings` is clean on Linux, x86_64-pc-windows-msvc and aarch64-apple-darwin | Closed. The generated Rust and C drivers contain `unsafe` too but are written into the scratch copy only, outside the workspace lint |
| macOS and Windows sampling | unverified | The code compiles and passes clippy for both targets; the runtime tests only run in CI. The unit of the `proc_listchildpids` return value on macOS was not checked on a Mac (the count is clamped defensively). A Windows pid that cannot be opened (access denied) yields only `os.threads` | First real test is CI on those runners |
| Linux sampling details | verified | `Pss_Anon` needs kernel 4.14 or newer and is reported missing with a limitation otherwise; kernel threads lack `VmRSS` and its parts. A zombie counts as gone | Closed |
| cgroup v1 here, and its figures | verified | `/proc/self/cgroup` shows `4:memory:/process_api/<id>/claude-code-bash` and `/sys/fs/cgroup/memory` is a v1 mount; that path does not exist under the mount (cgroup namespace), so sampling reads the mount root. A sample returned `cgroup.current` about 1.69e9 against `cgroup.anon` 675840: v1 `usage_in_bytes` includes page cache (Python runs showed about 2.2 GB, 2 GB of it file cache) | `cgroup.*` columns have scope `container` and `cgroup.current` is not comparable with `cgroup.anon`. v2 hosts (`memory.current`, `memory.stat`) are implemented but unrun here |
| JVM attach is not needed by the harness | verified | In the 24 agent runs and the driver run, NMT (`vmNativeMemory summary`), `gcClassHistogram` and `systemTrimNativeHeap` worked in-process through the DiagnosticCommand MBean on JDK 21.0.12 with no attach (trim output "RSS+Swap: 74892K->69208K") | `dla sample --pid` on a JVM still uses jcmd and the writable `/tmp` problem of the benchmark section. jcmd polls take 1 to 1.5 s (a JVM start per call), so a 200 ms sample gets JVM columns on about every fifth tick; `dla trend` tolerates missing values |
| JVM reflection into `java.*` | corrected | `setAccessible` on JDK internals fails on JDK 17+ without `--add-opens`, so the plan's bounded walk over instance fields via reflection goes through the Collection, Map and array APIs for JDK containers; reflection reads only application-class fields. Stable counts on `shop.OrderCache` and the `gateway.RequestContext` chain (3 objects per request) | Closed |
| `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS` handling | verified | The agent is appended to any existing value; every JVM, including `javac` and `jar`, prints "Picked up JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS". The agent skips tool JVMs by `sun.java.command` (checked with javac and jar). `jvm:main:build.sh:run` runs the unit-test JVM and the program JVM at every point; both dump and `dumps.rs` picks the right one | Gen records a note in the manifest gaps |
| Python 3.13 coverage and probe walks | corrected | `sys.monitoring` `PY_START` with `DISABLE` works for coverage, but code objects with `co_filename` `<frozen ...>` resolve under the cwd through realpath, so stdlib frozen modules were reported as project functions until they were excluded before the `DLA_PROJECT` prefix test. `gc.get_referents` on a function leads to its globals and so to the whole module graph; the walk now follows only a function's closure and defaults (and a bound method's `__self__`) and skips types, modules and code (walks of 3000 to 300k objects instead of the interpreter) | Closed |
| Python tracemalloc and the pre-GC twin | verified | `python-graph-churn`: pre-GC traced 3469 to 7665 KB while the post-GC value stays near 2600 KB. A trend on `py.traced.pre_gc` passes where the post-GC column is flat, which is the signal the alloc-test gc test needs. `py.traced` counts only allocations made after tracemalloc started | Closed |
| pytest-memray as a driver | verified | pytest-memray 1.11 `limit_leaks('0B')` fails a leaking test with the leaking sites (`builder.py:18/27/36` for `python-report-memo`); it needs memray installed (here from the survey pylib on `PYTHONPATH`). `dla harness gen --driver` writes the variant only when `pytest_memray` imports | Absent: a recorded gap, the standard-library driver is used |
| goleak, dhat-rs, tokio metrics and the JUnit 5 variant | unverified | `go.uber.org/goleak` is not in the offline module cache (only automaxprocs, multierr, zap under go.uber.org); no `dhat` or `tokio` crate is in the local cargo registry; `junit-platform-console-standalone` is not on the fixtures' classpaths. The goleak template was only compile- and run-checked against a stub module; the tokio `num_alive_tasks` API (stable from tokio 1.39) is recalled, not checked. `dla harness gen` writes no JUnit or dhat variant in M3 and records a gap | User decision: use them when available, with the standard-library driver as the fallback. The benchmark measures the fallback. memray attach captures are tier 4 and not used |
| Go command mode: `os.Exit` | verified | `os.Exit(0)` inside a function run by `go test` panics ("unexpected call to os.Exit(0) during test") in Go 1.24.7, so the harness recovers it and counts a normal end. `os.Exit(n)` with n not 0 ends the process without a dump; only `tick` records (`DLA_TICK_MS`) survive | Recorded as a limitation |
| Command-mode exit dumps miss what the workload dropped before exit | verified | `go-buffer-pool`: the pool and map are garbage once `main` returns, so the exit dump shows no plateau (`go.heap_live` about 160 to 176 KB). `rust-batch-buffers`: live at exit 544 B, peak 9.46 MB, RSS 10.9 MB. `native-glyph-cache`: live 4096 B because the cache is destroyed | A high-water mark shows in the peak columns (`native.peak_bytes`, and `os.vm_hwm`, which is also sampled while the process runs: test `native_peak_columns_carry_a_pool_plateau_that_live_bytes_hide`) or in driver mode, not in the live columns. Node ticks fire only when the event loop turns, so a synchronous main produces none |
| `os.vm_hwm` from the sampler | corrected | The plan has it always come from the sampler peak. With tree sampling that is wrong for wrapped runs: `go test` gave about 104 MB (compile and link processes) against a 9 to 49 MB test process (run on `go-subscription-workers`) | When the dump record's pid is not the sampled process, only the agent's own `os.*` values are used (a series limitation says so); for a bare process the value is the larger of the agent's and the sampler's peak. The sampler runs every 100 ms (the plan says 200 ms) |
| Node WeakMap bookkeeping | corrected | A WeakMap or WeakRef table keeps its capacity after entries die, so tracking registrations in one inflates `node.heap_used` linearly with the tracked count | The agent stores its records as hidden symbol properties on the tracked objects and sweeps dead WeakRefs before the last collection. `--expose-gc` is accepted in `NODE_OPTIONS` on Node 22 (v22.23.4) but the agent does not need it |
| Allocator shim design and cost | corrected | The plan stores a hash of the first project frame from `backtrace()`; a hash cannot select a project frame without symbols. The shim stores the first 8 return addresses and `dla` picks the first project frame after `addr2line` (in the Rust fixtures the project frame is at depth 4 or 5; `rust-tree-parent-links`: `src/section.rs:16`). Cost: 8 threads doing 32M allocations ran about 56 times slower (16.5 s against 0.3 s), from atomics and lock contention rather than `backtrace()` (`DLA_SHIM_SAMPLE=1000` did not help); `rust-batch-buffers` at step 220 took about 3.5 minutes for 9 points | The step rule now targets twice the workload default (the plan says four; commit "Size harness points at twice the workload default"). The shim adds a few MB of RSS, so compare `os.rss` only within shim runs. The macOS interpose path and the jemalloc purge were never built or run here. Windows has no shim |
| `malloc_trim(0)` as the purge discriminator | verified | `rust-batch-buffers` 10.87 MB to 7.00 MB (about 36%), `native-glyph-cache` 8.0 MB to 7.3 MB, while the leaks stay put: `rust-tree-parent-links` n 8000 unchanged at 88.95 MB, `native-frame-decoder` unchanged | Thresholds provisional (first row) |
| `raw_sites` and the dump record shape | corrected | Spec 1.5 types `raw_sites` as a map of numeric maps; the real shim emits `{stack key: {live_bytes, live_blocks, allocs, frames: [{a, m, o}]}}` plus `modules: [{path, bias}]` with hex strings. Typed parsing failed the whole record (test `shim_raw_sites_do_not_lose_the_record`) | `raw_sites` and `modules` are `serde_json::Value` in the dump type. The spec form `{metric: {addr: bytes}}` is reported as a limitation |
| `DLA_ROUNDS` semantics | corrected | A first reading set it to warm-up plus iterations plus extra; every driver template computes total rounds as `DLA_WARMUP + DLA_ROUNDS` | `DLA_ROUNDS` is the measured rounds after the warm-up (`iterations + extra`). The Python driver writes no warm-up marker, so `dla` treats the first `warmup` checkpoint lines as warm-up |
| Sampled runtime heap columns | corrected | `column_meta` marks live-readable heap columns as `after_gc`; a sampled `jvm.heap_used`, `go.heap_live` or `node.heap_used` is not read after a collection (test `series_uses_the_catalog_and_never_claims_after_gc`) | `dla sample` sets `after_gc = false`. Whether `dla trend` should gate those columns at all is open |
| Sampling backends | unverified | `--inspect=0` has no port on the command line; it is resolved from the process's listening sockets (`/proc/<pid>/net/tcp`, Linux only), so macOS and Windows need `--counters node-inspect=<port>`. SIGUSR1 kills a process without a handler, so it is sent only to an executable named `node` or `nodejs`, and only on an explicit `node-inspect` with no inspector found (tests `signalling_is_refused_for_a_process_that_is_not_node`, `auto_never_signals_a_node_without_an_inspector`). The Go pprof goroutine dump contains the profiling request's own goroutine, so `go.goroutines@net/http/server.go:<line>` is at least 1 and constant | Python 3.13 has no `sys.remote_exec` (3.14 adds it): `python-remote` ran only as the injected script under `python3` plus parsing, so the real path is unverified until CI has 3.14. Tokio metrics are readable only in-process; `dla sample` says so |
| Ledger version 2 and the M3 rules | corrected | New ledger lines are `schema_version` 2 and replay under the M3 rules; version 1 lines replay under the M0 rules (tests `version_one_lines_replay_under_the_m0_rules`, `a_version_one_ledger_stays_valid_and_new_lines_are_version_two`). A hypothesis starting with `N-` must be one of the seven ids (the `N-POOL` CLI test became `N-HWM`). A discriminator naming a finding outside the family does not count, for refute or for a non-leak confirm (the plan states it for refute only). `leak-refutation-required` means the leak-side root is not refuted | Closed |
| Residual regions | corrected | The plan lists four regions including `process`; `ResidualRow.region` is the finding region (heap, off-heap, non-heap) and has no `process`, so `dla gap --residual` writes three rows. Resolving an earlier open item "citing the new residual evidence" is not possible with the ledger API: the resolution is attached to a finding (the residual-origin finding first, else the newest id), which is wrong when an older finding closed the gap | Revisit with M7 calibration; a `Resolution` that carries evidence would remove the heuristic. Open items opened by `--residual` cannot link to a finding because none exists yet |
| Native and Rust at-exit figures and the C driver | unverified | Fixture README RSS figures (about 2.5 MB for `native-glyph-cache`) are lower than shim-run RSS (7 to 8 MB). The shim is not loaded for statically linked executables, Go, or runtimes with a private allocator (jemalloc or tcmalloc linked statically, JVM arenas, pymalloc below 512 bytes); musl is untested | Recorded as blind spots in `docs/what-dla-cannot-see.md` |
| Dynamic recall on new code | unverified | The agents, probes and tests were developed against the 20 M1 fixtures | M7's new fixtures, written after the code, are the real test of generality |
| Graders and `dla ledger annotate --holder/--site` | corrected | GRADING.md took a finding's holder and site from its `finding_added` record only; M3's annotate can fill both later, so the native-frame-decoder runs, which set the expected holder by annotation, scored 0/3 for grounded attribution | Both graders apply a `finding_annotated` holder or site the finding was added without; M1 and M2 regrade to identical files; tests in `grading_rules.rs` and `test_grade.py` |
| The mechanical acceptance policy on a two-edge cycle | unverified | `dla-bench dynamic` confirms the `children` edge of rust-tree-parent-links' `Rc` cycle (`section.rs:11`) because the allocation sites are nearer to it; the fixture expects the `parent` back-link | Listed as an expected failure in `dla-bench dynamic` with that reason; choosing the edge to weaken needs reading the code. The M3 benchmark model found the right edge in 2 of 3 runs |
| Group kills under dash | corrected | `sh -c "kill -KILL -- -pgid"` fails in dash ("Illegal number"), so harness process-group kills never ran; the timeout test passed only through the follow-up child kill | `kill -s KILL -- -pgid`; `/usr/bin/kill -KILL -- -pgid` (used where the binary is called directly) accepts both |
| Socket read timeouts on macOS | corrected | A read that hits `SO_RCVTIMEO` returns `WouldBlock` (EAGAIN) on macOS and `TimedOut` on Linux and Windows; the inspector client compared against `TimedOut` only (CI, macos-latest) | Both kinds count as a timeout, and a timeout caused by the overall deadline fails the exchange instead of returning a partial body |
| Harness execution on Windows | unverified | The harness has no allocator shim on Windows, no process groups and no `/proc`; command mode relies on `sh` wrappers. Its end-to-end tests (`crates/dla-workload/tests/harness_run.rs`) run on Linux and macOS only; harness gen and the per-OS sampling are tested on Windows | Windows command-mode support is a gap until a Windows harness path is built and tested |
| The unbound alloc-test on a flat workload | corrected | When the trend found no growth, `os.rss` alone decided the unbound test; Go's resident set steps once at a machine-dependent n, so `go-buffer-pool` (N-HWM) passed here and failed on the CI runner with no rule-out | With no gate-passing column, the runtime's after-GC heap column votes and `os.rss` is reported; RSS still votes when it passed the gate or no heap column exists (test `with_nothing_grown_the_runtime_heap_decides_an_unbound_test`) |
| M3 benchmark | verified | `bench/results/m3-gates`: leak recall 29/30, archetype-free recall 9/9, non-leak precision 12/12, no false confirmation (0/27), no overclaim (0/6), trap miss 1/15; holder attribution 23/29 (the misses are the two Node fixtures' holder kinds); unexplained growth share 0.52 over the 8 runs that used `--residual`, because growth measured as a count is not subtracted from byte totals | Labels arrive in M6; attributing counts in bytes and the residual threshold are on the M7 calibration list |

## Orchestration and entry paths (M4)

| Item | Status | Evidence | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| Subagents in benchmark children | verified | A `--probe-prompt` child (`claude -p`, default permissions) spawned a general-purpose subagent that ran `echo sub-ok` and returned it; the earlier assumption that the tool needs approval in `-p` mode was wrong | The runner allows `Agent` and `Task`, so worker dispatch can run in the benchmark |
| Process IDs inside benchmark children | verified (limitation) | The sandbox gives the child its own PID namespace while `/proc` belongs to another: a runner-side `sleep` (alive on the host) is absent from the child's `/proc` and `ps -p`; `ps` fails with `lookup self`; `jcmd -l` lists the child's own JVM through `/tmp/hsperfdata_*`, but `jcmd <pid> ...` and `jstack` fail with `non existent JVM pid` | No fixture attaches to a process from a child. `dla attach` is tested by `cargo test` against real targets on the host. Harness figures come from in-process dumps (`/proc/self`), which work; external PID sampling and `dla run abort`'s identity check do not work inside a child |
| Writable /tmp for benchmark children | verified | With `/tmp` in the sandbox `allowWrite`, `/tmp/x` and `/tmp/claude-0/...` are writable from the child; a directory passed with `--deny-read` is masked (a write lands in an empty overlay and the host copy is unchanged); adding every `/tmp/claude-*` directory to `denyWrite` breaks Bash | `/tmp` goes to the sandbox list only, not to the Edit/Write rules; denied paths under `/tmp` are also written to `denyWrite`; the operator passes their own session directory with `--deny-read`; the work root moved to `${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-$HOME/.cache}/dla-bench` |
| HPROF instance sizes | corrected | The survey and spec took CLASS_DUMP `instanceSize` as header-inclusive; HotSpot writes the field bytes only (`Journal$Entry` 12 and `String` 14, where `GC.class_histogram` reports 24 for both) | The reader ignores `instanceSize` and sizes an instance as header plus fields rounded to 8, assuming compressed references below 24 GiB; 2000 entries give 48000 B, the same as the class histogram. Retained bytes are approximate and every summary says so; counts and dominated counts match the prototype on all 16 survey dumps |
| HPROF time and memory | verified | A 305 MB dump with 5.6M objects: 2.9 s and 348 MB peak RSS end to end (4.5 s from `.hprof.gz`); the prototype used 668 MB on the same file | Object cap 20M and a dominator pass cap set `truncated` with a warning |
| Dump-series rule | verified | `jvm-rfq-dump-series`: `quotes.RfqDesk.OPEN` grows 788,256 / 1,576,448 / 2,372,832 B (13,502 / 27,002 / 40,502 objects) and passes; `jvm-rfq-dump-series-capped`: the first interval grows +49% and the second is flat, so the gate fails and the dump-plateau test favors non-leak; one or two dumps end with `dumps < 3` and the number of dumps still needed | Three dumps confirm, as decided; the capped fixture shows why two cannot |
| Same-process check for dumps | verified (limitation) | An HPROF header has no process ID | Dumps with different HPROF versions or ID sizes, equal timestamps, or class sets that do not contain the earlier dump's application classes are refused; two runs of the same program are not told apart |
| Artifact path through the ledger | corrected | Spec section 4 omitted that a finding passes `under_test` before `confirmed`, and `under_test` needs a harness record that an artifact run never has | On an artifact run a dump-authority growth series satisfies `under_test`; a site is refused on add, annotate and confirm, so no artifact finding can carry a source line. Walked end to end on `jvm-rfq-dump-series`: summarize, refute an N-WARM sibling with the dump-plateau discriminator, test, confirm with owner and no site |
| The `worker` key on ledger lines | corrected | The spec gives both the line and the `slice_*` records a `worker` field; in a flat JSON line they are the same key | One key on the wire; reading a slice record sets both |
| A worker killed mid-write | verified | SIGKILL of a real `dla --worker jvm` loop, 7 of 7 runs: the slice replays with at most one torn line, merge copies it, the run is incomplete with an open item `worker jvm did not finish (slice ends at line N)`, and a second merge changes nothing | Replay drops a torn last line of a slice (never of the main ledger) and the next write to the slice truncates it |
| heaptrack --pid stop | corrected | heaptrack 1.5.0's wrapper calls `heaptrack_stop` through gdb again in its EXIT cleanup; after DLA's own stop that second call aborts the target (SIGABRT, 5 of 5 runs); SIGINT or SIGTERM to the wrapper skips the cleanup and leaves a 0-byte output and a FIFO. `--pid` also refuses when `kernel.yama.ptrace_scope` > 0 even for root, writes `.zst` when zstd is installed, and ignores `-o` | DLA runs the wrapper with a gdb shim first on its PATH that makes the second stop a no-op, and sets the output location through the working directory |
| Node heap snapshot memory | corrected | The plan estimated 2x the heap in use; measured +851 MB peak (VmHWM 1,002,004,480 against 150,839,296 RSS before) for 67.3 MB `heapUsed`, 12.6x | The preflight estimates 12x `heapUsed` and refuses when the peak exceeds 0.8 of available memory. SIGUSR1 is sent while planning (the estimate needs `heapUsed`), so a refusal leaves the inspector open |
| jcmd heap dump estimate | verified | `GC.heap_info` used (422,295,552) predicted the dump file (423,636,216) within 0.4%; `GC.class_histogram` and `GC.heap_dump` both force a full GC; `Thread.dump_to_file -format=json` needs JDK 21 | Older JVMs fall back to `Thread.print -l` (tested with a fake jcmd only) |
| JFR consent tier | decided | A JFR recording holds allocation samples (class and stack) but also JVM arguments and environment variables (171 `jdk.InitialEnvironmentVariable` events in a test recording) | Tier 2; the preflight and evidence say the file may contain secrets |
| memray attach | verified (partly) | A 4 s capture on Python 3.13 took 8.6 s wall (attach 0.7 s); memray must be importable by the target's own interpreter; `memray stats --json` writes a file, not stdout. Python 3.14's `sys.remote_exec` path was not run | Tier 3 on every Python version; the precondition is checked first with the target's interpreter and `PYTHONPATH` |
| goref | unverified | The permission check refused running the `go install`ed `grf` binary | Implemented from its README and source and tested with a fake `grf`; marked unverified in the output and evidence |
| Consent tier table | corrected | The tier 4 line named memray attach captures, which record allocation stacks, not memory contents | memray attach moved to tier 3; JFR named at tier 2; tier 4 notes the Node snapshot cost |
| Sweep raw file names | corrected | The `dla` and `codeql` backends run for several languages, so `sweep/<backend>.json` was overwritten by the second language | `sweep/<lang>-<backend>.json`; M2 result ledgers still point at the old names |
| Ledger IDs in file names | corrected | A worker's evidence ID contains `/` (`jvm/E5`); the gap residual file name embedded it and would fail to write in worker mode | IDs in file names use `_` (`jvm_E5`) |
| Run-state lock | verified | `std::fs::File::lock` (Rust 1.89) around every state and coverage read-modify-write; 8 threads and 8 processes doing 50 updates each lose nothing | Minimum Rust raised to 1.89 |
| Concurrent installs by workers | corrected | `dla install` wrote to the shared tools directory without a lock | An install holds an exclusive lock on `<tools>/.install.lock`; the skill still installs in the core before `dla run dispatch` |
| Stop hook calling `dla run abort` | deferred | The command exists in M4; plugin hooks arrive in M6 | Wire it with the M6 hooks |
| Cleanup actions read back by `dla run abort` | corrected | The spec let abort execute any `run` argv and delete any `rm` path stored in `state.json`; a committed `.dla/` directory in a cloned repository could make abort run or delete anything | Abort refuses a run whose `project_root` is not this project and executes only what attach registers: `rm` inside the run directory, `.java_pid<pid>`/`.attach_pid<pid>` and `/tmp/heaptrack_fifo<pid>`; `run` only for `jcmd`, `memray`, `python3 -m memray` and the heaptrack stop shim under `attach/`. Attached records are identity-checked; on a reused pid only the `rm` actions run |
| Several writes by one worker at once | corrected | Six parallel `dla --worker node ledger add` calls (a subagent can issue parallel Bash calls) produced duplicate seq numbers and IDs, and one bad slice then stopped `dla ledger merge` for every worker | Each worker-mode ledger holds an exclusive lock on `ledger.<worker>.lock`; merge records an invalid slice as an open item plus a killed worker and continues with the others; a tear inside a multi-byte character is an ordinary torn tail |
| Repeated merges | corrected | A merge run while workers were still running left 'did not finish' items open after the workers finished, and a duplicate confirmed after the merge stayed hidden behind its candidate canonical | A later merge closes the item once the worker's `slice_done` is present and never re-raises a resolved one; duplicate groups are re-evaluated on every merge and the more advanced finding becomes canonical (chains are flattened) |
| Scope paths | corrected | An absolute or `..` module path, or a glob exclude, was stored as given and then matched every file, so the scope silently widened to the whole repository | `run init` refuses such paths and requires an existing directory; a scope that cannot be parsed matches nothing |
| Scoped harness copies | corrected | The spec copied in-scope files plus root build files only; a Go module rooted above the scope (`svc/go.mod` with scope `svc/a`) could not build | Build files in directories above the scope and the root `gradle/`, `buildSrc/`, `.mvn/` and `vendor/` are copied too. Cargo workspace siblings and path dependencies outside the scope are still not copied |
| Kotlin object holders | corrected | For `object Audit { val trail = ... }` the class-file census reported `Audit.INSTANCE` (a flat probe) because the property's backing field shared its line and was deduplicated away; the growing `probe:shop.codec.Audit.trail` column tied to no finding, so `alloc-test` judged the flat column and the policy refuted the real holder | The census reports the property (`shop.codec.Audit.trail`) and drops INSTANCE when the object has one; a passing `probe:<Class>.<field>` column ties to the finding whose owner is that field (confirm gate, report, alloc-test and gap). `dla-bench dynamic` now confirms jni-order-codec's Kotlin leak |
| jni-order-codec's native leak | unverified (mechanical policy) | The Rust code runs only inside the JVM, so the rust worker has no workload of its own; inside the JVM harness the native frames show only as `os.rss` growth | Listed as an expected failure of `dla-bench dynamic`; the two-worker acceptance (both slices merged, coverage for both runtimes) holds. A skill run can still reach it through the allocator shim or a native census holder |
| Shared scratch directories | corrected | `<run>/tmp/<backend>` was shared by every language and worker; codeql runs for jvm and python wrote the same database and SARIF | `tmp/<worker>/` in worker mode and `<lang>-<backend>` per sweep backend |
| Hostile heap dumps | corrected | A class whose super chain loops, or with tens of thousands of fields, made the reader allocate gigabytes and abort; a gzip bomb filled the disk and the refusal said to retake the dump | Super-chain loops and more than 1,048,576 own plus inherited fields are refused as malformed; inflation stops at 100x the compressed size (at least 64 MiB) with a distinct refusal, and a full disk says to free space. There is no free-space preflight before inflating |
| Attaching to the right process | corrected | jcmd was run against any pid (the attach client sends SIGQUIT, which ends nginx or gunicorn); memray executed the target's `argv[0]`; long attaches did not re-check the pid; a failed attach was marked stopped so abort skipped its leftover cleanup; memory figures came from DLA's own cgroup | jcmd needs positive evidence of a JVM (a `java` executable, libjvm in the maps or an hsperfdata file) before any call; memray uses only `/proc/<pid>/exe`; start times are re-checked in every wait loop and before each stop; an attach with cleanup left stays open for abort; `--out` cannot name DLA's own files; memory headroom is the minimum over the target's cgroup and its ancestors |
| Benchmark work root and child budget | corrected | A `--work-root` under `/tmp` was accepted although children can write all of `/tmp`; `--child-budget` accepted values that overflow in dla | Both are refused up front |
| Fixture byte figures | corrected | The dump-series READMEs quoted the prototype parser's retained sizes, which use a different size model | They now quote `dla summarize heap-graph`: OPEN 788,256 / 1,576,448 / 2,372,832 B; RECENT 536,272 / 800,272 / 800,272 B (the third dump is exactly flat) |
| Skill commands that do not exist | corrected | M5 text cited `dla gate check`, `dla ledger label`, `strong` and `explain` before they existed | They exist since M6: `dla gate check --stage <s> \| --final` is the gate (`dla run status` lists the same blocking items), and labels, `strong` and `explain` are ledger commands. The skill and the worker text cite them |
| Restarting a killed worker | unverified (limitation) | `dla run dispatch` refuses a name that is already registered | recovery.md says to resolve the worker's open items and start a new run for that runtime |
| Kotlin in CI | verified (trust on first use) | The dynamic job installs the Kotlin 2.0.21 compiler zip from the JetBrains GitHub release, pinned by a sha256 computed here (not compared with a published checksum); `build.sh` takes the stdlib next to `kotlinc`, else the `~/.m2` jar | Compare with a published checksum when one is available |
| Toolchain lints | corrected | CI's stable moved to Rust 1.99, whose clippy adds `useless_format` and `chunks_exact_to_as_chunks`; local 1.97 did not report them | Code updated; local checks now run on 1.99 |
| Workers started by the skill | corrected | In Claude Code 2.1.319 the Agent tool starts subagents in the background by default; in an unattended `-p` run the core ended its turn, and the session stopped the last worker before `dla ledger merge` and `dla report` ran (first benchmark smoke run of jni-order-codec) | The skill starts workers and verifiers with `run_in_background: false` in one message (they still run in parallel) and, if an agent is backgrounded anyway, waits in the same turn by polling `dla run status`. All three jni-order-codec runs of the M4 benchmark then dispatched two workers, merged both slices and reported |
| The router's target rule | corrected | The spec's 'ask once for the target; unattended: stop, never guess' was taken literally: on repository fixtures whose prompt describes the service ('the edge gateway process'), 5 of 75 runs of the first M4 benchmark opened a symptom run and stopped after three turns, with the source tree in the working directory | The working directory's source tree is the target unless the request names another one; only with no source there and nothing named does the skill ask once and, unattended, stop. The first run's numbers are kept in `bench/results/m4-gates/README.md`; the committed run uses the corrected skill |
| go-buffer-pool on the CI runner | unverified (flaky once) | The dynamic acceptance on CI ended once (commit beb9511) with no rule-out for go-buffer-pool and no passing column; it passed on the next CI run, here, and here under CPU load, where the deciding heap plateau test leaves a wide margin (rise 3,715 B against a limit of 9,975 B) | `dla-bench dynamic` now prints a failing fixture's decisions in the job log; read them if it fails again |
| go-buffer-pool on CI run 67 | unverified | No trend column passed. The unbound alloc-test was decided by `go.heap_live`, which read growth-continues in the extra cycles (purge not run), so there was no rule-out | The CI log now prints plateau figures (main and extra slope, rise, noise) for a failing fixture. Read them the next time it fails and decide the rule then |
| Confirmation from untied growth | decided (M5) | Across the M3 and two M4 benchmark runs, 9 confirmations cited only passing growth not tied to the finding; 7 were wrong (including both M4 false confirmations on the heartbeat decoy) and 2 right (a timer column one line off its site, and a native leak seen only in `os.rss`) | User decision: refuse. Ledger lines of schema 4 replay under `M5Rules`: a leak-side confirmation needs a cited passing column tied to the finding by finding, site, owner (probe or heap-dump holder column) or candidates, else `growth-not-tied`. Lines of schema 1 to 3 keep their rules, so earlier results replay unchanged |

## Backends and attribution (M5)

Cut 1: `dla summarize` with 18 adapters, the capture flags of `dla harness run`, and the per-operation
policy of `dla-bench dynamic`. Cut 2 (rows at the end of this table): Node, Go and Python adapters and
captures, known leaks, `dla correlate`, `dla attach bpftrace`, and the policy for them.

| Item | Status | Evidence | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| Adapter set of cut 1 | verified | `dla summarize --list` on the built binary printed 18 adapters (it now prints backend names grouped by operation and runtime plus a `missing` list; `--list --op` and `--list --runtime` print one row per adapter) over four operations (`heap-graph` 2, `type-histogram-diff` 4, `alloc-stack-diff` 7, `non-heap` 5); `cargo test -p dla-cli --test summarize_conformance --test summarize_jvm --test summarize_native` passed 39, 18 and 21 tests | `references/backends.md` has the op x runtime x backend table. `jfr-old-object-sample` is the only adapter with a tool (`jfr`); the others read files |
| Fixtures of the adapters | verified (limitation) | Fixtures are real output of JDK 21 (`jfr print`, `Thread.dump_to_file`, `VM.classloader_stats`, `VM.native_memory`), valgrind 3.22 (memcheck, DHAT, vgdb), heaptrack 1.5, gcc LeakSanitizer, trimmed under 64 KB; the smallest real `.jfr` is about 100 KB | The `jfr-old-object-sample` fixture is printed text; the binary path is covered by a test that records a `.jfr` at run time and skips when `java` or `jfr` is absent |
| The tie rule | decided (M5) | Phase 1a review confirmed 55 of 62 findings, 13 high; most were loose growth ties (`--finding` forcing a tie, simple-name and generic-type matches, package-blind resolver, merged stacks of different holders, removers and current thread frames as sites) | One rule: a column ties to a finding only by holder identity, site identity or type identity, never forced; ambiguous matches tie none; every tie records `rule` and key. Confirm side: `holder:` columns match only the same qualified holder, `probe:` columns keep suffix matching. `--finding` narrows, it never ties |
| Ambiguous ties | corrected | `tie::resolve` change: ambiguity ties none only for fuzzy matches (type identity, rule 3; a shorter owner form). Exact identity (a column whose site or writer, or whose qualified holder, is exactly a finding's) ties every finding at that place: `candidates` lists all, `finding` is the `--finding` one if among them, else the first by id. Rule 3 also counts a field's own declared type when it is a project class (declared in the sources, not a generic, JDK or Kotlin type) | `native-frame-decoder` (detector finding F1 and census finding F3 both at `src/decoder.c:29`) and `jvm-audit-chain` (self-linked `gateway.RequestContext`) failed the `dump_backed` check before this; the unit and conformance tests cover both |
| Own declared type and chain links | decided | `jvm-audit-chain`: the jcmd histogram's growing type `gateway.RequestContext` is the declared type of both the static root `RequestContext.latest` (F6) and the instance link `previous` (F8, `RequestContext.java:13`); two matches tied none, so the leak had no dump-backed evidence (full dynamic run, G5) | Rule 3 counts a field's own declared project class; an instance field whose type is its own declaring class is a link inside the growing structure, not its holder, and is left out; a static field of that type is the root and keeps it (test `a_self_link_field_is_not_a_holder_of_its_own_type`) |
| Artifact runs cannot tie by declared type | corrected | A heap dump run has no source: a growing type ties only through the accumulation class a prior heap-graph summary recorded; for an ArrayList-style leak that class is `java.lang.Object[]`, which is generic and never ties | Repository runs tie by the declared element type (package-aware); artifact runs tie by the `holder:` column of `heap-graph` |
| Redaction in summaries | verified | Names of classes, functions, files and categories pass through; values after `=` or `: `, URL queries, quoted literals and long key-like tokens become `<len:pattern:hash>`; thread-name groups show a pattern only when plain program text; a planted secret in a frame, function, module or path name is shaped in stdout, ledger and run files and kept with raw consent (conformance test per adapter) | Digits are normalized away from thread-name patterns, so raw consent cannot print a digit-bearing key; the thread-dump fixture sets `emits_values: false` |
| Evidence authority of summaries | decided | An artifact run records file-based evidence as `dump`; a repository run keeps the adapter's base (`live` for valgrind, heaptrack, dhat, lsan, shim groups, vgdb, proc-fd; jcmd text `live`; harness types and model JSON `test`) | Keeps the artifact path rule `owner_stands_in_for_site` working and lets `dump_backed` count them |
| Missing tool in auto and explicit mode | verified | With `jfr` removed from PATH, a `.jfr` input exits 0 with `backend: null`, `coverage_gap` and a `missing` row `jfr-old-object-sample`; `--backend jfr-old-object-sample` exits 2 `tool-missing`; printed JFR text falls to `jfr-old-object-text`. Bench `--without jfr` on `jvm-order-cache` passes; `--without valgrind,heaptrack` on `native-frame-decoder` ends as the hidden-tool expected failure | The coverage row of a tool-backed adapter is named after the adapter, not the tool; a conformance run on a machine without `jfr` needs a skip (request to the test owner) |
| Mixed capture formats | corrected | One call over histograms plus threads plus NMT put three file kinds into one `non-heap` command; `select_in` now refuses mixed formats as `mixed-formats` naming which backend reads which input | `dla harness run` prints one command per op and file kind (threads, class loader stats, NMT diff; valgrind XML versus vgdb files; heaptrack text versus diff) |
| JFR old-object samples | verified (limitation) | Real JDK 21 recordings: the magic is `FLR\0`; `jfr print` needs `-J-Duser.language=en -J-Duser.country=US` (the size column is locale-dependent) and `--stack-depth 64` (default 5 truncates); frames carry no file name (the site comes from the class resolver); class names over about 86 characters are truncated; `settings=none` with an enabled OldObjectSample gave zero events, only `settings=profile` with `path-to-gc-roots=true` gave events; the printed time has no zone | The adapter orders recordings as given (`ordered_by: argument`). The holder is taken from the nearest `Class Name:` line, e.g. `static:gateway.RequestContext.latest`; thread-root and `root:<system>` samples have no holder and never tie |
| JFR as evidence | verified (limitation) | `jvm-audit-chain` H2 at `--step 30000`: the `holder:static:gateway.RequestContext.latest` total passed the gate and tied to the census finding by holder, adding the writer `src/gateway/RequestContext.java:27`. At the default step the sample totals were 6, 9, 21 and 23 and the gate failed | JFR is a sample of old objects, not a census: a tie needs a leak large enough for a strictly rising sample count. A failed JFR gate never refutes |
| JDK 21 thread dump | verified | `Thread.dump_to_file -format=json` has only `tid`, `name` and `stack` per thread: no state, no creation site (verified on JDK 21 only; 19 and 20 untested) | Groups are keyed by state derived from the innermost frame, thread-name pattern, and `frame:<Class.method>`; the current frame is not a creation site, so thread-dump groups never tie. `non-heap` says so instead of pointing at `dla census` |
| Via holders (shared retention) | verified | The dominator holder cannot see shared retention: in the real Kotlin coroutine dump the retained set is dominated by the super root, not a thread. A search over static fields of application classes, then other classes, then thread and JNI roots finds `static:<Class>.<field>`; a synthetic coroutine series made with the hprof builder gave a via holder that passed the gate, tied by holder and became a candidate with its site | A via holder and a dominator holder with the same key are two quantities (container only versus the set kept alive through the field); the series takes the larger per dump (dominator 1232 B, via 609600 B in the test; the dominator column alone did not pass). The real reader found no via holder in a hand-written Java program with two static roots, so only the builder heap exercises it |
| Command-mode heap dumps | corrected | Dumps taken by `harness run --capture heap` in command mode are separate processes. Before the per-point series, `heap-graph` refused them (`dumps-not-comparable`): on `jvm-audit-chain` the printed heap-graph command was refused by the one-process check; on `jni-order-codec` (`--capture all`, JDK 21) the command passed the gate and tied `F1` to the static holder `shop.codec.Audit.trail`. Now see the row "Per-point heap series" | The plan item "print heap-graph for JVM harness runs" is now met for command mode: `dla harness run` prints a heap-graph command for command-mode heap captures with 3 or more files of one format (a per-point series). Other dumps of different processes are still refused |
| Per-point heap series | verified | `dla summarize heap-graph` forms a series over n when 3 or more inputs are all command-mode heap captures of one harness listed in `.dla/<run>/harness/H<n>/captures.json` (kind heap, distinct x, one label). It orders them by n, skips the one-process checks, keeps the format checks, uses the same strict-growth dump-series gate and dump-plateau discriminator, and says `series_kind: per-point` with the n values. Measured on copies of `jvm-audit-chain`: the holder `gateway.RequestContext.latest` tied to the census finding by holder. On `node-retry-scheduler`: the module variable `src.scheduler.shared` tied to its census finding (static/global) | Other dumps keep the one-process rule. Mixed driver and command captures, two dumps of one point, a repeated n and a missing n are refused. `references/stages.md`, `backends.md`, `jvm.md` and `node.md` describe it |
| Segmented HPROF cut by a kill | corrected | Review R21: a dump with `HEAP_DUMP_SEGMENT` records and no `HEAP_DUMP_END` could pass for a complete dump | Refused as truncated; the harness lists a heap or histogram file only when the agent's note names it without an error |
| Kotlin driver class path | verified (partly) | Driver-mode gen on `jni-order-codec` puts `kotlin-stdlib` on the javac and run class paths; the run completed with 24 capture files and reported the ffi site `native/src/lib.rs:40 -> shop.codec.Native.open`; the stdlib came from `~/.m2` (2.0.21) | Lookup order: `$KOTLIN_HOME/lib`, then the `lib` next to `kotlinc` on PATH, then the build script's version in `$M2` or `~/.m2/repository`. The first two branches were tested only with fake layouts (no `kotlinc` here). The hooks of a generated driver are still filled by hand |
| vgdb chain | corrected | Valgrind 3.22 `--vgdb-stop-at=exit` lets a standalone `vgdb <command>` release the process as soon as it returns, so one stop allows one call; the chain keeps one `vgdb --pid` relay open and speaks the gdb remote protocol (`qRcmd`, ack each packet, detach `$D`). gdbserver replies use run-length coding (`3*!9`); without decoding, a header line (`definitely lost in loss record 2 of 2`) was lost in testing | The plan text "run each vgdb call" is replaced by one relay session; a unit test covers the decoder. Walk: start at the last listed block, then the first, at most 12 queries, only the walk that ends is written |
| vgdb limits | verified (limitation) | Real C list-head program: the three files are written and `heap-graph` over them passed with `holder:static:registry`, tied to the census finding; `native-frame-decoder` (definitely lost) ends "no root found" with a `needed` text; a second static pointing at a block ends the chain at that symbol | A definitely-lost block has no holder: attribution is by allocation site (`alloc-stack-diff` ties `F2` at `src/decoder.c:29`). A wrapper command (make, sh) gets no chain; a limitation line says so. `valgrind-vgdb` was first tested on files produced by a script, then on the harness's own files |
| Static symbols versus census owners | verified | Census owners are file-path based (`src.<module>.<NAME>`, C `src.<file>.<name>`); valgrind prints Rust data symbols in v0 mangling (`_RNvNtCs..._15outline_service8registry8REGISTRY`) and the demangler handles it | A vgdb symbol ties to the finding whose owner ends with the module path and name; a root-module or bare C name ties only when exactly one finding ends with it; several match none and are listed |
| Memcheck, heaptrack and shim sites | verified | `dla harness run H1 --tool valgrind` and `--tool heaptrack` on `native-frame-decoder`, then `alloc-stack-diff`: tied `F2` by site `src/decoder.c:29`; raw `heaptrack.gz` through `heaptrack_print`; DHAT from `valgrind --tool=dhat`; `gcc -fsanitize=address` reports; `rust-tree-parent-links` under valgrind tied `F2` through its census writer `src/section.rs:28` | Memcheck XML from separate runs is a run-length series, not dumps of one process. `heaptrack_print` text is rounded (3.02M, about 1%); `-F` folded files carry exact bytes but no block counts. dhat-rs itself was not run |
| Shim stack groups | corrected | The shim's per-stack key is a hash of raw addresses, different per process; the harness's `symbolize.rs` exposes only per-site totals. The reader runs `addr2line` per stack and keys rows by the first 8 resolved frames; on the dumps of `native-frame-decoder` p1/p5/p9 it tied `F2` by site | Plan correction: shim-stack-groups symbolizes per stack itself. Without `addr2line` stacks stay as `module+offset`, nothing ties, and the coverage row `addr2line` reads `missing` |
| Descriptor counts | verified | `proc-fd` reads `ls -l /proc/<pid>/fd` listings and `fd.<kind>` dump values on the same dumps | Counts per kind only; it never ties and never prints a path |
| Rust allocator frames | corrected | Review R34: the Rust name rule trimmed project functions named `allocate` or `with_capacity` | The rule applies to bare file names and to files under `library/alloc/`; `operator new(` frames are allocator frames |

| `jni-order-codec` L1 | corrected | In the JVM worker the shim columns `native.live_bytes@native/src/lib.rs:40` and `:43` pass, but no finding of that worker sits at those sites; the census finding is in the rust worker, which has no harness, and ties never cross runtimes | Plan correction: the shim columns alone do not confirm L1. | JVM capture cost | verified | A `--capture all` run adds about 18 s and about 55 MB per fixture; a heap dump costs about 20 ms and 10 to 40 MB; a `jni-order-codec` driver run wrote about 150 MB | `--dumps` (default 4) bounds the dumps; the files stay after `dla run abort` |
| Go goroutine leak profile | verified (cut 2) | Go 1.27 made the `goroutineleak` profile generally available (Go 1.26: `GOEXPERIMENT=goroutineleakprofile`); with `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.27.x` it found 500 leaked goroutines created by `subscription.go:33` in `go-subscription-workers` and 0 in the closed decoy. The machine has Go 1.24.7 | Blind spot reproduced: channels reachable from globals are not reported, so an empty profile never refutes. | Peak columns in alloc-test | corrected | CI runs 58 and 60 failed `python-graph-churn` (no rule-out): `os.vm_hwm` passed the trend gate on the runner and voted in the unbound alloc-test, but a peak is never released by a purge. Local runs (3.12, 3.13, CPU contention, taskset) never passed the gate; forced by editing S1 | `os.vm_hwm` and `native.peak_bytes` no longer vote after passing the gate (reported; they vote only when nothing else exists). CI 63 then failed with `os.rss` named, and a local run under load (G5, 1 of 5) showed the vote that blocked it: `os.vm_data` and `os.vm_hwm` inconclusive (purge retained) while `os.rss` and `os.rss_anon` favored non-leak. A purge does not give reserved address space back, so `os.vm_data` joined the non-voting columns. A failing fixture now prints its passing trend columns and alloc-test column verdicts in the CI log (the artifact host is blocked here) |
| Coroutine captures in the ledger | unverified (limitation) | The census keeps every `L$n` spill (`L$0` and `L$1` both map to line 16 in the real fixture), but the ledger's finding identity (holder, site) keeps one candidate per suspension point | Recorded, not changed in M5 |

A unit test (`holders::tests`) checks that `names_holder` is false for it | Decision: `EvidenceKind::class()` does not change, because it is derived at replay for lines of every schema version. `dla_core::holders::names_holder(record, finding)` decides whether a retention-class record shows a holder (hprof and V8 snapshot `holders` and `via` keys, vgdb allocation site or root, alloc-stack `top[].root`); `histogram-diff` never does. `dla ledger strong` and `dla ledger label` use it, so a histogram is never the second class of evidence. A Population class was not added |
| Node V8 snapshot reader | verified | Streaming `.heapsnapshot` parser in `dla-parsers` (std and serde only): field counts from `snapshot.meta`, every index and edge-count sum validated, memory preflight (a file over a quarter of available memory is refused as `insufficient-resources`). `cargo test -p dla-parsers` passed 202 tests including a 400-round byte-mutation test that never panics. On the real 31 MB survey snapshot (358,769 nodes) in a release build: read 238 ms, whole analysis 325 ms | Strings are one `(string)` row and never named after their text. Not run on a multi-GB snapshot |
| Node snapshot facts | verified | Node 22: SharedFunctionInfo nodes (type `code`) carry an internal edge `script` to a node named with the script path; closures hold the SFI through `shared` and the context through `context`; a function context's ScopeInfo lists its names as hidden edges and the last is the function's name; timers hang on `timerListMap[ms]` through `_idleNext` and `_idlePrev`; `Timeout._onTimeout` is a property edge; listeners hang under an emitter's `_events` | Holder naming uses these; the `scripts.json` sidecar of the harness is not read by the adapters (kept as a fallback for older Node) |
| Node holder classes | verified | `node-heapsnapshot` on 3 snapshots of one process of each bench fixture: `node-retry-scheduler` copy gave `static:src.scheduler.shared` (static/global, retained 3.41, 6.83 and 10.25 MB, gate passed) tied by holder to F7, and `field:src.scheduler.RetryScheduler.latest` (container-field) tied to F4; `node-socket-heartbeat` copy gave `registry:timers:src.heartbeat.startHeartbeat.beat` (registration-table) tied by holder to F6, whose census holder is blocked-stack. At that time `needed` printed `annotate F6 --holder registration-table`; now `annotate --holder` fills a holder only when the finding has none, so when a finding already has a different holder `needed` says so and names the sibling route (`dla ledger add --sibling-of F ...`) for Node and JVM; `closure:src.heartbeat.startHeartbeat.session` and `Session.socket` became candidates F14 and F15. Output 1273 and 1642 bytes | A driver that held its own reference to the leaking object during the snapshot made the module variable a non-dominating holder (the first driver draft did this); same-named links (a chain's `previous`) are skipped. Candidates only for project holders with a qualified owner, at most 3 per run. The event-listener owner (`module.<emitter>.<event>`) cannot match census owners such as `src.gateway.Gateway.accept`, so those holders become candidates, not ties | Update (Node holder fix): the holder kind is the kind of the retention root (`Snapshot::root_kind`: registration entry, then module or `global` variable, then function context, else instance). Each passing growth column carries `holder_kind`. `cargo test -p dla-parsers` and `cargo test -p dla-cli --test summarize_node` passed. Copies of the fixtures: `node-retry-scheduler` `shared` and the column tied to `RetryScheduler.latest` read static/global; `node-socket-heartbeat` `Session.recent` reads registration-table, because it is retained through the loopback socket `_events`. On the live heartbeat run no column ties to the census timer finding
| Node snapshot series | corrected | Object ids are assigned in traversal order, so two processes of one program can overlap; the id check is only a heuristic | The pid directory is the guard: inputs from two different numeric parent directories are refused `dumps-not-comparable`; also refused: equal or non-increasing ids, or fewer than half the earlier ids present |
| Node harness capture | verified | `cargo test -p dla-workload` (5 harness_capture_node tests) and `cargo test -p dla-cli --test harness_capture_node` (4 tests, through the `dla` binary) passed. `dla harness run H --capture heap,alloc,nonheap --dumps 3` on `node-socket-heartbeat` (command mode, 9 points): 3 snapshots of 21 to 24 MB (about 0.5 s each), 3 profiles of about 100 KB, 3 `resources.json`; a driver run (hooks filled by hand) gave 4 snapshots of one process at 7.5, 11.8, 14.6 and 18.9 MB in about 1.2 s | Command-mode snapshots are separate processes: histograms only. The `captures` array lists each file; a refused snapshot becomes a blocked coverage row with exit 0 |
| Node resources | verified | `getActiveResourcesInfo` omits unref'd timers (the heartbeat fixture's 2000 live timers were absent from `active`), so the agent wraps timers; its type names differ from async_hooks (`TCPSocketWrap` versus `TCPWRAP`) and the agent aliases them. `resources.json` showed 300 live timers at `src/heartbeat.js:15` (0 in the cleared control); `node-resources` tied `Timeout@src/heartbeat.js:15` to F6 by site | async_hooks destroy events arrive one tick late: a resource closed in the current tick can still count as tracked (timers are exact). A WeakRef per tracked resource would keep it alive for a synchronous job, so none is used |
| Node heap profiles | verified (limitation) | `HeapProfiler.getSamplingHeapProfile` does not exist in Node 22; `--heap-prof` and stopSampling profiles parse identically. An esbuild bundle (survey project) mapped `o` in `out/app.js` to `src/a.ts:2` with 6 frames mapped | `//# sourceMappingURL` is not read; the adapter looks for `<script>.map` next to the script. A site tie by line happens only when the function starts on the finding's line; on the bench fixtures heapprofile tied to nothing. Window profiles are not summed (a stack passes only if its own survivors grow in every window) |
| Node snapshots and consent | decided (M5) | The spec asked for tier 4 for snapshots. `crates/dla-core/src/consent.rs` (module doc) states the decision: heap captures by `dla harness run --capture heap` (JVM dumps, Node snapshots) of a process DLA starts are tier 1, like the other things a tier-1 run builds and runs in the scratch copy; tier 4 applies to memory contents of a process DLA did not start (`dla attach jcmd --op heap-dump`, `dla attach node-inspector --op heap-snapshot`). The agent checks that about 12 times `heapUsed` is available (a 21 MB snapshot took 546 ms) | No tier-4 gate on `dla harness run --capture heap`. `harness/node/README.md` and the tier 4 row of `references/safety-and-effects.md` say the same |

| Ledger schema 5 holder correction | verified | `dla ledger annotate F --holder H --evidence E` changes a holder already set only when `E` is a `growth-series` (dump or live) written by `dla-hprof` or `dla-v8-snapshot` with a passing column tied to F whose `holder_kind` is H. `cargo test -p dla-core --test holder_correction` (8 tests, including v4 replay refusing a change and a forged `replaced_holder`), `cargo test -p dla-cli --test cli` (`annotate_changes_a_holder_only_with_matching_growth_evidence`) and a real `node-retry-scheduler` run (F5 container-field to static/global with E22) passed. `schemas/ledger-line.schema.json` regenerated with `dla schema --out schemas` | `--evidence` takes a comma separated list; every other conflicting annotation is still refused as `annotation-conflict`; a v4 line that changes a holder is refused on replay |

The heap profile has alloc and inuse objects and space and reflects the last completed GC (call `runtime.GC()` first). One stack appears once per size class | Only the debug=2 text can tie a goroutine group by site; protobuf goroutine groups (blocked frame) never tie. Function rows (`go-pprof-heap-functions`) never tie. The `.pb.gz` sniff runs before the HPROF reader, which takes any `.gz` |
