## Ledger schema 6 (M6)

| Item | Status | Evidence | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| Reachability of `strong` | unverified (limitation) | Survey scan of `bench/results/m5-gates` (75 runs): 6 of 49 confirmed leak-side findings cited a `retention-path` or `alloc-stack` record at all (3 and 3). Go, Rust, Python and Node repository runs have no retention source in the default flow, and the HPROF and V8 readers write both the growth and the retention record from the same dumps, which are one signal (`records_independent` needs tool, tool class and source to differ) | `strong` is reachable from a harness trend plus a heap capture read by a reader, a vgdb walk, a JFR profile with root paths, or `dla intervene`. Out of time leaves `confirmed`, which is a valid disposition. Measure how often the M6 runs reach `strong` before calling the limit acceptable |
| Retention records name no holder kind | unverified (limitation) | `retention-path` results of `dla-hprof`, `dla-v8-snapshot` and `valgrind-vgdb` carry holder keys but no `holder_kind` (only growth columns carry it). `strong-holder-mismatch` fires only for a record that states one | The check is in place and unit-tested with a synthetic record. Adding `holder_kind` to the readers' retention results would make it effective and costs result bytes |
| `explain` checks what is stated, not that it is true | unverified (limitation) | The checks are deterministic: the text names the site (or owner) and the holder, names an adding site that is a recorded writer, states the removal with a marker word, names the recorded remover for `conditional`, `wrong-key` and `unreachable`, and `observed` cites a retention path, an intervention or tied growth. Writers and removers come from annotations and the heuristic census, so "no remover" is an absence claim the census cannot prove | A text can pass and still be wrong. Truth rests on the verifier (the bundle leaves the explanation out) and on an intervention. The marker words are a closed list (`never`, `only when`, `key`, `never reached`, ...) and may reject a correct sentence that uses other words |
| Label ties for kinds whose result names no finding | unverified | `census-holder`, `static-hit`, `histogram-diff`, `non-heap`, `should-be-dead` and `residual` results do not name a finding, so a record counts for a label when its `finding`/`candidates` name the finding or the finding or its family cites it. Retention, allocation, growth, discriminator and intervention records need their own tie | A cited record that is about another holder can satisfy such a kind; the archetype `requires` lists use these kinds only next to a growth or retention kind |
| Strong accepts a new trend record in the same move | design choice | `dla trend` records written before schema 6 have no signal, so a finding confirmed on one cannot become strong until the series is trended again | `dla ledger strong F --evidence <retention id>,<new trend id>` cites both |

## Intervention (M6)

| Item | Status | Evidence | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| Decision thresholds of `dla intervene` | unverified (placeholder) | `growth-removed` needs the treated gate to fail on a flat or plateau column with a slope at or below 0.2 of the control slope (or an interval reaching 0); `growth-remains` needs a passing gate at 0.5 of the control slope or more. The numbers come from the survey; nothing measured them. The gate thresholds are the control trend's, themselves provisional | On the M7 calibration list with the gate thresholds. `judge_intervention` takes them from two constants in `dla-stats` |
| `--cap` is deferred | deferred | The plan's "bounded" intervention (wrap the writer in a size check) needs a per-language template and a rule for the receiver | `--patch` reaches the same result: a diff that adds the bound, judged as growth-removed or growth-remains. Add `--cap` after the M7 calibration |
| `exercised` guard is always `null` | unverified (limitation) | The harness records which entry points ran (`coverage.json`), not which lines, so DLA cannot tell whether the stubbed line ran | The record says so in its limitations; a stub of a line that never ran gives `growth-remains`, which refutes nothing. Line coverage would close it for Go (`-coverprofile` has lines) and Python (`coverage`) |
| Native and Rust limits | unverified (limitation) | Those harnesses have no holder probes; the control column is an allocator-shim site (`native.live_bytes@file:line`) or an os column | A stub of the allocating statement removes the site column (treated as removed when the other columns are present). A stub elsewhere may not move `os.rss` past the gate, so expect `inconclusive` there; the missing-column rule and `positive_control` carry the weight. Not exercised in the M6 tests, which run Python and Go |
| JVM `dla intervene` | unverified | The integration tests (`crates/dla-cli/tests/intervene.rs`) cover Python and Go. The JVM harness needs its agent jar and a driver that change with the fixtures | Run it on a JVM fixture in the M6 dynamic run |
| A stub changes what the program returns | unverified (limitation) | A stubbed writer can change control flow (a result no longer stored), so a run may exit non-zero or hang; `run_ok` catches the exit and the timeout, not a changed output | The record is `invalid` when the run fails; a changed output is not checked |
| Rebuild after the edit | verified | The harness build marker covers the build command, `driver/` and `agent/`, not `work/` sources (see the scratch-copy item). `dla intervene` clones into a new harness with no marker, and removes the marker of the clone after a `--rerun-control` run | Integration test `python_rerun_control_reproduces_and_is_recorded` |

## Fixture support (M6)

Gaps that `jvm-invoice-render` and `node-price-board` exposed in the dynamic policy, fixed in the probe, the HPROF reader and the policy.

| Item | Status | Evidence | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| JVM probe of a static `ThreadLocal` field | verified (JDK 21) | The probe measured the `ThreadLocal` object, which is flat, so `RenderContext.SEGMENTS` was refuted. It now sums the value the `ThreadLocal` holds in `threadLocals` and `inheritableThreadLocals` of every live platform thread (`size`: collection, map or array length; `reachable` and `bytes`: one bounded walk from all the values). On `jvm-invoice-render` (4 pooled workers) the column grows by 12.5 segments and 1.2 KB per invoice and passes the trend; on the trimmed control it is flat (`jvm_agent_probe_measures_threadlocal_values_on_pooled_threads`). `dla-bench dynamic` confirms the leak | The agent opens `java.lang` to its own module with `Instrumentation.redefineModule`, so no `--add-opens` flag is added to the launch. When the maps cannot be read (another JDK layout, a security manager) the probe abstains: no column, the reason in the limitations. Limits: virtual threads are not listed by `Thread.getAllStackTraces`, so their thread-locals are not read; a `ThreadLocal` held by an instance field or a local is not reached because probes start at static fields; the JDK 24 and later layouts of `Thread.threadLocals` were not run |
| Thread-local holder in `dla summarize heap-graph` | verified (JDK 21 dumps) | A pooled thread is also reached through its executor (`ThreadPoolExecutor.workers`), so the dominator tree gives its thread-local values to the super root and no holder named them. The reader now follows `Thread.threadLocals` and `inheritableThreadLocals` to `ThreadLocalMap.table[i].value`, reads the entry's `referent` (the `ThreadLocal`, which no strong edge reaches) and, when a static field holds that `ThreadLocal`, attributes the value to the holder `thread-local:<Class>.<field>` (holder kind `thread-local`, owner `Class.field`), summed over all threads. A value counts when its dominator chain reaches the thread object. Real dumps of `jvm-invoice-render`: 1393848, 5561736, 8321800 and 12542416 bytes over 4 dumps, gate pass, tied to the census finding by holder identity. `hprof_thread_local.rs` and `summarize_jvm.rs` test it on synthetic heaps | Values that the thread itself dominates are taken out of the thread holder, so one set of bytes has one holder. The tie rule is unchanged (holder identity, the owner equals the finding's owner). `--finding F` prefers the thread-local holder over the static holder of the same field. Not resolved: a `ThreadLocal` that no static field holds (the values stay with the thread holder `thread:<name>`, holder kind from its path as before), and thread-locals of virtual threads |
| Policy: a finding tied by a summarize call | verified | `node-price-board` L1 (`Board.latest`): closure contexts are invisible to the in-process probes, so no passing column tied to the census finding before the heap-graph step. The policy now drives a still-candidate finding that a passing summarize column ties like a harness-tied finding: N-WARM sibling, test, sibling refuted with the call's `favors leak` discriminator, confirm citing the call's growth evidence. Unit tests with a stand-in dla (`ops.rs`) and `dla-bench dynamic` | Only a finding with a site (an owner on an artifact run) and a leak-favoring discriminator is driven, once per site; a refuted finding stays refuted. The discriminator is the `dump-plateau` one of the heap-graph call, whose thresholds are provisional (not calibrated); the ledger accepted it as the refutation of N-WARM in the two fixtures. `EXPECTED_FAILURES` lost both temporary entries |
| `L-TLS` row: holder kind of the adapter | verified | The "Row L-TLS (jvm)" item above left the adapter's holder kind unchecked. The adapter reports `thread-local` with the static field as owner on the trap's dumps | The row's wording ("a retention path that starts at a thread") still holds for values on threads the reader cannot resolve to a static field |

## Gates and hooks (M6)

| Item | Status | Evidence | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| Plugin agents ignore frontmatter `hooks:` | verified (docs) | The Claude Code subagent docs say plugin agents do not load `hooks`, `mcpServers` or `permissionMode` from their frontmatter | The `dla:worker` SubagentStop gate lives in `hooks/hooks.json` (matcher `^dla:worker$`), not in `agents/worker.md`. The SKILL.md frontmatter `hooks:` block and `hooks/hooks.json` are kept equal by a drift test (`crates/dla-cli/tests/gate_hooks.rs`) |
| Stop hook block cap resets on a tool call | verified (docs) | The hooks docs describe the stop-hook loop guard as counting consecutive blocks without progress | `dla gate stop-hook` also counts three blocks with an unchanged missing list (`stop_blocks`, `stop_block_digest`) and then stamps the run INCOMPLETE and allows the stop, so a stuck gate cannot loop |
| `MultiEdit` is not a current tool | verified (docs, tool list of 2.1.321) | Not in the tool list of the hook docs or of a live session | No `MultiEdit` matcher; the write gate matches `Write`, `Edit` and `NotebookEdit` |
| Bash, Grep and `@file` mentions bypass the hook gates | verified (design) | PreToolUse matchers see tool calls, not file reads done by a shell or an `@file` expansion; `bash-hook` is best effort (basename and quoting rules) | The guarantee is the integrity seal: an edit to `ledger.jsonl`, `state.json`, `coverage.json` or `report.md` made outside `dla` is reported as tamper by the gate, `dla report` and `dla run status` until `dla run reseal --reason`. The archetype-table gate is a discipline aid, not a barrier |
| `onFailure: block` on PreToolUse handlers | unverified (version) | The field is documented for Claude Code 2.1.295 and later; the live check ran on 2.1.321 | README states the minimum version; on older versions a crashing hook fails open (the handlers themselves exit 2 on malformed input, so only a missing binary fails open) |
| Live hook check (2026-11-10, Claude Code 2.1.321, a small model, `bench/runner/tests/live_hooks.sh`) | verified | Write of `report.md`: PreToolUse deny, file not written. Read of `go-archetypes.md` before the classify gate: denied, canary text absent. After `dla gate check --stage classify`: read allowed, canary returned. `dla:worker` subagent: SubagentStop hook started and responded. Cost about $0.02 for the four runs | Hooks fire from `hooks/hooks.json` via `--plugin-dir` with `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/bin/dla`. Not separated: whether the SKILL.md frontmatter copy fires as well (the plugin-level entry fires either way). Skill hooks inside a subagent were not tested; hooks.json covers the worker |
| `effort` frontmatter for the skill | deferred (decision D13) | The skills documentation lists an `effort` frontmatter field for current Claude Code; it was not exercised in a live session here, and the plan's "high effort" is advice in the skill text | `SKILL.md` keeps one sentence ("Work at high effort") and sets no `effort:` key, so the benchmark arms (prose, gates, full) differ only in what they ship. `crates/dla-cli/tests/skill_text.rs` keeps the key out. Revisit after the M6 benchmark, with one extra arm |
| A worker cannot pass the classify gate | open (found in package E) | `dla --worker W gate check --stage classify` records nothing (only the core records a stage), and the read hook tests the core's `stages_passed`; the `dla:worker` hooks come from `hooks/hooks.json`, so a worker that reads `references/<lang>-archetypes.md` is denied until the core passes classify, which in a dispatched run happens after the workers finish | `agents/worker.md` tells workers to use `dla ledger label F --suggest` (it needs no file) and not to work around the denial. A later fix could record a per-worker classify stage in the slice, or let the read hook accept a worker whose slice passes the stage |

## Bench policy and graders (M6)

`dla-bench dynamic` runs the M6 steps after the investigation (`bench/runner/src/dynamic/policy.rs`; `bench/README.md` lists them). Results of the run on 34 fixtures with release binaries after the fix rounds: 32 pass, 2 expected failures (the two M5 ones, the policy cannot read the code), 0 failures, and every final gate passes with no known gap. The same with `--archetypes off`: see `bench/README.md`.

| Item | Status | Evidence | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bench policy: the finding's own site stands as the adding site when no writer is recorded | policy choice | The mechanical policy cannot read the code. The census recorded a writer for the owner of a finding on few fixtures (python `ReportBuilder.category_totals` has none), so `ledger explain` (which needs a writer) would refuse every time. The site is the line the leak was located at (the allocating or retaining line) | Reported in the policy notes of each run. It says nothing about the benchmark arms: the model reads the code and records its writers |
| `ledger explain` resolves census writers by the finding's owner only | verified | A detector finding at the same site as a census finding got no census writers (`rust-tree-parent-links` F2: `explain-no-writer` although the census had a writer for the census finding at the same site). The policy read the census by site and the ledger by owner and disagreed | The policy reads the census by owner, like the ledger. Not a ledger defect, but a trap for an agent: two findings at one site can differ in what `explain` accepts |
| `label-evidence-missing` fixes the policy can follow | verified | `native-frame-decoder`: the refusal prints `dla ledger label F1 --as L-HANDLE --evidence E10,E14` (the ledger has the records, the finding does not cite them); accepted when run as printed. The summarize fix (`--finding F`) ties a growth column but not a record, see the next rows | The policy follows the printed `--evidence` list, then runs the summarize commands that already ran again with `--finding F`, then retries once more |
| Label gap: alloc-stack records tie only through a root key against the owner | corrected (package H2) | `rust-meter-callbacks` (L-OWNERSHIP) and the native leak of `jni-order-codec` (L-HANDLE): after `dla summarize alloc-stack-diff <3 profiles> --finding F1` the call printed `"tied": ["F1"]` and its growth column tied by site (`src/session.rs:19`), but the label reported `lacks alloc-stack` and `E19 names no holder of F1`. valgrind and heaptrack stacks have no root; the detector and correlation findings have no owner. The strict tie was computed after the stack record was written, so the record carried none | The alloc-stack record now states the tie its growing stacks got: `finding`, `candidates` and `ties` (`stack`, `finding`, `tie` with `rule` and `key`), at most 3 ties, only for stacks that pass the dump-series rule and tie by the tie rule (exact holder or exact site/recorded writer; `--finding` only narrows). `dla ledger label` already counts a record whose `finding` or `candidates` name the finding; no looser match (no `SITE_TOLERANCE` for stacks) was added. A stack that ties to nothing writes nothing. Tests: `summarize_native::the_alloc_stack_record_states_the_tie_its_growing_stack_got`, `m6_rules::an_alloc_stack_record_counts_for_the_findings_its_stated_ties_name_only`. `ledger strong` still reads `names_holder`, which needs a root key for an alloc-stack record (not widened) |
| Label gap: L-TLS on `jvm-invoice-render` | corrected (package H2) | The finding's owner was `billing.RenderContext.<clinit>`. Cause: the class-file census read the call `ThreadLocal.withInitial` in `<clinit>` and named the owner after the compiler's method; the field raw at the same line has owner `...SEGMENTS`, and `sort_dedup` kept the first of the two by owner name (`<` sorts before `S`) | A holder created in `<clinit>` or `<init>` is owned by the field the same statement stores into (the next `putstatic`/`putfield` of the class on the same line; Kotlin `object` and companion properties compile the same way). The tree-sitter Java path does the same for a static block (`BLOCK = ...`) and a constructor (`this.x = ...`); a local variable keeps its method. When no field is stored the owner stays the initializer, and `sort_dedup` prefers a field owner to an initializer owner at one line. Tests: `jvm_class::a_thread_local_made_in_clinit_is_owned_by_the_field_it_is_stored_in`, `java::a_holder_created_in_an_initializer_is_owned_by_its_field`. A call in `<clinit>` with no store (`new Thread(..).start()`, a registration) keeps the `Class.<clinit>` owner (open) |
| Label gap: L-UNLOAD on `jvm-extension-reload` | corrected (package H2) | The class-loader columns of `summarize non-heap` tie to no finding (`--finding` ties only a column that already ties by holder, site or type). `summarize heap-graph --finding F3` wrote a discriminator naming `static:reports.EventBus.LISTENERS`, but the per-dump retention-path records listed only the three largest holders, so none named F3's holder | When `--finding` names a finding whose holder the dumps resolve, each dump's retention-path record adds that holder's entry to the largest three (and keeps it if the record must be cut to the size bound, dropping the smallest of the largest first). Test: `summarize_jvm::the_holder_a_finding_names_is_in_every_retention_path_record_even_when_small` |
| A `thread-local:<Class>.<field>` holder key names no owner | corrected (package H2) | `names_holder` read `static:` and `field:` keys only, so the dla-hprof key of a static `ThreadLocal`'s values (`thread-local:billing.RenderContext.SEGMENTS`) named no field: after the owner fix the L-TLS label still reported `E17 names no holder of F6` | The key is read as the static field it names, like `static:`. Exact field identity, no looser match; `thread:<name>` and `jni-global:` keys still name no field. Test: `holders::a_thread_local_holder_key_names_its_static_field`. With the fixes of this package `dla-bench dynamic` accepts L-TLS on `jvm-invoice-render`, L-UNLOAD on `jvm-extension-reload` (after the policy's `--evidence` retry) and L-OWNERSHIP on `rust-meter-callbacks`; the native L-HANDLE of `jni-order-codec` is still not labelled (open: its native stacks are shim groups, written as `histogram-diff` by `type-histogram-diff`, and the policy runs `alloc-stack-diff` on the JFR recordings only, so no native `alloc-stack` record exists to tie) |
| Retention-path records carry no holder kind, so `strong-holder-mismatch` never fires | corrected (package H2) | `dla-hprof` and `dla-v8-snapshot` retention-path results listed `key`, `retained`, `dominated` only; `named_holder_kind` found no `holder_kind` to compare with the finding's holder | Every `holders` entry (and every `via` entry of dla-hprof) carries `holder_kind`, the value the same holder's growth column carries (`observed_holder`: the kind of the retention root). `ledger strong` therefore refuses a retention record whose holder kind differs from the finding's holder (`strong-holder-mismatch`, fix `ledger annotate --holder <kind> --evidence <growth evidence>`). Consequence: a census finding of holder `registration-table` or `container-field` on a static field, which the dump shows as `static/global`, is refused strong until its holder is corrected; this is the intended check, not a loosening. Tests: `summarize_jvm::retention_paths_state_the_holder_kind_of_each_holder`, `summarize_node::retention_path_holders_state_the_kind_of_their_retention_root`, `m6_rules::a_stated_holder_kind_must_agree_with_the_finding` |
| Label tie for kinds whose result names no finding | corrected (package H2) | Package B counted a `census-holder`, `static-hit`, `histogram-diff`, `non-heap`, `should-be-dead` or `residual` record when "the finding or its family cites it". The family includes the sibling hypotheses, whose citations (the discriminators that refuted them) are not evidence about the root | A record counts when its result names the finding (`finding`/`candidates`) or the finding itself cites it (`supports`: origin, moves up to confirmation). The family still supplies the default candidate list for `--evidence`, but a sibling's or root's citation is no tie. No existing test needed the family. Test: `m6_rules::a_record_that_names_no_finding_counts_through_the_findings_own_citation_only`. Replay of a v6 line labelled on a family-cited record would now fail; no such ledger exists outside this milestone |
| JVM `ThreadLocal` probe with no value on any live thread | corrected (package H2) | When the thread maps could be read but no live platform thread held a value of the field, the probe returned `size` null, `reachable` 0, `bytes` 0 and no error: columns of zeros, which read as a flat series | The probe abstains (error, no figures, a limitation `no live platform thread holds a value of this ThreadLocal`): zero values listed is not zero values held (virtual threads are not listed by `Thread.getAllStackTraces`, a thread may have ended, the values may not exist yet). The limitation for unreadable maps no longer says `needs --add-opens`: the agent opens `java.base/java.lang` itself with `Instrumentation.redefineModule`. Tests: `harness_run::jvm_agent_probe_abstains_when_no_live_thread_holds_a_threadlocal_value`, and `jvm_agent_probe_measures_threadlocal_values_on_pooled_threads` still passes. A point with no value in a series that has values elsewhere is a gap there |
| Archetype rows without a source | corrected (package H2) | `rust-archetypes.md` said the blocks of an `Rc` cycle "are unreachable once the entry point returns" and a leaked `Box` pointer is "(definitely lost)" with no source; `jvm-archetypes.md` L-UNLOAD said one live reference "keeps the loader and all its classes" | Rust cells now say what the Valgrind manual (section 4.2.10) defines: "definitely lost" is a block no pointer reaches, "indirectly lost" a block only lost blocks point at (source [V1], fetched). JLS 12.7 (fetched) says a class may be unloaded if and only if its defining loader may be reclaimed, and that a reclaimable loader has no live reference to it or to any class it defined, from instances or code; the row states that and the consequence (a registry, static or thread-local that still references the loader, a class or an instance stops the unload), not the stronger sentence. `native-archetypes.md` L-HANDLE has the same unsourced "definitely lost" wording (open) |
| Final gate: the columns of a trend `dla alloc-test` writes cannot be tied | corrected (package H1) | 27 of 34 fixtures ended `dla gate check --final` with `trend_column T2:... is not flat and is tied to no finding`, fix `dla trend S3`. T2 is the trend of the alloc-test series; no growth evidence names it, and `dla trend S3` wrote T3 and left T2 untied | Rule: a trend column is held to the newest trend of its series. A non-flat column of an older trend is replaced when a later trend of the same series analysed a column of that name and that column is itself non-flat (`dla trend S3` after `alloc-test` moves the gate to T3, whose fix is the `ledger add --origin growth-series` the gate prints); a later trend in which the column is flat does not retire it (revised in the R1 fix round, see below). A column the `alloc-test` discriminator names (its `trend` and `columns` fields) is also tied to every finding that cites that discriminator, and the final gate wants one of them dispositioned; a column the discriminator did not test is not covered. Real untied growth stays missing. Tests: `gate_h1` (`a_trend_replaced_by_...`, `an_alloc_test_trend_is_dispositioned_...`) |
| Final gate: the residual open item of a merged ledger | corrected (package H1) | `jni-order-codec`: the worker's open item is `jvm/O1` and the merged ledger resolves it, but `coverage.json` names `"open_item": "O1"` (and the core had an `O1` of its own) | The gate matches a plain id against the same number under a worker prefix when that item is resolved and names the residual's region (`unexplained growth: ... in heap (S1)`). Test: `gate_h1::a_residual_row_naming_a_workers_open_item_...` |
| Final gate: `explanation` of an artifact finding | corrected (package H1) | `jvm-rfq-dump-series`: the finding has an owner and no site, no source file exists, and `ledger explain` refused `explain-no-writer`, so the gate item could not be met | On an artifact run the owner replaces the site in the text check, `--adds` may be left out (no writer can be known without source, and a given `--adds` is not compared with writers), `--removal` must be `not-found` (a dump shows no remover; other values are refused `explain-remover`) and `--tag observed` needs a retention path. The gate prints that command for an artifact run. Repository runs are unchanged. Tests: `artifact_rules::an_artifact_finding_is_explained_without_a_writer_...`, `gate_h1::the_final_gate_explanation_of_an_artifact_finding_can_be_met` |
| Final gate: `coverage` of a runtime the harness exercised | corrected (package H1) | Rust fixtures (`rust-meter-callbacks`, `rust-tree-parent-links`, `jni-order-codec`) ended with `no census has run for runtime native` and `no sweep has run for runtime native` because the valgrind and heaptrack captures add the native runtime to the coverage | The sweep stage asks for a census and a sweep only for runtimes the probe detected from the sources (the `runtimes` of the probe evidence, over all probes); with no probe evidence every coverage row is held to it. A model that ran only `--lang rust` now meets the item. Test: `gate_h1::a_capture_tool_does_not_make_its_runtime_need_...` |
| The sweep stage counted the row `dla probe` wrote | corrected (package H1) | The probe wrote a `dla` backend row (`ran`, 0 items) for every runtime, and the sweep stage read that row as "a sweep has run" | The probe no longer writes the built-in rules' row; only `dla sweep` does. The missing-backend rows of the probe (tool not found, with the install hint) are unchanged. Test: `gate_h1::a_probe_does_not_count_as_a_sweep` |
| A worker cannot unlock the archetype tables | corrected (package H1), unverified (live) | Only the core's passing `gate check --stage classify` recorded `stages_passed`, so in a dispatched run the read hook kept the tables locked for the workers | `dla --worker W gate check --stage S` records a pass under `worker_stages_passed.W` in `state.json` (the core's `stages_passed` is untouched). The read hook (and the Bash hook) lets a call inside a subagent through when its worker passed `classify`. The worker is found like the SubagentStop gate finds it: the last `--worker <name>` on a `dla` Bash command in the subagent's transcript. A PreToolUse call carries `agent_id` and the main `transcript_path`, not `agent_transcript_path` (documented for SubagentStop only); the subagent's file is taken as `<dir of transcript_path>/<its stem>/subagents/agent-<agent_id>.jsonl`, the layout the SubagentStop example shows. That path was not seen in a live PreToolUse call: if the file does not exist the core rule applies (deny), so a wrong guess fails closed. Check it with `bench/runner/tests/live_hooks.sh`. Tests: `gate_hooks::read_hook_opens_the_tables_to_a_subagent_...` |
| Hooks after the run ends | verified (by design) | The hooks resolve the run from `cwd` and `.dla/current`; a run whose status is complete, incomplete or aborted is not active | Every hook allows everything then (no output, exit 0): writes to `.dla/` and reads of the archetype tables are not guarded after the run. The integrity seals stay the guarantee: `report`, `gate check` and `run status` still compare each protected file with its seal and report a mismatch as a tamper item. Documented in `references/safety-and-effects.md` |
| `dla intervene` on JVM harnesses | partly verified | `jvm-order-cache`, `jvm-audit-chain`, `jvm-extension-reload`, `jvm-invoice-render`: the clone builds (`build_ok`), the edit is scoped, and every measured process exits 1 (`effect: invalid`, guards `run_ok` and `same_points` false). Checked on `jvm-order-cache`: the stub removes `ORDERS_BY_ID.put(...)` and the project's own test (`OrderServiceTest.repeatedRequestReturnsSameOrder`) asserts the cached order. The other three were not read | The effect is informational in the bench. A workload that asserts what the stubbed writer provides cannot show `growth-removed`; a `--patch` that keeps the call and removes the retention is the way, which needs reading the code |
| `dla intervene` results of the dynamic run (34 fixtures, release binaries) | informational | `growth-removed` 4 (python-ingest-two-leaks x2, node-retry-scheduler, node-socket-heartbeat), `invalid` 4 (JVM), `intervene-not-statement` 7 (the recorded writer is a declaration, a decorator or a condition), `intervene-no-control` 3 (node-price-board and node-socket-heartbeat: no passing growth record of the harness is tied to the finding), not tried 2 (multi-runtime fixture) | The writers are the finding's site (the policy cannot find the adding statement); an agent that reads the code stubs the statement |
| `ledger strong` reachability | informational | 9 findings reached `strong` in 39 attempts of the dynamic run (python-ingest-two-leaks 2, node-retry-scheduler 2, node-socket-heartbeat, jvm-audit-chain, jvm-order-cache, native-frame-decoder, the Kotlin leak of jni-order-codec); 2 findings had no eligible record. Refusals: `strong-second-class-missing` 18, `strong-not-independent` 12 | Matches the survey: strong needs a second class the default flow rarely writes. `confirmed` stays a valid end state |
| Graders: `verification` records | verified | `bench/GRADING.md` "Verification"; both graders produce the same `verification` block on `testdata/verify-sample` and on every committed result set (none has a counting record, so none gets the block). The pre-schema-3 form of the record (`"result": "agrees"`) counts for nothing | The block appears in `metrics.json` and `table.md` only for groups that have a record |
| Hook processes find the dla binary in a runner child | verified (mechanism), unverified (live) | `tests/run_fake.rs` runs the first hook command of the `full` variant (`"${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/bin/dla" ...`) from the environment the runner builds: the real shim reaches the binary through `DLA_BIN` (copy next to the working copies) and exports `DLA_PLUGIN_ROOT` and `DLA_DATA_DIR`. Whether Claude Code fires the hooks inside a `claude -p` child with the sandbox settings is the live check (`tests/live_hooks.sh`) | Read `stop_blocks` and `incomplete_reason` in the `run.json` of the first `full` runs: all zero and absent means the hooks may not have fired |
| Report status follows the final gate (R1) | corrected | `dla report` printed `Status: COMPLETE` for a symptom run with every checklist item answered and no consent, while `dla gate check --final` and `dla run status` listed the consent item. `view::status` only knew findings, open items, checklist, run status and tamper | `dla-report` takes its status line and its open list from `gate::missing(Final)`, the evaluation `run status` and the stop hooks use; the counts still come from `status`. Goldens named `complete` now render INCOMPLETE (the fixture ledgers lack consent, probe and sweep rows); `gate_complete_view` in `crates/dla-report/tests/render.rs` is the passing case. Test: `status_follows_the_final_gate` |
| Write killed between the write and its seal (R1) | corrected | A slice torn mid-append, or any protected file written by a `dla` process killed before `seal_file`, stayed a mismatch for good: `Guard::new` saw file != seal, skipped every later seal, and neither the worker's `ledger done` nor `ledger merge` touched the seal. Reproduced with the binary | A guarded write leaves `integrity/<file>.pending` until its seal is written. With a marker present the file is accepted as dla's own interrupted write only when the sealed bytes are still its prefix (appended files) or it equals the content the writer announced (`Guard::expect`, used by `state.json` and `coverage.json`). The next `dla` write of the file and `dla ledger merge` seal it again; the gate's fix text for such an item names `dla ledger merge`. A hand edit has no marker and stays a mismatch. Limits: a stale marker left by a crash would let a later append-only hand edit pass at the next `dla` write of that file (the threat model is accident, not an attacker); `report.md` is not guarded this way (the next `dla report` replaces it). A torn slice line the merge has dropped is no longer listed again by the final gate. Tests: `crates/dla-core/tests/interrupted_seal.rs` |
| Stalled worker item (R1) | corrected | `WorkerEntry.stalled` was written and never read, so a worker the stop gate let go, after `ledger done` with findings still open, got no item at merge although `recovery.md` said it did | `dla ledger merge` opens "worker <w> stalled: ..." once for a stalled worker whose slice is done and which still has undispositioned findings or items, and adds "stalled" to the "did not finish" item of one that never ended its slice. The merge report lists the workers under `stalled`. Tests: `slices::merge_opens_an_item_for_a_stalled_worker_...`, `a_stalled_worker_with_nothing_left_open_gets_no_item`, `a_stalled_worker_that_did_not_finish_says_so_in_its_item` |
| Worker stop counter digest (R1) | corrected | The digest summed the lines of every worker's slice, so a sibling writing between two stop attempts reset a stuck worker's counter | `digest_of` takes the worker and counts its own slice only. Test: `a_siblings_slice_writes_do_not_reset_a_stuck_workers_counter` |
| Stop hook wording (R1) | corrected | The stamp said "blocked 3 times", but the third attempt is the one the hook lets through | The reason reads "stop hook let the stop through after 3 stop attempts with no ledger change: <ids>"; docs say the first two attempts are blocked. `state.json` field docs (`stop_blocks`, `workers[].stalled`) still say "blocked three times"; they live in `state.rs` and the generated schema, outside this round |
| Write hook scope (R1) | corrected | The write hook protected the sealed run files only. `trend/`, `series/`, `gate/`, `bundle/`, `intervene/`, `alloc/`, `probe/` and the harness output are read by the gates and carry no seal, so an edit passed | `dla gate write-hook` denies `Write`, `Edit` and `NotebookEdit` of every path under `.dla/` except `<run>/harness/H<n>/driver/**`, the one place the skill has the model edit (the file `dla harness gen --driver` prints). The Bash hook applies the same exception to a command whose every `.dla` path is a driver file. `SKILL.md` and `safety-and-effects.md` say which files the seals cover and which only the hook covers. The skill names no other file for the model to edit, so there is no separate agent-hook allowance (the `DLA-HOOK` bodies are in the driver file). Tests: `gate_hooks` (`write_hook_denies_the_generated_files_...`, `bash_hook_denies_writes_to_the_generated_files_...`) |
| Final gate: re-trend and whole-process columns (R1) | corrected | (a) a later trend of a series retired an older non-flat column of the same name even when the later column was flat (a shorter window), hiding growth; (b) the alloc-test discriminator tied every column it listed, whole-process ones (`os.*`, `cgroup.*`, totals) included, to a finding that cites it, which the tie rule forbids | (a) an older column is retired only by a later trend whose column of that name is non-flat. (b) `gate::is_whole_process` columns (`os.`, `cgroup.`, `fd.`, `gap.`, `jvm.`, `go.`, `node.`, `py.`, `native.` totals without a site, `metric:*/total`) are never tied. The classify stage leaves them out; the final gate takes them as accounted for when `dla gap --residual` wrote rows for the column's region (all rows for `os.*`) that are under their threshold or over it with a resolved open item, or when a resolved open item names the column and trend. Fix text: `dla gap <S> --residual`. Tests: `gate_h1::a_flat_re_trend_does_not_retire_an_older_growing_column`, `a_whole_process_column_is_never_tied_and_is_accounted_for_by_the_residual` |
| Bench policy follows every fix the final gate prints (H3) | policy choice | After the fix rounds the gate prints a fix for every item the dynamic run met: `dla trend <S>` and then the `ledger add --origin growth-series` it prints for the new trend; `dla gap <S> --residual` for a whole-process column; `ledger explain F --tag inferred --removal not-found --text "<owner, holder, no remover found>"` for an artifact finding. The policy runs them, the way a model following the skill would. The earlier known gaps (alloc-test trend columns, the merged residual item, the artifact explanation) are gone and `known_gaps` is empty on all 34 fixtures | An item left over after the rounds is a failure of the fixture, not a known gap. `known_gap` in `policy.rs` returns nothing and stays as the place to name a gap together with a row here |
| Expected failures that now pass (H3) | corrected | `jni-order-codec`, `jvm-invoice-render`, `jvm-extension-reload` and `rust-meter-callbacks` reported `listed as an expected failure but it passes`: the labels are accepted after the H2 fixes. The entries and their `bench/README.md` rows are removed; the stale-entry check stays strict | Two entries remain (`node-socket-heartbeat`, `rust-tree-parent-links`). The `needs_archetypes` flag stays for a future label entry |
| `jni-order-codec` label needs no `shim-alloc-stacks` call in the policy (H3) | verified | The native L-HANDLE label is accepted on the run with the calls the policy already makes | Nothing added |
| `go-buffer-pool` alloc-test is not stable (H3, closed) | corrected | The non-leak fixture `go-buffer-pool` failed `no rule-out on a non-leak fixture` in about half the runs at `--parallel 2` (CI runs 67 and later, H3: 3 of 5 with `--archetypes off`, 0 of 5 on; the archetypes setting is not involved). In a failing run the alloc-test on `go.heap_live` and `go.heap_objects` read `growth-continues` in the extra window (main 3.2 extra 3.2 rise 9,919 noise 1,676; main 5.2 extra 4.0 rise 12,230 noise 1,324; main 3.5 extra 3.9 rise 12,040 noise 2,817), so the candidate stayed unresolved. Cause: the exit-dump live heap of this workload is about 160 KB at n=440 and about 180 KB from n=3,500 on, and the 20 KB difference is the Go runtime's own bookkeeping, not the harness and not the fixture: the heap profile at exit (`MemProfileRate` 1) shows `runtime.acquireSudog` (called from `gcMarkDone`) growing from 1.6 KB to 20 KB, `runtime.allocm` from 6 KB to 18 KB and `makeProfStackFP` from 4.5 KB to 6.9 KB between n=440 and n=7,480, and nothing of the harness or the fixture grows with n (the harness retains no record; `DLA_TICK_MS=0` gives the same 160 to 178 KB). The level then scatters by 3 to 5 KB between points and between runs, and the MAD noise of the 8-point extra window reads 1.0 to 2.8 KB, so a rise of 4 to 7% of the level passes the 3-noise-unit test in some runs (reproduced under CPU load: 1 of 8 windows, rise 13,350 = 7.3% of the level, noise 2,664; and once idle, rise 7,020 = 3.9%, noise 1,330, extra slope 2.3 against main 4.4). | The estimator is fixed, not the effect size. (1) The extra window's noise is the larger of its own MAD and the residual scatter (MAD of the residuals around the Sen fit) of the main window of the same column, so scatter the main window shows cannot pass as growth. (2) Alone this was not enough: over the 56 recorded windows of `go.heap_live` and `go.heap_objects` of this fixture (the runs of this investigation and H3) 4 windows read growth-continues falsely under the old rule, and 4 still did under (1) (rises 7,784 and 12,040 against 3 noise units of 7,597 and 8,451), the main window being no noisier than the extra one. So a gauge in bytes needs a rise of at least 32 KiB (`MIN_BYTE_RISE` in `alloc/judge.rs`) to be growth at all; a smaller rise is a plateau whatever the noise reads. The largest false rise measured is 13.4 KB, so 32 KiB is 2.4 times that. The value is a placeholder for M7 calibration. It is a cost: a leak of a small heap that rises less than 32 KiB over the extra window is called a plateau (its main-window trend and the other alloc-test columns still apply). The relative floor of the first variant (10% of the level) is gone; the 1% floor with its process reference stays. Tests: `the_main_windows_scatter_bounds_the_extra_windows_noise`, `a_main_window_shorter_than_three_points_adds_no_noise`, `a_byte_rise_under_the_minimum_is_a_plateau_however_clean`, `a_precise_leak_under_one_percent_of_rss_is_not_a_plateau` (original leak size restored). Verified: `go-buffer-pool` 10 of 10 pass with the go fixtures running together at `--parallel 3` (5 with `--archetypes off`, 5 on); full 34-fixture runs at `--parallel 2`, archetypes on and off: 32 pass and 2 expected failures (`node-socket-heartbeat`, `rust-tree-parent-links`) in each; on every fixture that ran an alloc-test, no leak-side column turned from growth-continues to plateau or non-leak (the one change is the decoy `go-subscription-workers-closed`, whose `go.heap_live` read growth-continues on a rise of 5,099 and now reads plateau) |
| `go-subscription-workers` fails `gate check --final` on `trend_column T6:metric:goroutine/total` | corrected | Found in the full run after the first `go-buffer-pool` fix; it fails the same way with the build of commit 124d917. The total of the goroutine-group series (`dla summarize non-heap`, series S6) is a whole-process column (`metric:*/total`): since R1 it is never tied to a finding, and the gate accounted for it only through residual rows of the same series. Since R3 (`residual_covers` filters rows by series) the rows of S1 no longer cover S6, and `dla gap S6 --residual` is refused (`residual-unavailable`: the series has no heap, off-heap or non-heap byte column to total). H3's run of the same fixture passed because before R3 any residual row of the run covered every series. So the R3 per-series change caused it | The total of a summarize-made series is the sum of its group columns, so the gate accounts for `metric:<category>/total` when at least one group column of the trend is non-flat and every non-flat group column is tied to a dispositioned finding (`gate::group_columns_state`). The total is still never tied to a finding. When a group is not yet tied the item names it and prints the fix of the group (`ledger add --origin growth-series`, then resolve); when no group is non-flat the item says nothing accounts for the total and prints `dla trend S`. `dla trend` no longer hints `dla gap --residual` for such a total. No command opens an open item for a trend column (open items come from `dla gap --residual`, `ledger verify` and the merge), so the open-item route the gate accepts for other whole-process columns has no way in for this one; that is left as it is. Test: `the_total_of_a_summarize_series_is_accounted_for_by_its_tied_groups`. Verified: `go-subscription-workers` passes alone and in the full runs |

| The final gate asked for the trend of the series `dla gap` derives, and then for a disposition of its `gap.accounted` column | corrected | Smoke run 2026-11-10, `go-subscription-workers`: the model ran `dla trend` on the gap series S6; the gate then required residual accounting for `T6:gap.accounted`, which is the residual accounting itself; the stop hook blocked three times and stamped the run INCOMPLETE | A series of source kind `gap` needs no trend and its trend's columns need no disposition: the residual rows of the source series and their open items govern it (`RunView.gap_series`, `gate.rs`). Test `gate_h1::a_gap_derived_series_needs_no_trend_and_its_trend_columns_no_disposition`. Rerun of the fixture: no stop block, no INCOMPLETE stamp |
| The goroutine leak pass never ran inside benchmark children | corrected | Smoke run: `p1-fresh-leak.log` read `verifying module: golang.org/toolchain@v0.0.1-go1.27.0.linux-amd64: Get "https://sum.golang.org/lookup/...": user denied`. Go verifies the toolchain module against the checksum database before it switches, and the child's sandbox has no network for sum.golang.org | The runner now copies the host's checksum database cache (`cache/download/sumdb`, about 5 MB, filled when the host fetched the toolchain) into each child's module cache, next to the linked toolchain. Verification stays on and works offline (checked: the same switch with `GOPROXY=off` and no network fails without the copy and prints `go version go1.27.0` with it). Rerun: coverage row `goroutineleak: ran, go1.27.0` |
| Usage per run on the full ablation | measured | Smoke run: 4 runs, Claude Code reported `total_cost_usd` 2.32 in all (0.35 to 0.80 per run, mean 0.58), 23 to 49 turns, mean wall 239 s. The M5 gates run measured 0.25 per run | Input to the M6 benchmark sizing; benchmark children use the session's subscription login, so these figures are usage, not charges |

| Item | Status | Evidence | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| Two overlapping `dla alloc-test` commands wrote the same ledger `seq` | corrected | Benchmark run `full/archetypes-on/go-buffer-pool/1`: both commands appended evidence with seq 41 and every later ledger command refused `bad-seq`. Cause: the lock covered only the file write and the seal, while each process had replayed the ledger earlier and numbered its line from that stale copy | Every writer of the main ledger now runs under one exclusive `ledger.lock`: it takes the lock, replays the file again when its size changed, builds the record (IDs included) from that tail, writes the line and updates the seal, then releases the lock (`Ledger::locked`). The lock is taken for unsealed runs too. Worker slices keep their per-handle lock. Tests `ledger_concurrency` (core: threads with stale handles, sealed and unsealed; CLI: 14 `dla ledger add` processes) fail without the change |
| A ledger an older binary left with a repeated `seq` could not be repaired from the refusal text | corrected | Same run | The `bad-seq` refusal now says that two writers appended the same seq and which lines are intact. `dla` has no command that rewrites ledger history: the text says to keep a copy, renumber `ledger.jsonl` by hand, run `dla run reseal --reason ...` (it records an audit line) or start a new run. Test `a_ledger_with_a_repeated_seq_names_the_repair` |
| Long commands ignored the run deadline | corrected | Benchmark runs `python-report-memo-bounded` (two timeouts at the runner's 1500 s): `harness gen --iterations 8 --step 3000`, `harness run --capture histogram,alloc --mode both`, `alloc-test --extra 3`. `require_time` only checked that the deadline had not passed when the command started | `harness run`, `alloc-test` and `intervene` estimate their duration from a least-squares line through the points already measured (an earlier series of the harness, the control series for `intervene`, else the first two points of the run) and refuse with `time-budget` when the estimate exceeds the time left less a reserve (the smaller of 20 s and a tenth). The fix names a smaller `--iterations`, `--step` or `--extra`. A point or driver that outlasts the deadline is killed at it. The series, the evidence and the output then say `partial run`, and `alloc-test` forces `favors: inconclusive` with no supports, so a partial result never refutes. Tests `harness_run::a_run_that_cannot_fit_the_deadline_is_refused_before_its_last_points`, `a_point_that_outlasts_the_deadline_is_cut_and_the_partial_series_says_so`, `a_known_pace_refuses_before_the_first_point`, `time_budget::*`, `intervene::python_an_intervention_that_cannot_fit_the_run_budget_is_refused_before_cloning`, `alloc_cmd::a_partial_run_never_favours_either_side`. `SKILL.md`, `agents/worker.md` and `references/recovery.md` had said a `time-budget` refusal means resolving everything as `time_budget`; they now say to run the smaller command the fix names, and to stop only when the deadline has passed or nothing smaller fits (test `skill_text::a_time_budget_refusal_that_names_a_smaller_plan_is_followed_not_ended`) |
| The time estimate is provisional | open | Single-size pace is proportional to the point size, which overestimates when start-up dominates; the first run of a harness decides after two points | Revisit with measured points from the M7 calibration; the 20 s reserve and the two-point decision are placeholders |
| A worker's slice lock is held for the life of the handle | open | `Ledger::load_for` keeps `ledger.<worker>.lock` until the command ends, so a long worker command (`alloc-test`) blocks the same worker's other ledger commands | Not changed here: slices already number correctly. Moving them to the take-lock-and-refresh scheme of the main ledger removes the wait |
| `ledger annotate --writer/--site` and `ledger add --site` took a line that is not the code | corrected | Benchmark run `gates/archetypes-on/node-price-board` (runs 2 and 3): `src/board.js:14` (a signature) and `:10` (a comment) were annotated while the holder `previous` was on line 15 | The command reads the project file and refuses a line outside the file, past its end, or blank or comment-only (comment syntax by extension: `//` and `/* */` for C-like languages, `#` for Python, Ruby and shell; the fix names `grep -n '<identifier>' <file>`). When the line lacks the last component of the owner, it still records and warns, naming the nearest lines (up to 3) that contain it. A file not under the project is a warning (`not found under the project`), not a refusal; an absolute path or one that climbs out of the project is never read, so no line outside the project is quoted back. Removers are not checked. Tests `ledger_m6::annotate_*`, `site_lines_use_the_comment_syntax_of_the_language`, `a_site_in_a_file_the_project_lacks_is_noted_not_refused`, `a_site_outside_the_project_is_never_read`, `ledger_add_checks_the_site_line_too`. Fixtures of other CLI tests that used blank, comment or out-of-file lines as sites were given code lines |
| `ledger label --suggest` hid rows and offered a forced fit | corrected | Benchmark runs `jvm-extension-reload` 2 and 3 never saw L-UNLOAD (cap of 6 rows); `node-retry-scheduler` took the first met row | `--suggest` lists every archetype of the finding's polarity, one string per row with its full summary: met rows (with the evidence to pass), then rows missing evidence (the kinds), then rows blocked by the holder (the holder and the row's holders) or an open sibling, then a closing `UNCLASSIFIED` row that says to compare each summary with the stated mechanism and gives the `--as UNCLASSIFIED --note` command. The commands that produce each missing kind sit in `produce` (once per kind), the annotate command for a wrong holder in `holder_fix`. The answer stays under 2048 bytes by shortening those commands (then dropping `next`), never rows: with the shipped table and 10 leak rows it fits. Tests `suggest_lists_met_then_missing_then_blocked_and_ends_with_unclassified`, `suggest_with_the_shipped_table_lists_every_leak_archetype_within_the_bound` |
| `ledger label --as` accepted an archetype the runtime's reference has no row for | corrected | Benchmark: `native-vendor-codec` expects L-OWNERSHIP, whose row is in the rust reference only. The warning added for it (`runtime_warning`) was ignored in the next runs: `node-retry-scheduler`/1 labelled L-STALE on node, `jvm-extension-reload`/2 labelled L-REGISTER on jvm | `dla ledger label F --as L-X` is refused with `label-runtime-row` (nothing written) when X is an archetype ID without a row in the finding's runtime reference, unless `--note` is given; the refusal names the runtime's rows and says to choose one or UNCLASSIFIED, or to keep X with `--note` saying why none fits. With `--note` the label is accepted and the note is stored in the `finding_labeled` record. `N-*` labels and UNCLASSIFIED are unaffected; when the runtime cannot be determined there is no check. The runtime is the language of the finding's site first (`.c .cc .cpp .h` native, `.java .kt` jvm, `.go`, `.js .mjs .cjs .ts` node, `.py`, `.rs`), else the worker prefix, else the worker of the command, else the only worker in `coverage.json` (benchmark `jni-order-codec`: the site of its L1 finding is `native/src/lib.rs`, the Rust JNI code of the jvm worker, so the runtime is rust, whose reference has no L-HANDLE row, and the label is refused without `--note`; that is the designed escape and the rule stays. The bench policy's `--note` retry after a `label-runtime-row` refusal covers `jni-order-codec` as it does `native-vendor-codec`. A `.c` site of a jvm worker takes the native rows without a note.) An unknown finding is reported as `unknown-finding` before the runtime row is looked at. `archetypes::runtime_rows` lists the rows, and a test keeps it equal to the `<lang>-archetypes.md` files. The check runs after the cheap ones: archetypes off, an unknown archetype, the finding's state and the label's polarity are refused first (read-only `labels::precheck`), so a `--note` is never asked for a label that `archetypes-off` or `label-state` would refuse next. `SKILL.md` and `agents/worker.md` say it in one sentence; `bench/runner/src/dynamic/policy.rs` follows the refusal and labels the expected cause with a `--note` (native `L-OWNERSHIP`). Tests `ledger_m6::a_label_with_no_row_in_the_runtime_reference_is_refused_without_a_note`, `a_note_keeps_a_label_with_no_row_in_the_runtime_reference`, `ledger_m6::archetypes_off_is_refused_before_the_runtime_row_check`, `the_labels_own_state_and_polarity_come_before_the_runtime_row_check`, `a_worker_finding_with_a_c_site_is_labelled_by_the_language_of_its_site`, `a_jvm_finding_at_a_rust_site_needs_a_note_for_l_handle` (a jvm worker finding at a `.rs` site labelled L-HANDLE: refused without `--note`, accepted with it), `the_labels_polarity_comes_before_the_runtime_row_check` (a confirmed non-leak sibling of the jvm worker, L-HANDLE: `label-polarity`, not `label-runtime-row`; fails without the precheck), `an_unknown_finding_is_reported_before_the_runtime_row_check` (fails without the unknown-finding precheck), `archetypes::runtime_rows_match_the_shipped_reference_files`, policy `a_cause_with_no_row_in_the_runtime_reference_is_labelled_with_a_note`. The label tests of other crates that used a cause outside the fixture's runtime pass `--note` |

Same test as above |
| The decoy `node-socket-heartbeat-cleared` could be confirmed honestly as a leak | corrected | Benchmark run `gates/archetypes-on/node-socket-heartbeat-cleared/3`: `LoopbackSocket.destroy()` never emitted `close` (a real `net.Socket` does), so a session the server ends stayed in the gateway map | `destroy()` emits `close` once, asynchronously (next tick), in both fixtures; `clientHangUp()` emits it once as well. A fixture unit test (`a session the server ends is released once the socket has closed`) fails without the change in the trap and passes in both. No comment names the cause (No-hints rule). Both READMEs carry the re-measured table (trap: 4.20 KB per connection, 16000 live intervals; decoy: 0 intervals, under 0.5 MB at every count). The follow-up benchmark then found a second defect in the same double (run `gates/archetypes-on/node-socket-heartbeat-cleared/3`, a correct confirmation): `Session.receive` went on dispatching the frames of a chunk after one of them (`bye`) closed the session, so a `subscribe` that followed it put the closed session into `Router.topics` after `close()` had released its topics. Both fixtures now stop dispatching once the session is closed; the fixture test `frames that follow bye in the same chunk are not dispatched` fails without the line. The workload never pipelines frames after `bye`, so the measurements did not change beyond noise |
| The dynamic policy left findings without a label decision | corrected | The final gate now names a `label` item for each confirmed leak-side finding | `bench/runner/src/dynamic/policy.rs`: the expected cause of a shipped row is labelled as before; any other confirmed finding (no expected leak, a differing holder, a cause without a shipped row, a refused label) reads `--suggest`, records the rows it met in the note and takes `--as UNCLASSIFIED --note`, because the mechanical policy cannot read the code. A `label` item left at the final gate is answered the same way. The bench policy passed only because it labels the census finding, whose owner `Board.latest` matches a `field:` key | `node_heapsnapshot.rs` now adds the holder the owner of the `--finding` finding names (exact owner, else the one holder it is a shorter form of; two holder keys with that owner add none) to each retention record, after the largest three, with its `holder_kind`; the size loop drops the smallest of the largest first and keeps the bound one (as `hprof.rs` does). `key_owner` maps `closure:<module>.<var>` to the owner the backend writes for that key; `registry:`, `instance:` and `thread:` keys still name no owner. Tests: `holders::tests::a_closure_key_names_the_captured_variable` (core, fails without the prefix), `summarize_node::the_holder_of_the_finding_is_in_every_retention_record_and_the_label_is_accepted` (real V8 snapshots of a program shaped like the fixture: the record lists the holder with `closure/continuation`, `ledger label F --as L-CAPTURE` is accepted citing it, `ledger strong` is refused for independence, not for the holder; without the `key_owner` change the label is refused `label-evidence-missing`), and unit tests of the bound-holder choice. Other producers: `hprof.rs` already did this; `vgdb.rs` writes one chain per point (no top-N list); there is no Python or Go retention-path producer. Not changed: `alloc-stack` keeps its `top` rows |
| Harness work-copy paths in node holder keys and owners; a wrong owner could not be corrected | corrected (paths); owner replacement built and removed | Benchmark runs `node-price-board/1` (F5 owner `.dla.<run>.harness.H1.work.src.board.previous`) and `/3` (the model added `src.board.Board.publish.previous`; the heap-graph output tied nothing to it and `ledger annotate --owner` refused to change an owner) | Stays: `module_of` names a script below `.dla/<run>/harness/<id>/work/` relative to the work copy, so keys and owners read `closure:src.board.previous` and `src.board.previous`, as the census names them (tests: `a_harness_work_copy_is_the_project_root_of_its_modules` and the integration test above); `ledger add --owner` and `ledger annotate --owner` warn (not refuse) when the owner names no holder in the growth or retention evidence they cite, listing up to three owners those records name, and read a `.count` (holder) or `.size`/`.bytes` (probe) column suffix only when the record also has the column without it (`holders::a_measure_suffix_is_stripped_only_when_the_record_has_the_bare_column`; a holder named `count` is read as `count`). Built and removed: an evidence-backed replacement of an owner (`annotate F --owner X --evidence E`, `replaced_owner`, `owner_changes`) was removed again after an adversarial check found that growth tied for one holder could confirm a finding about another in seven ways (site ties skipped, owner re-matching of probes, short probe names, holder columns with longer names, `type:` columns, ties written after the annotation, suffix misreads), and re-deriving every tie was not worth it. An owner is set once; `annotate --owner` only fills an unset owner and the refusal of a change says to add a finding with the owner the evidence names (`dla ledger add --origin growth-series --evidence <E> --site <file:line> --owner <owner>`) and merge the old one into it with `dla ledger duplicate <old> --of <new>`, or resolve it. Tests: `owner_correction::an_owner_already_set_is_never_replaced_and_the_refusal_names_the_fix`, `replay_refuses_a_line_that_carries_an_owner_change`, `ledger_owner::an_owner_already_set_is_refused_with_or_without_evidence_and_the_fix_names_the_way`. Schema 6 has no `replaced_owner`; `v5_replay` digests the plain Debug text, baseline unchanged. |
| `node-retry-scheduler` expects UNCLASSIFIED, but L-STALE's summary describes its mechanism | open | L-STALE says "A reference to logical state that is dead (removed, replaced or finished) is still held" (holders container-field and static/global). The fixture keeps finished attempt records reachable through `previous` links from the singleton's `latest` field; its README argues only against the collection, registration, closure and worker rows. Benchmark runs labelled it L-STALE (M6: 2 of 3; follow-up run 1: 3 of 3) with the evidence ties met; in follow-up run 2 the runtime-row refusal (no node row for L-STALE) led to UNCLASSIFIED in 3 of 3 | Not changed: the fixture and its expectation stay. Decide whether the fixture is an L-STALE leak (then `expected.toml` says L-STALE and the README argues the row) or whether L-STALE's summary or the fixture's README need a sentence that separates them |
| The label refusal led to a dead end | corrected | Benchmark run `gates/archetypes-on/jvm-extension-reload/2` (expected L-UNLOAD, F3 owner `reports.EventBus.LISTENERS`, confirmed early): `label F3 --as L-UNLOAD` was refused for `[growth-series + non-heap]`, `[unit-count-series + retention-path]` and `[growth-series + retention-path]`, and the fix named only `dla summarize non-heap ... or dla gap or dla harness run`. The model summarized the class-loader captures as non-heap (whole-process columns, tied to no finding), was refused again (`E22 (a non-heap record) names no finding and F3 does not cite it`) and took L-REGISTER. A record whose result names no finding counts only when the finding cites it, and a confirmed finding cannot cite a new record; the retention-path route (`dla summarize heap-graph <dumps> --finding F3`) was never named | Refusal text only; no tie was loosened. The fix names the producing command of every closest alternative of `requires` (fewest missing kinds, ties included), each command once, in the order of the list. When a record of a kind that names no finding (non-heap, census-holder, static-hit, histogram-diff, should-be-dead, residual) is excluded only because the finding does not cite it, the fix adds how such a record counts (cited when the finding moves, `dla ledger test\|confirm F --evidence E`, so before confirmation) and names the alternatives a confirmed finding can use. Under the 2048-byte bound `--suggest` keeps, in priority order: every row with its full summary (a row lists only the closest alternative's kinds; `or_missing` in the library), `produce` (short form first, so `next` fits; the long form with `--finding F` replaces it at the end only when everything still fits; the producers of the other alternatives are included, so the retention-path producer `dla summarize heap-graph` stays), `next`, `holder_fix`, `cited_only`; the first outputs that do not fit are dropped, never a row or a summary. For a confirmed or strong finding a row shows the closest alternative it can still meet: an alternative that needs a kind that names no finding and is not tied (it can no longer be cited) is passed over for the next closest one (the L-UNLOAD row of jvm-extension-reload reads `missing retention-path`, not `missing non-heap`); a row with no such alternative keeps its closest one. The note's list of the other alternatives leaves out every alternative that needs any such kind the finding does not cite, not only the kinds found among the records checked. The note fires only for a record excluded for lack of citation (not for authority) whose kind one alternative of the archetype needs; when every alternative needs such a kind it says no other alternative exists for a confirmed finding. Tests `m6_rules::a_refused_label_names_the_command_of_every_closest_alternative` (the exact refusal of this run), `a_refusal_without_a_non_heap_record_names_both_routes_and_no_note`, `suggest_rows_list_every_closest_alternative`, `the_note_says_no_other_alternative_exists_when_every_alternative_needs_such_a_kind`, `the_note_stays_silent_for_a_kind_no_alternative_needs`, `the_note_stays_silent_for_a_record_excluded_for_its_authority`, CLI `ledger_m6::suggest_for_a_jvm_finding_with_only_growth_tied_keeps_the_rows_and_the_heap_graph_command` (shipped table, the jvm-extension-reload shape: `next` and the heap-graph producer present, the L-UNLOAD row names retention-path), `suggest_never_drops_next_for_the_sake_of_the_long_form_of_produce` (fails with the long form first), core `m6_rules::suggest_shows_a_confirmed_finding_the_closest_alternative_it_can_still_meet` and `the_note_offers_no_alternative_that_needs_any_kind_the_finding_does_not_cite` (fail without the change); `suggest_rows_list_every_closest_alternative` now expects the retention-path alternative for the confirmed finding |
| Duplicates of one leak in the core ledger could not be merged | corrected | Benchmark run `gates/archetypes-on/python-report-memo/3`: three detector findings (F1-F3, `reports/builder.py:14`), the census finding F4 and four growth-series findings (F5-F8, owner `reports.builder.ReportBuilder.category_totals`) were all confirmed for one leak; F4 took L-COLLECT, the final gate's label item then made the model label the rest UNCLASSIFIED, and the grader takes the first confirmed match (F1). Only `dla ledger merge` wrote `finding_merged`, and only for findings of different workers | `dla ledger duplicate F --of G` (core and worker slices) writes `finding_merged` (canonical G, duplicate F) under the ledger lock when `duplicate::check` accepts the pair: both leak-side, top-level and not hypotheses, F not merged yet and G not a duplicate itself, neither refuted, and the findings are in one scope (the same worker, or both core, and the same runtime by the language of their sites when both are known) with exact identity: the same canonical owner (equality, not a dotted suffix; both owners qualified, a bare name such as `cache` matches nothing) or the same `file:line` site (tie rule: no tolerance of lines). The supports of F join G at replay, as in the cross-worker merge. In a worker slice both findings must be the worker's own, and the slice may now hold such a record (other `finding_merged` lines stay core-only). The replay check of `finding_merged` accepts the cross-worker case (different workers, as before) or the duplicate case (one scope, exact identity) and refuses the others. A refusal of `duplicate F --of G` when G is already a duplicate of F no longer names `--of F`. The report says "a duplicate of the same leak" for a merged finding, not "same site and holder". The final gate's `label` item already skipped merged duplicates; when an unlabelled confirmed finding has a labelled twin by the same rule, its fix starts with `dla ledger duplicate F --of G`. `SKILL.md` and `agents/worker.md` say it in one sentence. The Rust and Python graders already dropped the duplicate of a `finding_merged`; samples with core ids and with worker-prefixed ids were added. Tests `dla-core/tests/duplicate.rs` (12: `a_bare_owner_equal_on_both_sides_is_not_a_duplicate`, `the_worker_is_part_of_the_scope_in_core_mode` (a core finding against `jvm/F1`, and `jvm/F1` against `rust/F1`, for `duplicate` and for the replay check of a core finding against a worker's; fail without the worker comparison); merged state and carried supports, exact site, exact owner, scope, the python-report-memo shape, refuted refusal, self-merge fix), `gate_flow::the_label_item_of_a_duplicate_names_the_duplicate_command_first`, `ledger_m6::duplicate_refuses_...`, `grading_rules::a_core_duplicate_collapses_into_the_labelled_finding`, `test_grade.py::test_core_duplicate_collapses_into_the_labelled_finding` |
| The duplicate rule is not the cross-worker rule | corrected | The first rule used a site tolerance of 3 lines and dotted-suffix owners and did not check scope, so a core finding could merge into another worker's or runtime's, whose supports then counted there | `duplicate::duplicate_by` is exact identity in one scope (see the row above), as the tie rule requires. It adds no holder test: holders differ between a detector finding and a growth-series finding of one leak, and two findings at one site with the same owner are one place. Tests `duplicate::findings_of_another_runtime_by_site_language_are_not_duplicates_even_with_one_owner`, `a_site_that_is_not_the_same_line_is_not_a_duplicate` |
| A label without a row in the runtime's reference is refused, and some correct labels lack one | corrected in M7 (section M7) | Follow-up run 2 (`bench/results/m6-followup-2`): the refusal turned all three forced fits of `node-retry-scheduler` (L-STALE on node) into UNCLASSIFIED, and also refused L-HANDLE on `jni-order-codec`'s leak in the library's Rust JNI code (`native/src/lib.rs`: runtime rust, rows L-CYCLE and L-OWNERSHIP); the model then chose UNCLASSIFIED (#2) or L-OWNERSHIP (#3) instead of `--note`. Three fixtures expect a cause with no row in their runtime's reference (`jni-order-codec` L-HANDLE on rust, `native-vendor-codec` L-OWNERSHIP on native, `python-ingest-two-leaks` L-REGISTER on python) | Kept as a refusal. Decide in M7 whether the shipped rows should list these causes for those runtimes (the reference tables are the plan's D1 set), or whether the refusal text should state the case of code in another runtime's library |
| A timer finding's holder cannot be corrected from the resources capture | corrected in M7 (section M7) | Follow-up run 2, `node-socket-heartbeat` #1 and #3: the census gives the `setInterval` finding the holder `blocked-stack`; the capture that observes the timer table is `node-resources` (live timer groups tied by creation site), and a holder change accepts only heap-graph growth (`dla-hprof`, `dla-v8-snapshot`), so L-REGISTER stays blocked. The same gap keeps the fixture an expected failure of the mechanical acceptance | M7: let a passing `node-resources` timer or listener group tied to the finding by its creation site record `holder_kind registration-table`, and accept it for a holder change, with a tie test |
| `jvm.class:*` columns keep `jni-order-codec` INCOMPLETE | corrected in M7 (section M7) | Follow-up runs 1 and 2: the type-count columns of a JVM histogram series are not flat and tie to no finding; the fix the gate prints (`dla trend S<n>`) writes a new trend with the same columns | M7: give such columns a disposition path that the printed fix reaches (e.g. through `summarize histogram-diff` ties or the residual rows) |

## M7

| Item | Status | Evidence | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| A label for code in another runtime's library | corrected | `jni-order-codec`: L-HANDLE on the Rust JNI code (`native/src/lib.rs`) of a jvm worker finding was refused with the plain "no row" text, and the model chose UNCLASSIFIED or L-OWNERSHIP instead of `--note` | The D1 rows are kept. When the finding's site is in another runtime's code than its worker's, the refusal says so (a library the worker's program calls through JNI or FFI), lists that runtime's rows and prints `dla ledger label F --as <label> --note "library boundary: ..."`. A finding in its worker's own language gets the old text. `ledger_m6::tests::code_in_another_runtimes_library_is_refused_with_the_library_boundary_note`, `a_jvm_finding_at_a_rust_site_needs_a_note_for_l_handle` |
| A timer finding's holder from the resources capture | corrected | `node-socket-heartbeat`: the census holds the `setInterval` finding as `blocked-stack`; only heap-graph growth could change a holder | `ledger annotate F --holder registration-table --evidence E` accepts growth evidence written by `node-resources` when a passing `Timeout`, `TIMERWRAP` or listener group (`group:handle/<type>@<site>`) is tied to F by the finding's OWN creation site (`tie.rule` site and a `tie.key` equal to F's site; a tie through a writer's site is refused). It backs no other holder, no other group type, no other tie. The column carries no `holder_kind` field: the ledger infers the kind from the group and its tie, because `summarize/growth.rs` (not part of this package) has no field for it; a `holder_kind` on `Track` would make the evidence say it. `summarize non-heap` prints the annotate command in `needed` and names `observed_holder: {F: registration-table}` for such groups only, so the bench policy applies it (repair RB). The refusal text names the node-resources route for a finding in a JavaScript or TypeScript file only. Tests: `holder_correction::a_timer_or_listener_group_tied_by_its_site_backs_a_registration_table`, `the_timer_table_reader_backs_nothing_but_a_registration_table_tied_by_site`, `summarize_node::the_timer_group_corrects_the_holder_of_the_node_socket_heartbeat_finding`, `a_timer_group_made_at_another_line_corrects_no_holder`, `a_timer_group_tied_through_a_writers_site_corrects_no_holder` (and the writer-site case in `the_timer_table_reader_backs_nothing_but_a_registration_table_tied_by_site`). `node-socket-heartbeat` now passes the mechanical acceptance (`dla-bench dynamic --only node-socket-heartbeat`, archetypes on and off); its expected-failure entry is removed |
| `jvm.class:*` columns kept `jni-order-codec` INCOMPLETE | corrected | The per-type counts of a live JVM series (`byte[]`, `String`, the entry type) grow with the leak and tie to no finding (generic types never tie; a live series has no type tie); the printed fix `dla trend S1` rewrote the same columns | The final gate accounts for a `jvm.class:*` count that ties to no finding through the residual rows of its series, as it does for a whole-process column: `dla gap S1 --residual`, rows under their threshold or over it with a resolved open item. The classify stage no longer asks for a tie. A count tied only to undispositioned findings still needs their disposition. The tie rule is unchanged. `gate_h1::a_type_count_column_with_no_tie_is_accounted_for_by_the_residual`. `dla trend`'s `next` prints the same route (`trend::an_untied_type_count_column_is_hinted_to_the_residual_accounting_as_the_gate_prints`) and stages.md names the type-count columns. `py.type:*` columns are not included (no test, no Python reference line): they keep the old disposition path Limit: the accounting says the heap total is explained, not that this type belongs to a finding; the residual threshold is the provisional one |
| Residual share above 1 | corrected | `dla-bench dynamic` printed `residual_share` of 1.0009 to 1.3317 (`jni-order-codec`: heap residual 1448 of 1448, non-heap residual 0 of total -5.873). A per-row residual never exceeds `max(total, 0)`, so the share passes 1 only when a negative total lowers the denominator | The driver and both graders (`unexplained_growth_share`) count `max(total, 0)`. `dynamic_cmd::tests::residual_share_ignores_a_negative_total`, `residual_share_stays_within_zero_and_one`; grader tests `a_shrinking_region_does_not_lower_the_unexplained_total`, `test_a_shrinking_region_does_not_lower_the_total`. The survey's guess (mixed evidence records) was wrong. Historical regrades change only where a total was negative |
| Plan wording "zero checklist answers without evidence IDs" | decided | The ledger requires evidence for a checklist answer unless its status is `unresolved` (`checklist.rs`); an unresolved answer carries a reason and a remedy | `uncited_checklist_answers` counts answered and partial answers only and leaves `unresolved` out of both counts. Counting them would fail gate 7 on honest answers. The literal reading ("all answers") is a one-line change in `metrics_ext.rs` and `grade.py`. Documented in `bench/GRADING.md` |
| Plan wording "baselines never move down" | decided | The forced-fit rate is a ceiling: a baseline that "moves down" in value is a tighter one | Read as "never loosen". Higher is kept for second-leak recall and non-leak precision, lower for forced-fit rate. `--write-baseline F` judges the metrics against F (and `--baseline G`, the stricter value of each) before it writes, and replaces a stored value only with a stricter one. `m7_gates::a_baseline_never_gets_looser`, `write_baseline_judges_against_the_stored_file_before_writing`, `write_baseline_with_two_baselines_keeps_the_stricter_value_of_each` |
| Plan wording "mean of runs, 5 on close calls" | decided | Runs 4 and 5 hold only the close-call fixtures; the pooled mean would weight them like full runs | Means, standard deviations and the regression tolerance use per-run entries 1 to 3. Counts (zero-claim gates, decoys, pooled ratios) use every run |
| Gate arms and regression tolerance | decided | Only the `gates` ablation has an archetypes-off arm; the shipped arm is `full` | Gates 1 and 3 to 8 judge `full` with archetypes on; gate 2 compares `gates` on with `gates` off; gate 9 reports every arm and judges `full`; gate 10 judges every arm present in both files. The tolerance is `max(2 x the larger stdev, 1 / den_run of the previous release)`, never the smaller denominator of the two. A metric graded without `tokens_total` fails gate 9 with "the metrics do not have it" instead of passing on 0 tokens |
| Residual threshold noise and span | open | The residual evidence records `threshold` but not the `3 x noise / span` term behind it | `calib-extract` recovers `span` and `noise` from the series: for a heap or non-heap row from the trend of the primary column, for an off-heap row from the `gap.unexplained` column of the newest gap series derived from the row's series (the series the row was fitted on), else from `node.external`. On the `dyn-peak` logs the formula reproduces all 29 recorded thresholds. Recording the two numbers in the evidence would remove the recovery; not done here |
