| Site columns tied to findings that only share the file, or to a writer elsewhere in the body | corrected | `trend/candidates.rs` fell back to every finding of the same file (HEAD); round 1 replaced that with census writers of the finding's owner at the site or anywhere in its enclosing body, which still tied the wrong holder (`rust-delayed-queue` `orders.rs:28` to `Orders.open`, writer :30), gave `native-split-freelist` `pool.c:67` five findings (`block.next`, `chunk.next`, `chunks`, `bump`, `bump_end`) and `native-parcel-router` `intern.c:52` one more | A site column is a candidate for the named finding, the findings at its exact site, the findings whose own site is in its enclosing body (as at HEAD), and the findings with a census writer line EQUAL to the column's site (same file, same line). A writer elsewhere in the body ties nothing; a finding that only shares the file is never a candidate. `enclosing` starts at the line's first non-blank column. Tests `a_writer_in_the_body_of_a_column_ties_nothing_but_a_writer_at_a_column_site_does` (delayed-queue shape), `a_pool_column_ties_the_finding_at_its_site_and_no_field_written_in_the_same_body` (split-freelist), `an_interning_column_ties_the_finding_at_its_site_and_not_a_list_link_written_nearby` (parcel-router), `a_column_ties_to_the_census_writer_and_not_to_a_sibling_field_of_the_file` (slot-arena), `exact_site_then_same_function_or_class_then_the_named_finding`; four of them fail with the round-1 clause put back (seen). Static replays (`dla census` + `dla trend` on the saved series): `rust-delayed-queue` `orders.rs:28` and `scheduler.rs:52` tie nothing (HEAD: F1 and F2), `scheduler.rs:53` ties F2; `rust-slot-arena` `arena.rs:46` ties F2 only (HEAD: F1, F2, F3); `rust-ledger-sync` `journal.rs:26` ties F5 (`Batch.postings`, writer :26) only (HEAD: F2 the rc-cycle detector at :8, F3 and F6 `Posting.batch`, and F5), `journal.rs:20/25` and `outbox.rs:23` tie nothing (HEAD: four findings each), and `outbox.rs:44` is unchanged (F1 detector, F4 detector, F9 `Outbox.queue`, all at the exact site :44); replayed with the run's own ledger, so the ids are the run's (a replay without the sweep's detector findings numbers them differently); `native-split-freelist` `pool.c:67` ties F11 only (round 1: six); `native-parcel-router` `intern.c:52` ties F3 only (round 1: F1 and F3). `dla-bench dynamic`, archetypes on, the five fixtures: `native-parcel-router`, `native-split-freelist`, `rust-delayed-queue`, `rust-ledger-sync` pass. `rust-slot-arena` confirms `Arena.pages` (F2, census site `arena.rs:24`) and fails only because the fixture's `alt_sites` does not list :24 (a fixture item of the F stage, not a tie fault). Limit: an allocation line that is not a writer line (`orders.rs:28`) ties nothing, so such a column needs the writer's column or a probe |
| Scratch copy stamped files with fresh times and carried object files | corrected | `copy_project_scoped` and `copy_tree` used `fs::copy`, so every file got a copy-time mtime in listing order and `make` rebuilt only some objects; objects and executables built in another tree kept that tree's debug paths. The native `dla probe --build` ran `make` in the project tree, so an executable linked straight from sources (`app: main.c lease.h`, `cc -g -o app main.c`) stayed up to date in every later copy | Copies keep modification times and permissions; the content is written through a handle opened for writing, the time is set, then the permissions are copied (a 0444 source keeps its time). Object files (`.o`, `.obj`, `.lo`) are not copied when git does not track them (a tracked one is a shipped input, such as a prebuilt `blob.o` the Makefile links, and is kept); outside git there is no telling, so none is copied. Archives and shared libraries (`.a`, `.so`, `.dylib`, versioned `.so.1`, `.so.1.2.3`) are left out when git does not track them (except under the root-level build-tool directories `gradle`, `buildSrc`, `.mvn` and `vendor`, where they are kept even untracked: wrappers and vendored binaries), in the project copy and in the harness clone alike (`copy_tree` now takes the project to ask git); outside git every library counts as shipped. A gap line counts the skipped files. The native probe build now runs in a copy under the run's tmp directory (`probe/src/build.rs` `copy_for_build`), as Go, Rust and Python build in scratch, so the project tree is never written. In a git work tree the copy takes the files the harness copy lists (`git ls-files -z --cached --others --exclude-standard`, one shared function `dla_probe::project_copy::git_files`, also used by `scratch.rs`), with no directory skips, and leaves out what the harness copy leaves out through the same shared functions (`project_copy::is_dropped_build_output`: untracked object files and untracked libraries; `project_copy::link_for_copy`: a symlink that dangles or points outside the project). In a git work tree a tracked `build/common.mk` is copied (a Makefile `include build/common.mk` builds) and an ignored developer-built `native/build/` is not (a CMake project in a subdirectory builds). Outside git it walks the tree, leaves out `.git`, `.dla`, `node_modules`, `__pycache__` and, at the project root and at the detected build root, `target`, `venv`, `.venv`, `build`, `out` and `dist`, and every object file (no git to tell a shipped one), and takes everything else. A listing of zero files (an enclosing repository ignores the project) counts as no git work tree, in both copies, and the tree is walked this way. Absolute symlinks into the project are re-pointed into the copy in both paths (`project_copy::link_for_copy`, `repointed_link` for `copy_tree`); modification times and permissions are kept. Limit: outside git a project that ships a prebuilt object and no rule to rebuild it no longer builds in either copy (HEAD copied every object). The copy is removed when the build ends (nothing in the probe reads it). The probe's executable is therefore no longer in the work copy at `harness gen`, so `plan_native` also sets `DLA_SHIM_ONLY=<name>` for `make && ./prog` when `./prog` does not exist yet (an extension-less relative path after a build step; a bare name, an absolute path, a path with `..`, a script, or a command with no build step keeps the shim in every process, as before). `dla harness run` checks this after the build step and before the run command: when the run env has `DLA_SHIM_ONLY` and the command's program (cwd-relative) exists but is not a native binary (`is_native_binary`: a libtool `#!/bin/sh` stub that execs `.libs/prog`), it drops `DLA_SHIM_ONLY` for that run (only when its value is the basename of that program, as `gen` set it, so the JNI harnesses' `DLA_SHIM_ONLY=java` is kept; for a `sh -c "<piece>"` run spec the program is the first word of the body after any `NAME=value` words, found with `commands::split_env`, the function `gen` uses, so `LD_LIBRARY_PATH=/x ./prog` is read as `./prog`) and adds one limitation (`... is not a native executable ... not narrowed to that name`); no `harness.json` change. When the program is a symbolic link to a native binary of another name (`ln -s .libs/prog-real prog`), the run narrows to the resolved name instead (the shim compares the name of `/proc/self/exe`, which resolves links) and says so in one limitation. The shim's macOS path never set the executable name (`exe_path` was read from `/proc/self/exe` only), so any `DLA_SHIM_ONLY` there matched no process; it now takes `realpath` of `_NSGetExecutablePath`, and CI builds the shim and runs a program through a link on Linux and macOS (`tests/shim/alloc-shim.sh`; run here on Linux only). A plain binary keeps the narrowing (judged by reading: the plain half of the wrapper test checks only that no note is printed and the column is measured). | JVM class loader holder (`jvm-rule-compiler`) | corrected | The probe of a static whose value is a `ClassLoader` counted nothing for a loader (the walk skipped any `ClassLoader` object, so `reachable` and `bytes` were 0 and nothing of its fields was walked): flat however many classes it defined, so `alloc-test` read `favors non-leak` and the policy refuted the finding; `jvm-classloader-stats` (`group:classloader/quotes.RuleLoader`) passed its gate but tied to nothing. Round 1 added only the `.classes` column and still did not walk the loader's own project fields | The agent probe of a `ClassLoader` value counts the loaded classes whose defining loader it is (`Instrumentation.getAllLoadedClasses`, the `probe:<owner>.classes` column) and also walks the loader's own fields declared below `java.lang.ClassLoader` (a project subclass's fields): `probe:<owner>` is the classes plus those objects (filed non-heap, with the `.classes` sibling; `build.rs` sets the region from the sibling); `column_meta`, which sees a name alone, still answers heap for it (documented there; only the series builder knows the sibling, and no gate reads a `probe:` column by name), `probe:<owner>.bytes` the shallow bytes of those objects. With no Instrumentation the probe abstains. A `classloader` group of `jvm-classloader-stats` ties by type identity (`summarize/loader_tie.rs`, rule `type`) to the finding whose holder is declared in the project with exactly that loader class (package-aware; a prefix, another package's simple name, a JDK loader, a hidden-classes row and a generic type never match). The project's classes are scanned once per `summarize` call. The next-step hints say how a `classloader` group ties. Docs: `jvm.md` (probe description), `evidence-and-report.md` (`.classes`), the doc comments of `column_owner` and `probe_column_matches_owner`. Tests `harness_run::jvm_agent_probe_counts_the_classes_a_static_class_loader_defined` (fails with the branch off), `harness_run::jvm_agent_probe_walks_the_own_fields_of_a_static_class_loader` (`class Defs extends ClassLoader { final List<byte[]> cache; }`: the bytes column grows, `.classes` stays flat; fails without the field walk, seen), `build::a_class_loader_probe_is_a_non_heap_primary_column_with_classes_and_bytes_of_its_fields`, `summarize_jvm::a_loader_group_ties_to_the_finding_whose_holder_is_declared_with_that_loader_class` and `two_findings_holding_the_same_loader_class_tie_none` (fail with the tie off), `a_loader_group_ties_by_exact_class_identity_only`, `loader_tie::only_a_plain_class_name_is_a_loader_class`. Known limit, kept: two findings whose holders are declared with the same loader class tie none (the rule is `type`; ambiguous matches tie none, as `tie-rule` says); the probe route ties both. The heap-graph walk still skips `ClassLoader` internals (`ClassLoader.classes`); the probe route covers the fixture |

| Open repair items at the end of M7 phase 1 | corrected, except (8) | Independent checks of the phase-1 repair round found these. (1) `dla-bench gates --write-baseline` writes even when a gate fails, so a second identical run passes against what the first wrote: write only when every gate passes, or only the M7 baselines. (2) `--write-baseline F --baseline G --baselines-only` replaces F's previous-release block with G's, against the flag's help. (3) `crates/dla-workload/src/harness/run.rs` `finish` and `Drop` remove the live entry before marking the process stopped: a stop signal between the two leaves `stopped_at` unset; a panic in `register_process` while the live lock is held deadlocks in the child guard's drop. (4) `summarize non-heap` gives `observed_holder` only for a group whose `finding` is the finding, not for one that lists it among `candidates`. (5) No test shows that an untied `py.type:*` column is left to the residual rows. (6) `calib-extract` picks the newest gap series when the evidence names none; the evidence's `raw` file names the fitted one. (7) Go has no L-REGISTER row while the shipped known-leaks row for the Go ticker names L-REGISTER, so the runtime-row check would refuse it: accept an archetype a shipped known-leaks row names for that runtime. (8) `dla-bench dynamic` ignores a fixture's `[toolchain]`; Java versions match by prefix; `growth::Track` carries no `holder_kind`. (9) `dla-bench dynamic` printed only `label-evidence-missing` for a label refused for missing evidence, so a CI failure (jni-order-codec once failed with `shim-alloc-stacks: gate fail, tied none`) did not explain itself. | (1) `gates::run`: with `--write-baseline`, nothing is written when a gate fails (exit 1, the output names the failed gates); `--baselines-only` (the M7 baseline run) writes the three baselines even then. Test `write_baseline_refuses_when_a_gate_fails` (two identical runs; the file never appears); GRADING.md. (2) With `--baselines-only` the file's own previous-release block is kept, not `--baseline`'s. Test `baselines_only_writes_the_baselines_when_a_gate_fails_and_keeps_the_files_release_block`. (3) `ChildGuard::finish_with` marks the record stopped before it removes the live entry; `Drop` marks first and removes with `live_remove_nowait` (`try_lock`), so a panic in `register_process` with the live lock held does not deadlock. Tests `finish_never_leaves_a_process_neither_live_nor_stopped` (a hook between the two steps stands in for the signal) and `a_panic_while_registering_does_not_deadlock_the_guard_drop` (a test hook panics before `register_process`). (4) `non_heap::run_diff` computes `own_tables` over every candidate of a timer or listener group whose own site is the group's tie key, and only for the `node-resources` tool (`ledger::TIMER_EVIDENCE_TOOL`, now public). Test `a_timer_group_listing_the_finding_among_its_candidates_corrects_the_holder`. (5) The code leaves an untied `py.type:*` column to the ordinary path (`is_type_count` is `jvm.class:*` only, as references/python.md says). Test `an_untied_py_type_column_is_not_left_to_the_residual_rows`. (6) `calib::derived_gap_series` reads the `derived` id of the evidence's `raw` file when the result names none (a raw file that names none means no gap series was used). Test `an_off_heap_row_uses_the_gap_series_its_raw_file_names_when_the_evidence_names_none`. (7) `ledger_m6::rows_refusal` also accepts an archetype a shipped `known-leaks.toml` row names for the finding's runtime (`known_leak_archetypes`); the refusal text names that route. Tests `an_archetype_a_shipped_known_leaks_row_names_for_the_runtime_is_accepted_without_a_note` and `an_archetype_no_row_or_known_leak_names_for_the_runtime_is_still_refused`. Still open: Java versions match by prefix; `growth::Track` carries no `holder_kind`. The holder comes from, in order: the finding's own holder; the holder of a `data/known-leaks.toml` row that the run's probe evidence lists `true` for the runtime and whose `when` source regex matches the site line (rows that disagree give none; the policy parses the file with the `toml` crate and reads the rows itself, `dla-bench` has no `dla-core` dependency); a holder the alloc-test evidence names. Tests: `archetypes_data` (12), `skill_text`, `archetype_lists` pass; `validate::a_version_pair_may_not_swap_one_tool_for_another` is coverage of the version-pair check, not a fix |
| FIXTURES.md notes from the builders | corrected; the README policy list and the `dynamic_cmd.rs` header comments were brought in line with the policy that remains after the reverted `alloc-stack-diff` step and the tied-finding driver (the rows "Passed `alloc-stack-diff` that ties no finding" and "`drive_tied` skipped a tied finding without a discriminator") | Builder reports of phase 2 | New section "Writing a fixture the checkers can measure": command mode reads retained state at exit (a static, package or module root), Rust `process::exit` or a static, bytes per step for an alloc-shim site column, no doubling capacity, a bounded look-alike for a flat decoy |
| Node gc purge compared a pre-collection reading with a post-collection one (`node-signal-compose-24`) | corrected | `purgeRecord` in `harness/node/dla-preload.js` took `before` from the last point's pre-GC reading and `after` after the point's forced collections, so every post-GC Node column with per-step garbage read "released" and `alloc-test` favored non-leak (N-HEADROOM) for a real leak (`alloc-test` favored non-leak; `--purge none` favored leak) | `before` is now the last point's own post-GC `values` (`node.heap_used`, `node.heap_total`, `node.external`, `node.array_buffers`, `os.rss`: the readings the series holds), then one more forced collection (when `gc` is available) and `after` is read with the same keys; `*.pre_gc` columns carry no pair (the judge reads a column's own name, a missing key gives purge `not-run` and claims nothing). Tests: `harness_run::node_gc_purge_of_retained_growth_with_garbage_is_retained` (real `node`; a small retained growth, one kept object every tenth step, plus a 2000-element array of garbage per step; seen failing with the old `dla-preload.js` on both checks: `before` is not the last point's reading, and with that equality removed the drop is over 0.2 of the growth, the "looks released" check; that check is an inline copy of the judge's 0.2 rule, not the judge). `alloc_cmd::tests::a_node_gc_purge_record_with_the_series_own_reading_as_before_is_retained` and `a_purge_record_without_the_columns_key_abstains` use hand-written records with rounded numbers and pin the judge's behaviour; `judge.rs` is unchanged, so no judge test can fail without the preload change, and the preload change is tested by the real-`node` test above (the brief's "judge unit test on a recorded purge record" is therefore a pin, not a regression test of D). Known consequence: the series is already post-GC, so one more collection frees almost nothing and every Node purge column with growth now reads `retained`; for a plateau column the judge's rule (plateau + retained is inconclusive) then applies unless the `.pre_gc` twin shows headroom, and `node.heap_total`, `node.external`, `node.array_buffers` are new purge keys (they read not-run before). The Node purge therefore no longer supports non-leak on a plateau column by itself and adds a leak vote where the plateau test is inconclusive. `dla-bench dynamic` on the 12 Node fixtures (archetypes on): 11 pass, `node-signal-compose-24` fails (the leak is in a Node-internal registry and ties to no column; the acceptance stage lists it as an expected failure); its `alloc-test` now reads `node.heap_used` plateau growth-continues, purge retained, favors leak (was favors non-leak). Archetypes off: the same, 11 pass and `node-signal-compose-24` fails as before. The Python, JVM and Go gc purges still take `before` before their forced collections (not changed here). |
| `growth-not-tied` fix offered a resolve the ledger refuses | corrected | `require_tied_growth` (`machine.rs`) ended its fix with `dla ledger resolve F --as suspected_static_only --reason not_exercised --remedy ...` without `--evidence`; for a finding that cites no static-class evidence the ledger refuses that with `static-evidence-required` | The fix offers `--as suspected_static_only ... --evidence <id of the finding's static-class record>` only when the finding cites one, else `dla ledger resolve F --as unresolved --reason tool_missing --remedy "<text>"`. Test `m3_rules::the_growth_not_tied_fix_offers_a_resolve_the_ledger_accepts` takes the `--as`, `--reason` and `--evidence` flags from the printed line and applies them as the ledger move for both cases (`--reason not_exercised` with the evidence; `tool_missing` without), and both are accepted; suspected_static_only without evidence is shown refused; seen failing with the old fix text |
| `gate check --final` listed `gap --residual` again after the series had a gap row | corrected | For one series the final gate listed both `dla ledger resolve O<n>` (item `residual`) and `dla gap S<k> --residual` (item `trend_column`); running gap again after the resolve opened a new open item each time (a model looped to O70) | `gap --residual` is listed only while the series has no gap row; with a row, the `trend_column` and `residual` items list only `dla ledger resolve O<n> --by <F>` or `--as unresolved ...` (a row over threshold without an item still lists gap, which raises it). The items name the rows of the column's own region (all rows for a column of no region; all of the series' rows when none is of that region), so a heap row without an item is not sent to resolve another region's item. `dla trend S` follows the rule over all of S's rows, not by region: it hints `dla gate check --final` instead of `dla gap S --residual` for a whole-process or type-count column once S has a gap row and either an unresolved item or no row over its threshold without an item (rows under threshold or resolved also get the gate hint), and keeps `dla gap` while a row over its threshold has no item and no item is open. So for the two-region case below trend hints the gate while the gate lists gap; that needs a row over its threshold with no item, which `dla gap` never writes. `dla gap S` (the split) likewise names `dla gate check --final` instead of `dla gap S --residual` once S has any gap row. Tests: `gate_h1::once_a_series_has_a_gap_row_the_final_gate_lists_the_resolve_and_not_gap_again` (no row: gap; row without item: gap; row with open item: the resolve only, in both items; after `ledger resolve O1` both items are gone; seen failing with the old `residual_fix` forced to the gap text), `gate_h1::a_type_count_column_with_no_tie_is_accounted_for_by_the_residual` (two regions: a heap row without item and a non-heap row with O1 lists gap, not the resolve of O1; seen failing with the all-rows version of `residual_fix`), `trend::a_series_with_a_gap_row_is_not_hinted_to_run_gap_again` (gap hint for a row without item, gate hint for a row with an open item; seen failing with the old any-row rule); `gap::the_split_routes_unexplained_growth_to_native` (a second split of S1 after `gap S1 --residual` names the final gate, not the residual; seen failing with the gap-row check off); the `dla-report` golden `tests/golden/incomplete.md` now lists `dla ledger resolve O1 ...` for the off-heap row that has O1 (test `render::golden_incomplete_with_open_items_and_coverage` failed on the old golden) |
| `type-histogram-diff` `needed` sent non-JVM models to accumulation classes | corrected | The `needed` text of `histogram.rs` told every model to run `heap-graph` for the holder's accumulation class; only the JVM ties a type (`TypeIndex` is JVM-only, tie.rs; generic types never tie) | When the origin's runtime is not the JVM (Go function rows, Node types, and the harness dumps `harness-types` reads (the Python and JVM agents write `types` into their dumps; the Node agent keeps its types in `resources.json` and the Go templates write none): `harness-types` takes the runtime from the first dump with types that names a parsable one), the text says types never tie by name there and names what ties: a holder probe on the finding's owner, or a column at its site; only for Node does it add owner-named columns (timers, listeners, resource group) and the `holder:` column of heap-graph, which has no Python or Go backend. Tests: `summarize_go::a_heap_stack_ties_by_its_allocation_site_and_a_function_row_never_ties` (Go rows) and `summarize_go::the_harness_types_needed_text_follows_the_runtime_the_dumps_name` (Python and JVM dumps as the agents write them, plus a hand-written Node-runtime dump that shows the text follows the `runtime` field; seen failing with the runtime left unset; the `holder:` column is named for Node only), `summarize_python::growing_types_pass_and_tie_to_nothing_without_a_recorded_accumulation_class` (Python text names the holder probe and not heap-graph; it failed on the earlier text that named heap-graph for every non-JVM runtime) and `summarize_node::the_class_histogram_diffs_snapshots_and_ties_to_nothing` (Node heap-snapshot types) assert the text; `skill_text` and the other summarize tests pass. A dump with no `runtime` keeps the JVM text |
| The gap residual of one series does not subtract confirmed growth measured in another series | decided (no code change) | `gap/residual.rs` subtracts a finding's confirmed growth only from growth evidence of the series being analysed; evidence from another series is listed as a limitation ("growth evidence is from S?, not S?; not subtracted") | A finding confirmed on series S1 does not reduce the residual rows of S2. Run `dla gap` on the series that carries the growth the finding was confirmed on, or resolve the open item by the finding (`dla ledger resolve O<n> --by F`) |
| A JUnit-repeat driver for a JVM service whose tests take no iteration count | corrected | A real run on a Kotlin Spring Boot service had 48 JUnit tests and no workload that took an iteration count, so nothing was exercised. `dla harness gen --lang jvm --junit [--workload jvm:test:<file>] [--tests pkg.Class[#method],..]` (`--driver` alone stays the plain driver with hooks, see the row "Fixes after the second independent check of the JUnit driver") writes a driver harness with no hooks: `driver/DlaJUnitLoop.java` runs the selection through one JUnit Platform Launcher once per unit and calls `DlaDump.checkpoint` after each round (warm-up 1, rounds 8, step 2, timeout 1800 s; the existing flags override). The build step is a script (`dla-junit-build.sh`, run by `dla harness run`, so gen runs no project code): Gradle through an init script that prints `sourceSets.test.runtimeClasspath`, Maven through `dependency:build-classpath` plus `target/test-classes:target/classes`, offline first with an online fallback, then `javac -proc:none -nowarn` and a Java argument file (`java @run.args DlaJUnitLoop`). Checked here with Gradle 8.14.3, Maven and JDK 21: a Java and a Kotlin Gradle project and a Maven project, each a static list that grows by 1 KiB per `touch()` in a JUnit 5 test; the probe on the list rose by exactly 16 over the 8 measured rounds of 2 units (Kotlin: the heap column). Tests: `harness_junit::{gen_writes_a_driver_that_runs_the_test_class_with_no_hooks, flags_override_the_defaults_and_tests_select_classes_and_methods, a_selector_that_is_not_a_class_name_is_refused, a_kotlin_test_class_is_named_after_its_class_not_the_kt_facade, junit4_and_testng_tests_are_refused_naming_the_vintage_engine, driver_is_always_the_plain_driver_with_hooks_never_a_junit_repeat, a_maven_project_gets_its_wrapper_or_mvn_and_the_maven_branch, the_build_script_has_both_tool_branches, gradle_project_series_grows_with_the_repeated_tests, kotlin_gradle_project_series_grows_with_the_repeated_tests, maven_project_series_grows_with_the_repeated_tests, a_failing_test_is_counted_and_named_in_the_log_but_the_series_is_kept, a_class_that_matches_no_test_ends_the_run_before_any_checkpoint, when_every_unit_fails_the_last_stderr_lines_name_the_first_failure}`, `commands::tests::junit_plan_builds_with_the_script_and_runs_java_with_an_argument_file`, `templates::tests::{every_template_fills_cleanly, drivers_carry_their_hooks}`. The end-to-end tests print `skipped: ...` and pass when gradle, mvn, java or the network is missing | Deviations from the survey's design, each on purpose. (1) The launcher is added through a detached configuration of the engine's version, not a `testRuntimeOnly` dependency: it needs no BOM and changes no project configuration. (2) The manifest's key mode is `fresh` only (a JUnit repeat has no keys; `dla alloc-test` assumes `fresh` for every driver; `dla harness run --mode both` runs only the declared mode, see the row "Fixes after the second independent check of the JUnit driver"). (3) The build is a script file in the driver directory, not an inline `sh -c`, so it can be read and tested. (4) Not verified here: a `@SpringBootTest` application with real services (no Spring or broker in this environment; the behaviour rests on Spring's documented context cache and the survey's prototype of repeated `launcher.execute`), Maven multi-module builds (the module is built from its own directory; sibling modules must be installed), the Windows spelling of the build script (it is `sh`, like the other driver builds). The build step has the 600 s limit of every harness build: a cold Gradle resolution of a large service may need a second `dla harness run` |
| `fqcn()` appended `Kt` to every Kotlin class | corrected | `jvm::fqcn` named a Kotlin file `FooTest.kt` with `class FooTest` `FooTestKt`, so the Gradle workload command `--tests pkg.FooTestKt` matched no test. Now a test selector (`fqcn`) is the file's stem when the file declares `class\|object\|interface <stem>`, else the first non-abstract top-level class or object that has a test annotation, else the first non-abstract one (`ApiTests.kt` with `class GetTest` is `GetTest`; annotations on the class line such as `@Suppress("x") class GetTest` are accepted; comments are ignored), else `<Stem>Kt` (top-level functions); the class of a `java <fqcn>` main (`main_fqcn`) is `<Stem>Kt` whenever the Kotlin file has a top-level `fun main`, even beside `class <Stem>`. Tests: `jvm::tests::kotlin_classes_keep_their_name_and_only_function_files_get_kt` (also the class-plus-main, renamed-class and helper-class cases), `harness_junit::a_kotlin_class_with_a_top_level_main_runs_the_kt_facade` (workload command ends in `app.ServerKt`) and `harness_junit::a_kotlin_test_class_is_named_after_its_class_not_the_kt_facade` (the last seen failing with the old rule) | A test file with several test classes gets the first concrete one that has a test annotation; pass `--tests` for another. Tests: also `jvm::tests::the_test_class_is_found_in_less_regular_kotlin_layouts` (same-line annotation, comments that mention `@Test` or a class, abstract base class; judged by reading against the old regex, not run reverted). `@file:JvmName` is not read; the Maven workload command still uses the file stem |
| The JUnit gap text of the JVM driver | corrected | The manifest recorded "junit-platform-console-standalone not used ... JUnit variant is generated only when the jar is on the classpath", and `harness/jvm/README.md` said no JUnit 5 variant existed, though none was ever generated. The gap is gone, and the README describes the real variant (the M3 row on the JUnit 5 variant above is superseded for JVM) | The Launcher-based driver needs no console-standalone jar |
| Fixes after the independent check of the JUnit driver | corrected | Gradle configuration cache (`org.gradle.configuration-cache=true`) made the init script's task unserializable, so the build failed: the build script now passes `-Dorg.gradle.configuration-cache=false` (test `a_gradle_project_with_the_configuration_cache_on_still_builds`, seen failing without the flag). Units that fail: the driver exits 2 (`DLA-JUNIT every unit failed: <first failure>`, run refused with `harness-failed`, no series) only when every unit failed, and 3 when no test passed or failed; an earlier version also refused runs where more than half failed, which closed leaks whose own test asserts a fresh state (`Cache.touch(); assertEquals(1, size)` fails on 17 of 18 units after the leaky call). Now such a run keeps its series and the summary line counts `failed_units` (`a_test_that_breaks_on_leaked_state_still_gives_a_series`, seen failing with the half rule: `harness-failed`; `a_few_failing_units_keep_the_series_and_are_counted`: 2 of 18 units fail, series kept and `failed_units=2`, passes with and without the rule); jvm.md says an assertion on accumulated state is the leak and a connection error is `env_unavailable`. A `@ParameterizedTest` can be selected as `pkg.Class#method(java.lang.String,int)`, `--tests` splits on commas outside parentheses, and a launcher failure prints its cause chain (`a_parameterized_test_is_selected_by_its_signature`, `a_selector_with_parameter_types_passes_gen_and_a_broken_one_does_not` seen failing; `harness_cmd::tests::tests_flag_splits_on_commas_outside_parameter_lists`). `--junit` for another language is refused as such before the workload lookup (`junit_for_another_language_is_refused_as_such`, seen failing on a Go project). `kotlin.test` tests are accepted when a junit-platform-engine is on the class path (`kotlin_test_and_renamed_kotlin_classes_are_accepted`, seen failing). The Maven branch of the build script uses `target/...` relative to the project directory and forward slashes, and cuts the repository prefix after normalising backslashes (`the_maven_branch_keeps_paths_relative_and_reads_backslash_class_paths` with a stand-in `mvn`, seen failing with absolute paths). The end-to-end tests skip only when the online attempt of the build failed to download (the script logs `DLA-BUILD the offline attempt failed` between attempts), and the every-unit-fails test now asserts the `harness-failed` refusal. Help and fix texts: `--driver`, `--step`, tool-missing wording; the docs say the run uses `java` from `PATH`, not the Gradle test task's JVM | The Windows Maven path is judged by reading and the stand-in test (no Windows host); A Kotlin test file takes the first class with a test annotation (`kotlin_classes_keep_their_name_and_only_function_files_get_kt`, seen failing with the old first-class rule), `--junit` with another language is refused before the workload lookup, also with no discovery result, and the `--tests` fix names `--lang jvm` (`junit_for_another_language_is_refused_as_such`) |
| A generated harness was never run before findings closed `no_harness` | corrected | On a Kotlin service `dla harness gen` wrote H1, nothing ran it, and 55 of 60 budget minutes were left when every finding closed `suspected_static_only(no_harness)`; the run left no trace of whether a run was tried. `dla harness run H<n>` writes `harness/H<n>/attempt.json` (`harness`, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `outcome` ran, partial or failed, `reason` no_harness, build_fails, time_budget, hooks_unfilled, interrupted or the refusal code, `message`) once the manifest loads (nothing is written for an unknown harness or a manifest that does not load), including SIGINT and SIGTERM (the stop handler writes `interrupted` before it exits). A failure on a driver whose DLA-HOOK bodies are still the template's (`DLA-HOOK <name> has not been filled in` in a driver file) is written as `hooks_unfilled` and does not count as a try; neither does `time_budget` (the run was refused for time) or `interrupted`. Not sealed, not in the ledger. A panic writes nothing; an `interrupted` attempt is not a try (see the rule row). Tests in `crates/dla-workload/tests/harness_run_attempt.rs`: `a_successful_run_writes_a_ran_attempt`, `a_failed_build_writes_a_failed_attempt_with_reason_build_fails`, `a_run_with_no_usable_point_writes_reason_no_harness`, `a_refusal_before_any_point_writes_the_refusal_code`, `an_expired_budget_writes_reason_time_budget`, `an_unknown_harness_writes_nothing`, `a_failure_on_a_driver_with_template_hooks_writes_reason_hooks_unfilled`; and `gate_harness::a_run_stopped_by_a_signal_writes_an_interrupted_attempt`. Seen failing without the change: `a_successful_run_writes_a_ran_attempt`, `a_failed_build_writes_a_failed_attempt_with_reason_build_fails`, `a_run_with_no_usable_point_writes_reason_no_harness`, `a_refusal_before_any_point_writes_the_refusal_code`, `an_expired_budget_writes_reason_time_budget` and `gate_harness::a_run_stopped_by_a_signal_writes_an_interrupted_attempt` (no marker written); `a_failure_on_a_driver_with_template_hooks_writes_reason_hooks_unfilled` with the `hooks_unfilled` branch of `write_attempt` removed (the reason becomes `no_harness`). `an_unknown_harness_writes_nothing` is a negative test and passes without the change. The `partial` outcome is judged by reading (no test cuts a run with the deadline). The write is in `dla harness run` only: a panic or SIGKILL writes nothing. The fix text of a JUnit repeat harness (manifest tools name `junit-platform-launcher`) that fails with every process non-zero no longer says "fix the harness or its hooks" (it has no hooks): it names the tail of the logs and, for tests that need a service that is down, recording `env_unavailable` on `intake.workload` (`harness_run_attempt::a_failing_junit_repeat_harness_does_not_send_the_caller_to_hooks`, seen failing with the junit branch disabled: the old text came back; a non-JUnit harness keeps the hooks wording) | Environment-blocked runs are recorded by the model on `intake.workload`, never as an attempt reason |
| A generated harness must be run or its blocker recorded before `no_harness`, `not_exercised` or `time_budget` | corrected | Same run: nothing in the ledger rules, the gate or the Stop hook tied a generated harness to the closure of findings. `gate::unrun_harness` picks a harness of the finding's runtime that `dla harness gen` wrote (its manifest loads and the ledger has its generation evidence; a clone made by `dla intervene` has none and is never held), that was not tried (no `attempt.json` other than `hooks_unfilled`, `interrupted` or `time_budget`, no `run-*.json`, no series from it) and whose blocker is not recorded (`intake.workload` answered `unresolved` with env_unavailable, tool_missing, build_fails or privilege_declined and a remedy after the harness was generated). It returns nothing when another harness of the runtime measured something (an attempt that ran, a `run-*.json` or a series), so an abandoned driver does not hold the closures after a `--junit` harness ran. A harness whose build failed (last attempt `build_fails`) with nothing else of the runtime run and no blocker recorded keeps the rule, the close is refused with `harness-not-run` and the step is to fix the build error and run it again; a run refused for time keeps the rule too, and for that case the step is `dla harness run <H>`; the attempt of any other outcome (ran, or failed on the workload) clears it. Seen failing with the failed-build branch made to return nothing: `an_attempt_that_ran_or_failed_the_workload_clears_the_rule_and_a_failed_build_keeps_it`. `gate::harness_not_run` adds: run not aborted or incomplete, and more than the larger of 10 minutes and 20% of the budget left, the nearer of run and worker deadline. `dla ledger resolve --as unresolved` or `--as suspected_static_only` with one of the three reasons is refused with code `harness-not-run`; `dla gate check --final` (and the worker final) lists a Missing item `harness_not_run` with id `H<n>` for each unrun harness that has a finding closed that way, which also feeds the Stop hook and `dla run status`. The fix names the DLA-HOOK bodies only when the driver still has template hooks, and the `--junit` hint only for a JVM one. Not in `machine.rs` (replay of old ledgers has no run directory). A finding's runtime is `worker_key`, else the only runtime that has a harness. In worker mode a worker is held only by harnesses whose generation evidence is in its own slice. Tests in `crates/dla-cli/tests/gate_harness.rs`: `an_unrun_harness_refuses_the_covered_reasons_and_the_final_gate_lists_it`, `the_reasons_that_name_a_blocker_are_never_refused`, `a_ledger_closed_before_the_rule_is_listed_by_the_final_gate` (also run status and the Stop hook text), `no_finding_closed_means_no_missing_item`, `an_attempt_that_ran_or_failed_the_workload_clears_the_rule_and_a_failed_build_keeps_it`, `a_run_file_or_a_harness_series_clears_the_rule`, `a_blocker_on_intake_workload_after_generation_clears_the_rule`, `the_same_answer_before_generation_does_not_clear_it`, `under_the_budget_floor_or_in_a_stopped_run_the_rule_does_not_apply`, `a_workers_nearer_deadline_lifts_the_rule_for_its_findings`, `a_finding_of_another_runtime_is_not_held_by_the_harness`, `no_manifest_means_no_rule`, `worker_mode_refuses_and_lists_the_harness_of_its_own_runtime`, `an_intervention_clone_is_not_a_generated_harness`, `a_failure_on_a_driver_with_template_hooks_does_not_clear_the_rule`, `another_harness_of_the_runtime_that_measured_releases_an_abandoned_one`, `the_fix_names_the_hooks_only_for_a_driver_with_template_hooks`, `a_refusal_says_what_the_last_attempt_was`, `the_rule_costs_one_load_of_the_series_not_one_per_closed_finding`. Seen failing with the matching hunk removed: `interrupted` counted as a try fails `a_refusal_says_what_the_last_attempt_was` and `a_run_stopped_by_a_signal_writes_an_interrupted_attempt`; with the rule made to never apply (`harness_not_run` returning `None`), thirteen tests of `gate_harness.rs` fail (run again here): `an_unrun_harness_refuses_the_covered_reasons_and_the_final_gate_lists_it`, `a_ledger_closed_before_the_rule_is_listed_by_the_final_gate`, `a_failure_on_a_driver_with_template_hooks_does_not_clear_the_rule`, `a_refusal_says_what_the_last_attempt_was`, `a_run_stopped_by_a_signal_writes_an_interrupted_attempt`, `another_harness_of_the_runtime_that_measured_releases_an_abandoned_one`, `the_fix_names_the_hooks_only_for_a_driver_with_template_hooks`, `the_same_answer_before_generation_does_not_clear_it`, `under_the_budget_floor_or_in_a_stopped_run_the_rule_does_not_apply`, `worker_mode_refuses_and_lists_the_harness_of_its_own_runtime`, `a_workers_nearer_deadline_lifts_the_rule_for_its_findings`, `an_attempt_that_ran_or_failed_the_workload_clears_the_rule_and_a_failed_build_keeps_it` and the scale test `the_rule_costs_one_load_of_the_series_not_one_per_closed_finding`; the seven that pass without the rule (`a_finding_of_another_runtime_is_not_held_by_the_harness`, `a_blocker_on_intake_workload_after_generation_clears_the_rule`, `a_run_file_or_a_harness_series_clears_the_rule`, `an_intervention_clone_is_not_a_generated_harness`, `no_finding_closed_means_no_missing_item`, `no_manifest_means_no_rule`, `the_reasons_that_name_a_blocker_are_never_refused`) assert that the rule does not apply, so they are meaningful next to the positive ones. The bench policy runs every harness it generates with `dla harness run`, so each has an `attempt.json`; `dla-bench dynamic` runs of `jvm-audit-chain` (a second harness cloned by `dla intervene`, effect invalid, closures `not_exercised` after it, which the first version of the rule refused) and `python-vendor-wheel` (harness fails, closes `no_harness`) pass. Cost: the rule is evaluated once per runtime, not once per closed finding, and the series-to-harness set (`RunView.harness_series`) is built in the same pass over the series files as `gap_series`, so the rule adds no series load. The scale test (362 findings closed `no_harness`, 30 series of about 21 KB naming another harness, an unfilled driver H1) takes the final gate from 5.8 s (old per-finding evaluation, debug build; about 37 s for `gate check --final` on 30 series files of 21 KB holding 12 columns x 60 points, which cost more to load) to 0.06 s and asserts under 1 s. `dla ledger resolve` with one of the three reasons skips the rule, and the load of every series, when the run has no `harness/H<n>` directory | After `dla ledger merge` the core final gate judges every worker's harness against the run's deadline only (a worker is given pool/n), so a worker that stopped at its own nearer deadline can leave its harness listed in the core: run it there or record the blocker (intended; no deadlock). The refusal says `never run with its DLA-HOOK bodies filled in` or `interrupted before it measured` when an attempt of that kind exists. Any unrun generated harness of a runtime that nothing else of that runtime measured holds the closures; the escape without running it is the `intake.workload` blocker answer |

(2) An explicit `--driver` is always the plain driver with hooks, also for a JUnit 5 test file (a `main` under src/test of a Gradle project worked with `--driver` before and was refused as a JUnit repeat); `--junit` (alone or with `--driver`) is the JUnit repeat; the old switch of `--driver` to the JUnit repeat is removed. (3) The `--tests` fix text of the early check names `--lang jvm` for any language. (4) A method name in `--tests` may hold spaces and most punctuation, but no comma, `(` or `#` (Kotlin backtick tests: `--tests 'pkg.GetTest#publishes to the broker'`); quotes, backslashes, `#`, `(` in the name and control characters stay refused because the selector is embedded in a Java string. (5) Kotlin class names: annotations on the class line, comments that mention `@Test` or a class, and an abstract base class holding the tests are handled (`jvm.rs`). (6) `dla harness run --mode both\|repeated` on a manifest whose `key_modes` lacks the requested mode runs only the declared modes and adds a limitation to `run-S<n>.json` (`run.rs`, one hunk in the mode selection; a plain driver declares both modes, so it is unchanged); the second identical `repeated` series is gone. | JVM coverage said nothing was exercised after hundreds of iterations | corrected | A real Kotlin Spring Boot 3.3.5 Gradle project (Kotlin plugin, `Routes.kt` holding `UserController` and the top-level `routeCount`, `UserService` with a map, `Util.kt` with a top-level `slug`, `billing/Invoices.kt` declaring `com.acme.shop.billing`, a `@SpringBootTest`) gave, with the dla built before these changes: (1) `dla harness gen --lang jvm --junit` then `dla harness run`: `jvm 16 iterations, 0 of 7 entry points exercised` although the test ran `UserService.lookup`; (2) a driver harness calling one service: `3520 iterations`, 0 exercised (the third real run's `56320 iterations, 0 of 48`); (3) a command harness running the boot jar (`java -jar build/libs/shop.jar` and a `curl` loop): `160 iterations`, only the two `UserService` entry points matched, and the list stopped at the 4000-class cap. Causes, each found in the dump or the code: the coverage section was written only by the agent's `exit` record, and the exit hook stays quiet once a checkpoint was written, so a driver or JUnit repeat never wrote one (in the JUnit run the only coverage came from the `net.bytebuddy.agent.Attacher` helper JVM that Byte Buddy starts for the test libraries, which loads the agent through `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS`: nine JDK attach classes); `underProject` accepted only `file:` code sources, so a Spring Boot jar's classes (`jar:nested:/x.jar/!BOOT-INF/classes/!/`, older `jar:file:/x.jar!/BOOT-INF/classes!/`) were never the project's and the alphabetical fill under the cap kept the project's classes only because `com.acme` sorts early; the path match could not place a Kotlin file holding a class of another name (`Routes.kt`), a package directory without the root (`billing/` for `com.acme.shop.billing`) or top-level functions (`UtilKt`). The 4000-class cap and the match were not the whole story: a driver that calls one service never loads the controller, which is an honest zero. | The agent writes the coverage section in a driver's last checkpoint too, and in its first, second, fourth, eighth... checkpoint so a repeat killed at its timeout or the run deadline still reports the classes it loaded (the harness takes the union of every section), ranks the project's classes first and never cuts them (cap 20000 for them, up to 4000 more for the rest), leaves test output directories and DLA's own drivers out of the project's classes, recognises a class as the project's by a code source under `DLA_PROJECT` (the outer jar of `jar:file:` and `jar:nested:` locations; not a nested library, Spring Boot's loader, or, once packages are known, a jar's root) or by a package of the new `DLA_PROJECT_PACKAGES` (the packages the scratch copy's `.java` and `.kt` files declare, set by `dla harness run`, documented in `harness/README.md` and `harness/jvm/README.md`), skips the Byte Buddy attach helper JVM, and lists the source file of each project class (`package/path/File.kt` from the class file's `SourceFile` attribute, read for project classes only) and the count of project classes. `cover.rs` places an entry by path as before; by the `SourceFile` list, when a discovered file of that name declares the same package (read from the file) and the entry's owner class is itself loaded from that package (a loaded sibling class of the file is not enough); and a top-level function by its facade class (`<Stem>Kt`, or the file's `@file:JvmName`) loaded from the file's own package, so `kotlin.text.StringsKt` is not the facade of a project's `Strings.kt`. The coverage section gains `project` and `sources` (`harness-dump.schema.json`), the worker row `project_classes` (`coverage-manifest.schema.json`), and the report's `Coverage:` line reads `jvm 750 iterations, 0 of 225 entry points exercised, 37 project classes loaded` when no entry point matched but project classes loaded (the harness run output carries the same as a limitation). `dla run status` prints no coverage line, so it is unchanged. The three scenarios now give 6 of 7 (JUnit, `routeCount` excluded because `RoutesKt` never loaded), 3 of 7 (driver on `UserService` and `slug`) and 6 of 7 (boot jar). The match is by loaded class, not called method (an entry point of a loaded class counts). A class only DLA's own probe loaded counted too: the probe lookup called `Class.forName` on a class no test or driver had loaded, so a Maven project whose JUnit test only asserts 2 + 2 read `2 of 5 entry points exercised` (the probes named `OrderCache`), the zero-coverage gate item did not fire and a hook driver that called no project code read the same; the agent now looks a probe's class up among the loaded classes only (a class that is not loaded holds nothing, and the probe records `class not loaded`), and uses the class loaders only when it has no instrumentation handle (then it lists no coverage either). `harness_jvm_coverage::a_probe_never_loads_a_class_into_the_coverage` (a real JVM; a project class that nothing reaches and a probe that names it: the class is not in the coverage section and the probe record says it was not loaded; seen failing with the lookup restored: `zz.shop.Never` was listed), `harness_jvm_coverage::locations_and_the_source_file_attribute_are_read_by_the_agent` (nested and jar locations, percent-encoding, nested libraries, `SourceFile` of a class named differently from its file), `a_drivers_last_checkpoint_carries_the_coverage_with_the_project_first`, `a_package_the_sources_declare_ranks_classes_whose_jar_lies_elsewhere`, `a_driver_cut_off_before_its_last_checkpoint_has_still_written_the_coverage` (a real JVM with the agent; the locations test also pins that test output directories, also with Windows separators, and DLA's own drivers `DlaLoop` and `DlaJUnitLoop` are not the project's, and that the Byte Buddy attach helper is a tool JVM); `cover::tests::a_package_line_with_a_comment_or_a_byte_order_mark_is_still_read` (a `package` line followed by `//` or `/* */`, or after a byte order mark); `harness_junit::a_kotlin_spring_boot_test_run_exercises_the_entry_points_of_the_classes_it_loads` (Gradle, Kotlin, Spring Boot 3.3.5, a `@SpringBootTest`; A fixed budget cannot fit both a 5-candidate and a 362-candidate project | `dla run init` with neither `--budget` nor `DLA_DEFAULT_BUDGET` uses `auto` (plugin `time_budget` default `auto`): it starts at 60 m and is sized by the first passing `dla gate check` at `sweep` or later (the run's own check sizes it for good, a worker's check in a dispatched run raises it, see below; `--final` does not size, it passes only when nothing is open), to `clamp(20 + 15*R + 3*C, 60, 180)` minutes from the start (R runtimes with a candidate, C candidates: top-level, non-duplicate findings of any state, sibling hypotheses not counted, one site counted once; in the core and in a dispatched run alike); the deadline never moves earlier (an extension made before sizing stays on top), every unfinished worker deadline moves by the same delta, the estimate is stored in `state.json` (`budget.auto`, `settled`, `settled_workers`, `estimate`, `extensions`, serde defaults, schema regenerated, no version bump) and the gate prints `budget (auto): 3h for 1 runtime and 362 candidates (20 + 15x1 + 3x362 = 1121 min, capped at 3h)`. Tests that ran: `budget::tests::{parses_units, auto_is_accepted_in_any_case, rejects_bad_input, a_fixed_parse_refuses_auto}`; `dla-cli/tests/budget_auto.rs` `{init_defaults_to_an_auto_budget_and_a_fixed_value_stays_fixed, init_refuses_a_bad_budget_and_names_auto, the_first_passing_gate_at_sweep_settles_an_auto_budget_once, a_small_project_keeps_the_floor_and_a_large_one_hits_the_cap, settling_never_moves_the_deadline_earlier, a_later_stage_settles_when_the_sweep_gate_did_not_run, the_final_gate_does_not_size_the_budget_from_nothing, an_extension_made_before_sizing_is_kept_and_the_gate_line_says_so, a_fixed_budget_never_settles, settling_moves_the_deadline_of_every_unfinished_worker, a_workers_passing_gate_settles_too, a_worker_with_dispositioned_candidates_sizes_the_run_from_them, the_worker_that_passes_first_sizes_the_run_from_every_workers_slice}`; `dla-core/tests/budget_auto.rs` `{the_estimate_follows_the_formula_with_a_floor_and_a_cap, duplicates_and_siblings_do_not_count_and_closed_findings_do, workers_size_the_budget_in_turn_and_it_only_rises_until_every_worker_has, a_smaller_estimate_from_a_later_worker_never_lowers_the_budget, the_settled_line_names_the_cap_and_the_floor, settle_moves_the_deadline_to_the_sized_total_once, settle_never_moves_the_deadline_earlier_and_a_fixed_budget_never_settles, settle_moves_every_unfinished_worker_deadline_by_the_same_delta, an_extension_made_before_sizing_stays_on_top_of_the_sized_total, candidates_at_one_site_count_once_before_they_are_merged, a_state_written_before_the_auto_budget_still_loads}`; `skill_text::the_plugin_time_budget_defaults_to_auto_and_still_takes_a_fixed_value`; `dla-bench dynamic --parallel 1 --only jvm-order-cache` and `--only python-report-memo` still pass (fixed budgets). In a dispatched run each worker's first passing gate at `sweep` or later sizes the budget again from every registered worker's slice (each loaded read only, no lock) plus the main ledger, with R at least the number of workers, and may only raise it (the deadlines shift by the difference); `budget.settled_workers` records who has, and the budget is final once every registered worker has (a stalled worker leaves the last raise in place; `dla run status` and the gate line show the estimate the budget was sized to, the largest). Before this a dispatched run was sized by the first worker to pass, which is the one with the fewest candidates: reproduced with a python worker holding 40 candidates and a go worker with none, where go's sweep gate passed first and printed `for 2 runtimes and 0 candidates` (`1h`, final); it now prints `2h50m for 2 runtimes and 40 candidates` and stays final once python has passed. `the_worker_that_passes_first_sizes_the_run_from_every_workers_slice` was seen failing with the gate counting only the worker's own view; the raise-only rule, `settled_workers` and the final flag are tested in `dla-core/tests/budget_auto.rs`, judged by reading and not reverted. A worker cannot ask the core to extend (the core waits on the Agent calls), so stages.md says it closes `time_budget` and the core extends after the merge. Candidates at one site count once (the census and the sweep rules each open one at the same holder before duplicates are merged). Bench children run `dla run init --budget ${user_config.time_budget}`, so the gates benchmark now gets `auto` if Claude Code substitutes the plugin default (not checked without a real run): 60 m for a small fixture, 180 m from 49 candidates of one runtime (it starts rising above 60 m at 9) where it got 60 m; `dla-bench dynamic` passes `--budget 2h` and is unchanged. | Skill text for the extendable budget | corrected | The skill let a model close findings `time_budget` without offering the user more time, and never said an unattended run must not extend | SKILL.md `## Time budget` (init takes `auto` or a fixed value, `intake.budget` cites the init evidence, one instruction between the floor and the deadline: while more than the floor (the larger of 10 minutes and 20% of the budget) is left, never ask whether to continue and never close `time_budget`; at or under it the core of an interactive run asks once "extend by <to_finish> minutes to measure the next <n> candidates?", `dla run extend <dur>` on yes, and closes only on a no or no answer; a worker and an unattended run (the runner gave the consent) never extend and close with a remedy. The Stop hook says the same under the floor and offers the extension first to an interactive core run: `gate_hooks::{an_unattended_core_after_a_refusal_with_nothing_smaller_that_fits_is_told_to_close, stop_hook_under_the_floor_offers_an_interactive_core_the_extension_first, stop_hook_after_a_refusal_with_nothing_smaller_that_fits_counts_as_the_floor, a_workers_refusal_never_puts_the_core_at_the_floor, a_worker_stop_obeys_its_own_refusal_and_not_the_cores_or_a_siblings, the_reason_after_a_refusal_with_most_of_the_budget_left_says_what_happened}` (the first seen failing with the unattended check removed from the budget line: it and two older tests failed) and the unit tests `gate_hooks::tests::a_long_item_text_is_clipped_before_the_budget_line_and_the_pointer` and `toolctx::tests::every_time_budget_refusal_of_a_tool_is_noted_for_the_reason_check`. A refusal that said even the smallest run does not fit (the `none_fits` field of a line of `time_refusals.jsonl`, true at a spent deadline and when no smaller run is suggested, false with a suggestion) puts an interactive core at the floor above it, and its reason says a command was refused with nothing smaller that fits, not that too few minutes remain. Only the core's own refusals count for the core and a worker's only for that worker, so a worker's refusal at its own deadline leaves the core's instruction to measure what is open. A refusal from before the budget was last extended does not count; the state records no time when an auto budget was raised by a later sizing gate, so such a refusal still counts. The Stop hook clips the item texts first, so the budget line and the pointer to the full list survive the length limit. Seen failing with the change reverted: the worker-refusal test and the refusal-reason test (core counting a worker's line, old wording), the clip test (clip removed), the refusal test (the core matching a worker's line, and the writer of `none_fits` forced to false). The worker side of the hook is covered by `a_worker_stop_obeys_its_own_refusal_and_not_the_cores_or_a_siblings`, seen failing with the worker's lookup replaced by the core's (the worker's hook then counted the core's line, and the test failed at its first assertion). The keep-going bullet, `references/stages.md` and `references/recovery.md` agree), `references/stages.md` (the formula), `references/recovery.md` (budget exhausted), `references/evidence-and-report.md` (the `Time:` line), README.
