| CodeQL traced build: wrong directory, lost Java, a fix that could not work | corrected (fake codeql only) | A module's traced build was started through the process's working directory, which CodeQL ignores for `--command` (it runs in the source root unless `--working-dir` is given); a mixed project with tier 1 and a failing build became `blocked` and lost the Java that the buildless run reads, so consenting gave a worse result than not consenting; every failure said to fill the cache with `dla probe --build`, which is offline too and cannot; test sources were counted as Kotlin sources, so a Java project with Kotlin only in tests swapped its buildless database for a traced `classes` build | `create_args` passes `--working-dir <copy>/<build dir>` with `--command`. When the traced create fails and the project has Java main sources, the Java is analysed with `--build-mode=none` and the limitation reads `Kotlin files not extracted: the traced build failed (exit n): <line>`; Kotlin only stays `blocked` with the failure. The cache hint (run the project's own build online once, or a `dla harness run` of a JUnit or driver harness, whose build step retries online) is added only when the log shows an unresolvable dependency. Test sources are not counted. The build copy is removed after analysis. Tests: `adapters::codeql::tests::{commands_are_built_for_java_without_a_build, the_cache_hint_is_given_only_for_a_cold_dependency_cache}` and `tests/sweep.rs::{a_kotlin_project_gets_a_traced_build_in_a_copy, a_failed_traced_build_falls_back_to_the_java_and_says_why, a_kotlin_only_project_whose_traced_build_fails_is_blocked, kotlin_only_in_tests_does_not_trigger_a_traced_build}`; the working-directory, fallback and test-source tests were seen failing with each change reverted, the cache hint test was seen failing with the old markers. The project's own wrapper (module or root) is preferred over `gradle` or `mvn` on PATH (`the_projects_wrapper_wins_over_a_tool_on_path`, seen failing with the old order); the cache hint also matches `UnknownHostException` and no longer matches `cannot access` (a compile error). A root wrapper stands in for the module's own build only when the root build is the same kind (a Gradle `classes` task never compiles a Maven module; `a_root_wrapper_of_another_build_kind_does_not_stand_in_for_the_modules_build`, seen failing with the kind check removed). The cache hint matches the offline markers of the harness end-to-end tests (`Unable to tunnel`, `Network is unreachable`, `Connection refused`, `connect timed out`, `Could not GET 'http` and `Could not HEAD 'http` (not a bare `Could not get`, which Gradle also prints for an unknown script property: `a_build_script_error_is_not_a_cold_cache`, seen failing with the bare marker), unknown host; the list is kept in the adapter and the two test files, not shared across crates; `the_cache_hint_names_every_offline_marker_of_the_harness_build`, seen failing with the new markers removed). The fallback limitation uses the same Kotlin file count helper as the plan (`1 Kotlin file`, `one_kotlin_file_reads_in_the_singular` and the pinned text in `tests/sweep.rs`) and leads with the Kotlin note; the order and the cut at a word of the coverage reason were established by reverting each on a copy and seeing `a_failed_traced_build_falls_back_to_the_java_and_says_why` fail, and the singular text by reading. `the_traced_build_prefers_the_wrapper_and_falls_back_to_the_tool` expects the `.bat`/`.cmd` wrappers on Windows (not run there) |
| Sonar row named backends with no rule for its shapes, and the probe wrote it `missing` | corrected | The server-only reason listed every backend that ran, detekt included (it has no rule for S5164 or S2095) and backends that ran on nothing; `dla probe` still wrote `jvm/sonar` as `missing` and listed it among the tools to install; detekt ran, and reported Ran, on a project whose only Kotlin was `buildSrc` | The reason names only Error Prone, SpotBugs and CodeQL, and only when they analysed something; `dla probe` writes the same `blocked` server-only row and no longer lists sonar in `tools_missing`; detekt is not applicable (`no Kotlin sources`) when the only Kotlin is build logic. Tests: `adapters::sonar::tests::the_reason_is_fixed_server_only_and_names_the_backends_that_ran`, `tests/sweep.rs::sonar_is_a_server_only_blocked_row_without_an_install_hint`, `dla-probe` `known_leaks::sonar_is_a_blocked_server_only_row_not_a_missing_tool`, `install::tests::a_failed_install_leaves_the_sonar_row_blocked_and_marks_other_backends_missing`, `adapters::jvm::tests::kotlin_only_in_build_logic_makes_detekt_not_applicable` (the sonar unit test and the detekt test were seen failing with the change reverted; the probe and install tests by reading, the install one fails only if the early return is removed) |
| JVM non-heap growth could not be decided, and was closed for time | corrected | A real Kotlin Spring Boot run saw `jvm.nonheap_used` rise about 28 B/iteration with a flat class count and one NMT diff, and closed the item `time_budget`. Causes, found in the code and checked on JDK 21: the agent read `VM.native_memory summary` at every point and kept only the total, so no category had a series and nothing could show a plateau; `dla alloc-test --finding F` on a non-heap finding with no tied column fell back to `jvm.threads` (a flat counter, so a plateau, so a non-leak verdict for the wrong reason); the skill text named `thread or goroutine stack bytes` as the JVM non-heap primary; `dla sample --counters jcmd` named `jvm.classes_loaded` the cumulative non-CDS counter (`java.cls.loadedClasses`: 7 on an idle JVM that holds about 450 classes, 30 121 after defining and dropping 30 000) while the harness column is the number loaded now. | The JVM agent adds `jvm.nmt.<category>` (committed bytes of metaspace, class, code, compiler, symbol, arena_chunk, thread, from the summary it already reads), `jvm.classes_unloaded`, `jvm.jit_seconds` (when compilation time monitoring is supported, else a limitation) and, only without NMT, `jvm.metaspace_used` and `jvm.codecache_used` (pool readings); the catalog files them as whole-process non-heap columns (they tie to nothing and the non-heap residual rows account for them: a gate test shows no `trend_column` item, with a heap row over its threshold, and a non-heap row over its threshold waits for all of them); the alloc-test pick for a JVM non-heap finding is `jvm.nonheap_used`, which must plateau together with every `jvm.nmt.*` category that passed the gate in the latest trend and with `jvm.threads` and `os.threads` (those two when they passed the gate, or when no trend of the harness exists yet: thread stacks are not in the pools, and a thread leak tested as a non-heap finding was refuted by a flat pool figure); a required column that keeps growing holds a non-leak verdict back as inconclusive, with a limitation naming it, and a leak verdict stays the primary's; on a test with no bound finding no `jvm.nmt.*` category votes or counts as a passed column (compiler and arena chunk swing by tens of MB on a flat workload and, by passing the gate by chance, took `jvm-pool-warmup` off the heap rule: it failed once in two runs with 'no rule-out'), they are only reported; while `jvm.classes_loaded`, `jvm.classes_unloaded` and `jvm.jit_seconds` are reported and never vote (`jvm.jit_seconds` and `jvm.classes_unloaded` also join the columns the unbound pick never votes on); the Go non-heap primary is unchanged (`go.goroutines`); the jcmd sample's `jvm.classes_loaded` is the cumulative counters of both the heap and the CDS archive minus the unloads, and the unloads are `jvm.classes_unloaded`. The route (`ledger add --origin residual --region non-heap`, an `N-WARM` sibling, `alloc-test`, refute and confirm, `resolve O --by`) is in `references/jvm.md`, `stages.md` section 7 and `evidence-and-report.md`. Measured on an idle driver (no workload, 150 checkpoints, JDK 21): the agent's own checkpoint work (class histogram, NMT call, probe walks) raised `jvm.nonheap_used` by 2.3 MB, 0.8 MB of it in the first 15 checkpoints and about 4 KB per checkpoint at the end (Code and Metaspace, no class growth). The earlier claim that the alloc-test stays fair because its extra window repeats the same work was wrong on a small JVM: the fade straddles the main and extra windows. The agent now runs its own readings `DLA_WARM_ROUNDS` times (default 30, 3 seconds at most) before the first checkpoint reads its values; that removes about two thirds of the early rise (first 15 checkpoints of 60: 0.60 and 0.83 MB without, 0.24 and 0.27 MB with) but not the drift of 10 to 20 KB per checkpoint that stays (also with 150 rounds, and with the type, NMT and probe readings skipped one by one, so it is not those): on an idle driver the alloc-test of a non-heap finding gave leak 3 of 3 without the warm-up and a mix of non-leak, leak and inconclusive with it (an earlier measurement; the 7 of 7 below is the later one); a driver whose only work is four hot methods (flat class count) gave non-leak 2, leak 2, inconclusive 2 of 6. Not solved: a small JVM's non-heap verdict from a single-process driver is not reliable (measured: `--extra 24` and `--step 5000` still gave leak, since the drift is linear in the window; idle drivers read leak 7 of 7 times at the default extra), so a leak verdict on `jvm.nonheap_used` with a class count that is not rising, an extra rise under the drift line and no required column rising is turned into inconclusive (the output carries `caution`, `next` prints no refute or confirm and says to give each checkpoint more work, with `--junit --step` kept for a JUnit repeat). The drift line is the larger of 1 MiB and 2x the measured drift (20 KB, the highest of 10 to 20 KB measured on idle drivers) of every extra checkpoint, so it is the 1 MiB floor up to extra 25 and grows with `--extra` above it; a required column rises by its kind, and every rising column is collected first: `jvm.threads` rises when its plateau reads continues or its net rise over the extra window is more than 2 threads, whatever its plateau reads, and `os.threads` only when its plateau reads continues (a thread count has no checkpoint jitter, and the plateau test cannot judge a count that grows only at some checkpoints: two real slow leaks, a daemon thread every 10th and every 15th unit with no trend, read inconclusive with net rises of 10 and 6 threads; threads added in bursts near the end of each window read plateau with a net rise of 5 or 6, and are growth too); a `jvm.nmt.*` byte category rises only when its plateau reads continues (bytes jitter), and `jvm.nmt.thread` also rises with a thread count that itself rises (a required column whose net rise is over the jitter; the category does not rise with a count within the jitter or one that failed the trend gate); the class count is not flat when its plateau reads continues, or reads inconclusive with a net rise of more than 8 classes. The two constants were measured on drivers that start no thread (idle, hot loops, a growing heap, slow idle; many runs under load, some with the JVM told 4 to 64 processors): the net rise of `jvm.threads` was 0 in 59 runs, so the thread jitter is that plus 1 (the floor of 2, as HotSpot starts and stops compiler threads by itself); `os.threads` is not judged by its net rise, because a later run of a driver that starts no thread, with the JVM told 32 processors (`-XX:ActiveProcessorCount=32`), rose 4 on it (HotSpot's own GC worker threads: series 38, 43, 42) while `jvm.threads` rose 0, and read a leak with a printed confirm; `os.threads` that rises alone (its plateau reads continues, `jvm.threads` and the NMT thread category not rising) is named with the thread capture and the result stays inconclusive; the JVM loads a few classes at moments tied to elapsed time, not to the workload, so the net rise of `jvm.classes_loaded` was 0 or 2 in 24 fast idle runs but 4 to 6 in 13 of 15 runs whose unit only sleeps (the run is slow; 5 was the largest that read inconclusive and 6 the largest that read plateau), so the class jitter is the largest rise seen (6) plus a margin of 2, and a class leak that adds 8 or fewer classes over the extra window cannot be told from it by the count and, while the count does not read continues, reads inconclusive, never a leak (a count that reads continues is a rising column whatever its net rise). The order: when a thread count or an NMT category rises, the result is the growing-column reading, which names all rising columns (the class count too when it is not flat), keeps the leak verdict for a thread count (an NMT category makes it inconclusive, unless the class count is not flat too: a class leak always raises Metaspace, so it keeps its verdict and the ledger commands stay beside the NMT capture) and gives the thread capture as `next` whenever a thread count rises, never a longer `--extra` (on a partial run, whose verdict is capped at inconclusive, `next` holds only the capture, with no refute, confirm or longer `--extra`); only when no required column rises and the class count is not flat is the class count named alone (the verdict stands, except on a partial run, whose note says it is inconclusive instead); only when nothing rises and the class count is flat is it the agent's drift; a result with no `jvm.classes_loaded` column is read as an unknown class count (no class note and no made-up rise), and with nothing rising it gets the agent-drift reading with a caution that the class count was not recorded or could not be judged; a command-mode harness (a fresh JVM per point) has no checkpoint drift and gets no override. A required `jvm.threads` that rises (its plateau reads continues, or its net rise is over 2 whatever the plateau reads) holds a non-leak verdict of the pools back as inconclusive, with the limitation naming it (six daemon threads started late, both pools and both counts reading plateau, used to be refuted); a thread count at a plateau with no rise is not listed as "could not be judged yet" when the result is inconclusive for another reason. A held-back column that keeps rising gets the same `next` as the capture; one that only could not be judged gets the longer `--extra`. When the alloc-test favours leak on the non-heap route, the route text says to confirm F and resolve the item `--by F`. `harness/jvm/README.md` and `jvm.md` state the limit and the line. Tests: `pick::tests::the_non_heap_primary_of_a_jvm_is_its_byte_column_not_the_thread_count`, `go_keeps_its_goroutines_as_the_non_heap_primary` (a guard: it cannot fail with the old list), `every_nmt_category_that_passed_must_plateau_with_the_pools_and_the_signs_only_report`, `compile_time_and_unloads_never_vote_on_an_unbound_test`, `a_passing_nmt_category_neither_votes_nor_stops_the_heap_rule_on_an_unbound_test`, `a_thread_count_that_grows_must_plateau_before_the_pools_may_refute_a_non_heap_finding`, `alloc_cmd::tests::a_growing_required_column_holds_back_the_plateau_of_the_primary` (the wiring: it calls `decide`, which `run` uses), `a_small_non_heap_leak_verdict_on_a_flat_class_count_carries_the_agent_caution`, `the_drift_line_scales_with_the_extra_window`, `a_rising_required_column_is_named_and_never_blamed_on_the_agent`, `the_drift_next_keeps_the_junit_mode`, `a_class_count_with_a_net_rise_is_not_flat` (net rises of 19, 12 and 9 classes are no flat count and name `jvm.classes_loaded`; 8 is jitter), `a_class_leak_beside_a_rising_nmt_category_keeps_its_verdict_and_the_ledger_commands`, `a_thread_count_that_reads_plateau_with_a_net_rise_over_the_jitter_is_growth`, `a_class_column_that_could_not_be_judged_is_unknown_not_flat`, `a_thread_count_that_reads_inconclusive_with_a_net_rise_is_growth_not_the_agents_drift` (net rises of 10 and 6), `a_thread_rise_within_the_jitter_of_an_idle_driver_is_the_agents_drift` (also `os.threads` with a net rise of 4 on a plateau, beside a flat `jvm.threads`), `os_threads_that_rises_alone_is_named_with_the_capture_and_stays_inconclusive`, `a_thread_count_that_plateaus_is_no_open_column_and_one_that_rises_is`, `a_thread_count_that_rises_while_the_pools_plateau_holds_the_plateau_back`, `an_nmt_byte_category_rises_only_when_its_plateau_reads_continues`, `classes_and_threads_both_rising_name_both_and_keep_the_thread_capture`, `the_class_note_states_true_numbers` (a rise that reads continues says it keeps rising and by how much; a partial run never says the verdict stands), `a_result_without_the_class_column_is_read_with_a_caution_not_a_made_up_class_note`, `a_held_back_thread_count_with_a_net_rise_is_rising_not_levelling` (the held-back and open-column split counts a thread count's net rise), `the_nmt_thread_category_joins_only_a_thread_count_that_itself_rises`, `a_partial_run_keeps_no_ledger_command_after_the_thread_capture`, `a_required_column_that_only_could_not_be_judged_is_no_growth`, `the_nmt_thread_category_rises_with_the_thread_count`, `a_thread_leak_with_its_nmt_category_points_at_the_thread_capture` (`next` names `threads.json`, not `nmt-diff.txt`), `a_column_that_only_could_not_be_judged_still_gets_the_longer_window`, the end-to-end `alloc_test_jvm::an_idle_driver_is_never_a_leak_verdict_over_a_long_extra_window_either` (extra 20 and 100; it passes with the old flat 1 MiB line too, since the idle rise at extra 100 is just under 1 MiB, so the scaling is covered by the unit test `the_drift_line_scales_with_the_extra_window` alone, judged by reading), `a_driver_that_keeps_daemon_threads_is_not_blamed_on_the_agents_drift` (a real driver whose unit keeps daemon threads), `a_driver_that_starts_a_daemon_thread_every_tenth_unit_keeps_the_leak_reading_and_names_the_thread_count` and `an_idle_driver_without_a_trend_is_never_a_leak_verdict_and_names_no_thread_count` (real drivers with no trend, so that the thread counts are required; the idle one runs the default extra and 100 on a fresh project each, because an alloc-test writes a trend of its own that would drop the thread counts from a second run on the same project, and it asserts that both thread columns are in the result; both read the thread naming from the full result file, since stdout carries no limitations; where the JVM is told it has many processors and `jvm.nonheap_used` plateaus, the every-tenth-unit test accepts an inconclusive result that holds the thread capture in `next`, and the idle tests check `ledger confirm` only when the result carries the caution); the whole `alloc_test_jvm` test binary passed in 4 rounds of two copies running at the same time (8 of 8 executions, 9 of 9 tests each, with the thread rules of this row), and on an earlier version once each with the JVM told 8, 16 and 32 processors (`-XX:ActiveProcessorCount`; not repeated since `os.threads` stopped being judged by its net rise, so a run with 32 processors told is not covered by a test; the figure behind the change is one real run of a driver that starts no thread), the end-to-end `alloc_test_jvm::the_agents_warm_up_takes_most_of_its_own_early_non_heap_growth_off_an_idle_driver` (an idle single-process driver, class count asserted flat, the rise from the first reading to point 15 with the warm-up under 0.85 of the rise without, measured again once before it fails, and on Linux with a cold rise of 250 KB or more also the rise from point 1 to point 15 under 0.75; see the row about the warm-up comparison of the agent), `judge::tests::a_required_column_that_does_not_plateau_holds_a_non_leak_verdict_back`, `a_jit_shaped_non_heap_curve_that_has_levelled_off_is_a_plateau`, `a_steady_per_iteration_non_heap_rise_keeps_its_slope_and_continues` (dla-cli), `series::catalog_tests::the_jvm_non_heap_columns_are_whole_process_non_heap_levels` (dla-core), `gate_h1::the_jvm_non_heap_category_columns_are_never_tied_and_the_non_heap_row_accounts_for_them`, `jcmd::tests::the_cumulative_class_counter_becomes_the_number_loaded_now` and the live `sample::jcmd_backend_reads_heap_threads_and_nmt` (classes loaded above 100 on a real JVM), `harness_run::jvm_agent_heap_and_probe_show_the_order_cache_leak` (the new columns, region non-heap) and `jvm_agent_without_nmt_reads_the_pools_instead_of_the_categories` (a real JVM with and without NMT), and the end-to-end `alloc_test_jvm::a_loop_that_warms_up_is_judged_a_plateau_on_the_non_heap_bytes_and_the_root_is_refuted` and `a_loop_that_defines_a_class_per_iteration_stays_a_leak` (a real JVM through `harness gen`, `harness run`, `ledger add --region non-heap`, `alloc-test` and the printed refute, test and confirm; the leak loop defines one hidden class per iteration and keeps it, the warm-up loop stops at 5000 classes; both skip without java, javac or sh). Seen failing with the change reverted (by editing the code, running the `alloc_cmd` unit tests, restoring the file; the first six mutations were run against the final code, the rest were seen on earlier versions of it and not repeated): thread jitter set to 1 (one unit test failed: `the_nmt_thread_category_joins_only_a_thread_count_that_itself_rises`), to 5 (the within-the-jitter test failed) and so high that no net rise counts (eight unit tests failed); the net rise of `os.threads` counted again (three tests failed: the within-the-jitter test, the plateau-holds-back test and the daemon-thread test); `os.threads` alone no longer inconclusive (its test failed); a thread count that plateaus listed as an open column (its test failed); the hold-back of a rising thread count removed (its test failed); (earlier versions:) the thread rule restored to need a plateau that is not read as one (the plateau-with-a-net-rise test failed); the thread-rise rule of `jvm.nmt.thread` loosened to any required thread count (the NMT thread test failed); `keeps_ledger_commands` without its partial term (its test failed); the class verdict dropped beside a rising NMT category (the class-leak test failed); the class jitter set back to 3 (the class-count and class-note tests failed); a class column that was not judged read as flat (the could-not-be-judged test failed); the classes-and-threads order flipped (its unit test failed); the partial-run wording off (the class note test failed); a missing class column read as flat (the no-class-column test failed). Judged by reading: the call of `keeps_ledger_commands` in the alloc-test run (a real run cut by the deadline was not made here), the idle no-trend driver test, which reads the same before the change, and the class-leak-beside-NMT reading on a real driver, which only the unit test covers. Seen failing on an earlier version and not repeated: `a_class_count_with_a_net_rise_is_not_flat` (jitter limit set to 10), `a_required_column_that_only_could_not_be_judged_is_no_growth` (all required columns counted as rising), `the_nmt_thread_category_rises_with_the_thread_count` (the thread category not joined to the thread count), `a_thread_leak_with_its_nmt_category_points_at_the_thread_capture` (thread branch of `deciding_capture` off), and `a_driver_that_keeps_daemon_threads_is_not_blamed_on_the_agents_drift` (required-column check removed); judged by reading: the drift-line, rising-column, held-back split and JUnit-`next` unit tests and the command-mode and JUnit wiring from `manifest.mode` and its tools; the four new pick and decide tests (NMT filter on the unbound path removed, thread requirement removed, `require_non_leak` replaced by the plain overall verdict), the idle-driver test (the `warmUp()` call commented out: 635 KB with against 611 KB without), both end-to-end tests (non-heap list without `jvm.nonheap_used`: the warm-up loop tested `jvm.threads`, the leak loop was judged non-leak), the `pick` list tests (`the_non_heap_primary...` and the NMT test; the Go test is a guard and cannot fail) and the NMT block disabled, the gate test (the `jvm.nmt.` catalog arm removed: a heap row over its threshold then leaves `jvm.nmt.*` columns as items), the jcmd unit test (cumulative count not reduced), the sample test (shared classes not added: 7 loaded), the two harness_run tests (the agent's category and pool lines disabled). Judged by reading: the two judge tests that pin existing plateau behaviour on non-heap numbers (the judge code is unchanged), `judge::tests::a_required_column...` is a unit test of the function alone; the wiring is the `alloc_cmd` test above (the end-to-end leak test does not cover it, because the wrap only changes a non-leak verdict), the drift caution's own unit test is of a new function and was not seen failing, its wiring is seen failing in `alloc_test_jvm::a_small_non_heap_rise_on_an_idle_driver_is_never_a_leak_verdict_and_prints_no_refute_or_confirm` (4 of 4 runs fail with the override disabled); the warm-up runs at a checkpoint only and after the pre-GC readings, because the class histograms it runs force full GCs that turned a command-mode exit record's `values_pre_gc` and a `--purge gc` before-reading into post-GC values (heap 78 MB / 1.5 MB read 2.2 / 2.2 MB, +0.7 to +3.4 s per point): `alloc_test_jvm::a_command_mode_exit_record_still_reads_the_heap_before_the_collection` (seen failing with the warm-up before the readings: pre-GC 2 212 080 against post-GC 2 211 944); the column count: a harness run writes 52 columns on a small project in command mode and 61 of 64 for a driver with no probe, the new columns rank as Other in the cap, which drops type counts (`jvm.class:*`) and group columns first, then Other, then probes and baselines, so on a driver the new columns push type counts out. The thread limitation reads as one sentence on a full and on a partial run, and does not say "alone" about a list of two columns (`alloc_cmd::tests::the_thread_limitation_reads_as_one_sentence_whatever_the_run`, seen failing with the old wording restored). Bench (`dla-bench dynamic --parallel 1`): `jvm-rule-compiler` (a real Metaspace leak) pass, `jvm-order-cache` and its bounded control pass, `go-private-module` and `go-subscription-workers` (with its closed twin) pass with the baseline's verdicts and numbers; `go-feed-merge` fails with `leak L2 (internal/indexer/indexer.go:24) is not confirmed`, as it did in the earlier log (same message, same alloc-test columns: `go.goroutines@internal/fetcher/session.go:30` leak, `os.rss` non-leak, `os.vm_hwm` leak), so the Go pick did not change it. With the final thread rules `jvm-order-cache`, `jvm-rule-compiler`, `jvm-pool-warmup` and `jni-order-codec` passed once each with the same confirmed, leaks found and no failures as the acceptance run (passed-column counts moved by a few noise columns). After the unbound-pick and thread changes `jvm-pool-warmup` passed 4 runs of 4 (it failed 1 of 2 before the change), and `jvm-rule-compiler`, `go-private-module` and `jvm-order-cache` pass. The sample's live `jvm.nonheap_used` of `dla sample --counters jcmd` stays Metaspace only (not changed here). |
| Findings could only be closed with a reason that was not true | corrected | A real Kotlin Spring Boot run measured JVM non-heap growth (28 B/iteration, flat class count, one NMT snapshot) and could not decide it; the ledger had no reason for "measured, and the measurement cannot decide", so the finding was closed `unresolved(time_budget)` with 50 of 60 minutes left ("the ledger made me pick a reason from a fixed list"). Every printed fix pre-filled `time_budget` | New reason `inconclusive` (ledger version 7, `M7Rules`; the enum has it last, so the derived order is unchanged). It is accepted for `unresolved` only (`suspected_static_only` is by definition never exercised: `invalid-value`) and only when a measurement is tied to the finding's family (`machine::tied_measurement`): a discriminator or intervention (authority test, live or dump) whose `finding` is in the family; a growth record that a member of the family cites (the gate's tie rule ties a finding that cites the growth evidence to every non-whole-process column of it, passed or not; a record whose passed columns are all whole-process ties to nothing, a record that failed its gate and has no passed column ties to nothing too when every column it measured is whole-process (`dla trend` now writes `failed_beyond_process`, the count of failed columns that are not; a record from before the count keeps the benefit of the doubt), and the raw `growth-series` record of `dla sample`, which has no trend and no gate and which the gate never ties, counts only when a column it lists is not whole-process) or whose passing column is tied to a member of the family; a discriminator or intervention that the family cites; or a residual, non-heap or capture record already in the supports of a member. A `workload` record (the harness ran), a whole-process column and evidence cited only on the resolve line do not count, so `inconclusive` is not the new `time_budget`; otherwise the new refusal `measurement-required` names the measured routes (`dla alloc-test`, `dla trend`) or `--reason not_exercised`. Open items take it when `dla gap --residual` opened them or their finding passes; checklist answers refuse it. `is_measurement` is shared with `view::measured`. A version 6 line carrying it is refused on replay (an older build would not have checked it); `closes_without_exercise` and `is_blocker_reason` now decide every reason explicitly (`inconclusive` is neither). The verifier decides from holder and mechanism whether the finding is a defect. What `dla ledger verify` accepts on a leak-side finding: an `N-*` label is a known archetype ID, so it is recorded, the label disagrees and the ledger opens the disagreement item with the verifier's mechanism and keeps the finding `confirmed` (the report shows `Verified: DISAGREES`; the user reads it, the finding is not turned into an unresolved one); so the variant needs no new disposition: the verifier gives the `N-*` ID, starts the mechanism with `reads as non-leak:` and the reply with `DISAGREES: reads as non-leak`, and a passing gate is said to show complete records, not a leak. | JUnit repeat, no-harness and failed-build steps in the Coverage warning and the final gate | corrected | After a JUnit repeat (`dla harness gen --lang jvm --junit`) reached none or a minority of the entry points, the gate fix and the report's warning said to fill the DLA-HOOK bodies of that harness, which has none, and running it again repeats the result. With no harness on disk the warning said `dla harness gen --lang <rt>` even where `dla harness gen` refuses for lack of a workload (its fix names `--driver`), so the golden `zero_coverage_explained.md` contradicted its own open item. A harness whose build failed was answered with "make a driver", and a blocker recorded on `intake.workload` was not mentioned. The bench `finish` recorded `env_unavailable` on `intake.workload` for a runtime whose real refusal was `workload-required`, which also silenced the rules for every other runtime | `gate::driver_step` passes over a harness whose manifest names `junit-platform-launcher` when it looks for a driver to fill, and when the harnesses of the runtime that ran are all JUnit repeats (a JUnit repeat that was only generated, or that failed to run, does not count, nor does one that ran next to a command harness) it says the project's tests reach N of M entry points and names `dla harness gen --lang <rt> --driver`, filling its hooks and `dla harness run <the new id>`; the same text is the gate fix, the Stop hook's text and the report's warning. `gate::open_harness_step` returns a `HarnessStep`: the unrun harness first, then `Blocked` with the reason and remedy recorded on `intake.workload` (the warning ends `Blocked: intake.workload records ...` instead of a step), then the driver step, then, with no harness, plain `dla harness gen --lang <rt>` only when a main or script workload with an iteration argument was discovered (`workload/*.json`), else the `--driver` step. `gate::build_failed_harness` names a harness only when no hook driver of the runtime has built (a hook driver has built when it ran or its last attempt is not a build failure): then a harness whose attempt record is `failed` with reason `build_fails`, nothing measured, generated after every harness that ran, or a hook driver that never built, gets the step that points at the build error and the `build_fails` blocker, the same in the warning, the gate fix and the Stop hook. The newest hook driver that built is the harness whose DLA-HOOK bodies the step names, so an older failed build never hides it and the `build_fails` escape is not offered beside it. The edited clone `dla intervene` makes keeps its source's mode and tools, so `hook_drivers_in` leaves out the clone: `dla intervene` writes a `clone-of` marker file into the clone's directory the moment it makes it (an intervention stopped by a signal or a refusal before it records anything leaves no `intervention` record), and a harness an `intervention` record names as `treated_harness` is left out too, for runs made before the marker; after an intervention the step still names the driver that was filled, not the clone. When the build failed and no iteration ran, the build failure is said once: by the step when it is the build step (the cause then says only that no iteration was recorded), and by the cause (`gate::unbuilt_harness`) whenever the step is not the build step of `gate::build_step_harness` (a newer harness generated and not run, command harness, hook driver or JUnit repeat, or a blocker recorded on `intake.workload`), and the cause names nothing when a newer harness was tried and failed another way, since that is then the latest reason. The bench `finish` now closes each leftover static finding `suspected_static_only(env_unavailable)` with a remedy naming the refusals `dla harness gen` and `dla harness run` gave and the `--driver` fix, and records nothing on the workload item. Docs: evidence-and-report.md states the real warning rule (skipped only after iterations, when the exercised entry points cannot be measured: Rust and native, or Python without `sys.monitoring`), stages.md and recovery.md name the JUnit and build-failure branches and the command-harness branch, harness/jvm/README.md reads cleanly. Tests in `crates/dla-cli/tests/keep_going.rs`: `a_junit_repeat_has_no_hooks_so_the_step_makes_a_hook_driver` (a JUnit harness at 0 and at 20 of 225 entry points, then a hook driver H2), `the_no_harness_step_follows_what_gen_would_do`, `a_recorded_workload_blocker_replaces_the_step_in_the_warning`, `a_harness_whose_build_failed_is_not_answered_with_another_driver` (also a hook driver whose build failed after a command harness that ran), `an_older_build_failure_does_not_hide_the_hook_driver_that_ran`, `an_older_hook_driver_that_never_built_does_not_hide_a_newer_one_that_ran` (a failed hook driver and a newer hook driver that ran, at 0 and at 20 of 225 entry points; a failed hook driver and a JUnit repeat that ran gets the build step), `the_hook_driver_that_never_built_is_sent_to_its_build_error` (a hook driver whose build failed, then a command harness that ran: the step points at the build), `the_step_names_the_newest_built_hook_driver_and_never_an_intervention_clone` (two hook drivers that built, an intervention clone that ran, one with no attempt record, and one with only the marker file and no `intervention` record, two hook drivers that never built, a failed hook driver alone, a driver that ran then a newer failed one), `a_failed_build_is_said_once_even_when_the_step_is_another_harness`, `a_failed_build_is_said_once_for_every_step_that_is_not_the_build` (a command harness, a JUnit repeat and a blocker beside the failed build, and a newer driver that failed to run), `the_tests_count_is_named_only_when_the_junit_repeat_is_what_ran`, `the_refusal_of_a_close_for_lack_of_a_harness_gives_the_same_step_as_the_warning`; `render::zero_of_many_after_iterations_is_flagged_next_to_measured` and the golden `zero_coverage_explained.md` (the warning and the open item now agree); `cover::tests::loaded_project_classes_without_a_matched_entry_are_counted_and_said` (now with the singular text). Seen failing with the change reverted: the keep_going tests (JUnit filter and text removed; `gen_needs_driver` forced false; blocker branch removed; `build_failed_harness` forced `None`; the check that a built hook driver comes first removed (the hook-driver test and three older ones failed); the intervention-clone filter removed, the marker check removed, the newest-first order dropped, the never-built driver taken from the oldest instead of the newest, the cause that names the failed build when the step is another harness removed, the cause decided by `build_failed_harness` instead of the step, and the newest-tried condition of `unbuilt_harness` dropped (each failed the clone and newest test or one of the two said-once tests); the build-order and `ran`-only JUnit conditions dropped; the refusal's `needs_driver` forced false), and the Blocked line with a remedy that ends in a period (it printed two periods before the trailing period was trimmed). The singular text test was seen failing with the plural wording always used. Judged by reading: the Stop hook, which prints the same gate fix. Bench: `dla-bench dynamic --parallel 1 --only <f>` for jni-order-codec, go-private-module, jvm-billing-private-repo, native-vendor-codec, rust-private-registry and python-vendor-wheel: every row has the same confirmed, refuted, leaks found, harnesses and errors as the previous acceptance run, no failures. A harness `dla harness run` refused for time writes an attempt with reason `time_budget`; it is no try (like `hooks_unfilled` and `interrupted`), so after `dla run extend` the harness is still the one to run and the closes that say nothing ran are refused with `dla harness run H1`, instead of the step turning into "make a driver" and the rule lifting. A harness that never built, when no harness of the runtime ran and no hook driver built, also refuses the closes `no_harness`, `not_exercised` and `time_budget` while more than the floor is left: the refusal, the final gate item `harness_not_run` and the open finding's fix give the build step (fix the build error that `dla harness run H` prints, or record `build_fails` on `intake.workload`; the reason `build_fails` is never refused), the same as the report's warning. An open finding whose default close the ledger would refuse for an unrun, unbuilt or missing harness has that step as its `fix` in `dla gate check --final` and `dla run status`, and the Stop hook shows it; `coverage_contradiction` is listed before the findings. Tests in `keep_going.rs`: `a_harness_refused_for_time_is_no_try_after_the_budget_was_extended`, `a_harness_that_never_built_refuses_the_closes_and_names_the_build_step` (also: a harness of the runtime that ran, and a blocker on the workload item, lift it), `an_open_finding_names_the_harness_step_where_its_close_would_be_refused` (no harness, an unrun harness, a harness that ran), each seen failing with its change removed (the new no-try reason, the failed-build branch, the finding step; the last also failed the first two). The build-failure refusal of a hook driver names `harness/H/driver` (the file the build log names) instead of the work copy; judged by reading. The `clone-of` marker is checked by `intervene_cmd::clone::tests::the_clone_is_marked_the_moment_it_is_made_with_the_id_it_came_from` (a real clone of a harness directory; the marker holds the source id and the source has none), seen failing with the marker write removed from `clone_harness`. `NoHarness::fix` (the refusal of a close for lack of a harness) now gives the same step as the warning: `--driver` and the hook bodies when `dla harness gen` would refuse for lack of a workload, plain `dla harness gen --lang <rt>` otherwise. | JVM coverage counted no project class when the project root or a class location is spelled through a symlink (macOS runner: 0 of 2 classes in `harness_jvm_coverage`) | corrected, to be confirmed in CI | On the macOS runner the temp dir is under `/var/folders`, a link to `/private/var`, so the project root from `DLA_PROJECT` and the JVM's code source location were two spellings of one directory, and `DlaDump.java` compared them as paths without resolving links: `isProjectClass` and `isTestLocation` found no class under the project and the count read 0 (the three tests `a_drivers_last_checkpoint_carries_the_coverage_with_the_project_first`, `a_driver_cut_off_before_its_last_checkpoint_has_still_written_the_coverage` and `a_probe_never_loads_a_class_into_the_coverage` failed with 0 where 2 are expected). Reproduced on Linux with the project root given by a link to the directory that holds the classes, and in the agent's own check with a location spelled through a link. A run through `dla harness run` hands the agent the real scratch root and the project's packages, so it is reached only when the root is spelled through a link and no package is known, or when a loader keeps a link spelling. | The agent judges a location by the names as written and, failing that, with every symlink resolved (a part that does not exist stays as written below the longest part that does; a link kept inside the project, such as `build` or `out/test`, still counts by its own name, and results for existing paths are kept so a large class path is not resolved again for every class). Tests: `harness_jvm_coverage::a_project_reached_through_a_symlinked_directory_still_counts_its_classes` (project 0 where 2 are expected with the old agent, seen failing) and `harness_jvm_coverage::locations_and_the_source_file_attribute_are_read_by_the_agent` (its link cases for both spellings seen failing with the old agent; its link-inside-the-project cases pass with the old agent and guard against resolving links instead of adding to the names). The three macOS failures themselves can only be shown by the macOS runner. |
| A driver that keeps daemon threads made its harness exit 1 on the macOS runner (`alloc_test_jvm`) | corrected, to be confirmed in CI | The unit of `a_driver_that_keeps_daemon_threads_is_not_blamed_on_the_agents_drift` started three threads that never end per unit, about three thousand in one JVM; macOS limits a process to about two thousand threads, so the JVM would die with "unable to create native thread" and every measured process exited 1 ("every measured process of mode fresh exited with a non-zero code"). Judged from the code and the macOS limit: the log of the runner holds only the exit code, and the limit was not reproduced here. | The unit starts one thread per five units (ten per point, about two hundred in all), which still raises the thread count at every point. A failed command of the `alloc_test_jvm` tests (the harness run, the `alloc-test` rerun, which starts about twice the threads, and every other call through the helper) now prints the tail of every harness log of the project, so the next failure explains itself (the print was seen with a unit that throws and with an `alloc-test` call that fails; the test passes with the change on Linux, JDK 21). |
| The warm-up comparison of the agent asserted relations that a loaded machine breaks (`the_agents_warm_up_takes_most_of_its_own_early_non_heap_growth_off_an_idle_driver`) | corrected, to be confirmed in CI | Two defects of the test, neither of the product. First, on the macOS runner the warm run started higher than the cold run (the warm-up ran: 9.5 MB against 9.0 MB at the first point) and its first step was a fraction of the cold one (about 35 KB against 200 KB), but the early rise over the later points (point 1 to 15) was the same for both: the cold run rose only about 340 KB, which is the steady drift of about 25 KB per point and a small first step (warm 327 to 364 KB against cold 336 to 347 KB in two runs); the comparison of that rise was made only where the cold rise was over 500 KB. Second, the start value and first-step assertions that replaced it depend on how much CPU the JVM gets, because the warm-up stops after 3 s and a loaded machine warms less. With four harness runs at once (what the test binary's own threads do on a four-core runner) 2 of 88 pairs measured here failed them with the warm-up working (JDK 25: cold first step 64 KB, warm 51 KB, over the 0.7 line; JDK 21: ratio 0.84), an independent check saw 2 of 24, and two cold runs against each other gave a first-step ratio of 0.54 to 1.85, so the line lay inside the range of noise. The earlier row called these assertions true on every build; that was not shown, and the measured ranges are below. The 500 KB line also switched the early-rise comparison off on JDK 25 (cold rise 0.33 to 0.42 MB here, 0.36 to 0.47 MB seen elsewhere) and at times on JDK 21. | The test no longer asserts the start value or the first step. It asserts one relation on the rise from the first reading to point 15: the warm run rises by less than 0.85 of the cold run (the warm-up has loaded the checkpoints' own code before the first reading). Measured with four copies of the measurement at once, on JDK 21 and JDK 25 here: warm over cold 0.28 to 0.54 in 88 pairs; two cold runs against each other 0.88 to 1.15 in 64 pairs. Measured by an independent check: warm over cold at most 0.58 in 41 Linux pairs and 0.66 and 0.74 in the two macOS runner logs; two cold runs 0.72 to 1.38. The line lies between the two ranges measured here but inside the one measured elsewhere (0.72 once), and the ranges are close on the macOS side. A first pair that passes ends the test (a pass is not measured twice), and only a first pair that fails is measured again, which guards a working warm-up that reads near the line; the two are judged together (sums) before the test fails. On Linux, where the cold rise from point 1 to point 15 is 0.25 MB or more (JDK 21 0.50 to 0.69 MB, JDK 25 0.33 to 0.42 MB here), the warm rise must also be under 0.75 of it (measured 0.29 to 0.64 here with four runs at once, at most 0.69 elsewhere; it is the first to give way on a machine loaded far beyond that: with sixteen copies of the test at once 4 of 16 failed on each JDK even with the second pair, all on this relation, while the 0.85 relation held; the warm-up stops after 3 s and warms less); this runs on JDK 25 and is not claimed on macOS, where that rise is the drift alone. The class count stays flat in both runs. Tests: `alloc_test_jvm::the_agents_warm_up_takes_most_of_its_own_early_non_heap_growth_off_an_idle_driver`, run 20 times on JDK 21 and 20 times on JDK 25 with three other copies running beside each run: 20 of 20 passed on each (no run needed its second pair); with `DLA_WARM_ROUNDS=0` on both sides 12 of 12 failed on JDK 21 and 12 of 12 on JDK 25. Not shown here: the macOS runner (its two logs are why the line is 0.85 and why the early-rise comparison is Linux only), and how often a pair of two cold runs falls under 0.85 (none of 64 here, 0.72 once elsewhere) |
| A point that outlasts the deadline was refused instead of cut on a slow runner (`harness_run::a_point_that_outlasts_the_deadline_is_cut_and_the_partial_series_says_so`) | corrected, to be confirmed in CI | On the macOS runner the refusal read `estimated at 7 s but only 3 s remain` with a fix of `--iterations 5` or a smaller step: after the first two points the run extrapolates the pace linearly, and a second point that took about twice the first (a few tenths of a second each) gave an estimate over the 3 s left before the constant 4 s deadline of the test, so the run was refused with `time-budget` before the cut it tests. Points on that runner cost a few tenths of a second, not more; the extrapolation of two uneven points is what overshot. | The test now has three fast points and a fourth that would sleep 60 s, so only two points are estimated after the second, and its deadline is derived from a dry run of the same command on a fixture of its own (twelve times the cost of one point plus 3 s, at least 8 and at most 30 s, so a second point that costs up to about a second and a half more than the first is still not refused). The failure messages carry the measured cost. Seen failing with the cut disabled (the point ran its 60 s to the end), and passing with the second point made to cost 0.4, 1.0 and 1.5 s more on Linux; a stall of 2 s in the second point is still refused, which is the product's estimate and not the cut. |
| Tests that started a copy of `sleep` named `java` failed on a runner whose coreutils is one multi-call binary | corrected, to be confirmed in CI | On Ubuntu 26.04 `sleep` picks its tool by the name it is started under, so a copy named `java` exits at once (`process N is not running`). It no longer depends on how `sleep` is built. Tests: every test of `attach`, `summarize_conformance` and `attach_tools`, run with the stand-in first on PATH and with the normal PATH on Linux. Seen failing before the change, passing after. The macOS symlink branch of the target in `summarize_conformance` and `attach_tools` is judged by reading only (off Linux the product takes the executable name from the process arguments) and waits for the macOS runner |
| `jcmd` backend could not read `GC.heap_info` of JDK 25 | corrected | JDK 25 prints `total reserved 262144K, committed 221184K, used 3204K` for G1 where JDK 21 prints `total 221184K, used 3204K` (the committed size, with the same flags); the Serial collector's generations are `DefNew` and `Tenured` instead of `def new generation` and `tenured generation`; and the Metaspace lines are gone. `parse_heap_info` rejected G1 and Serial, so the jcmd counters were blocked, and on the runner `sample::auto_picks_jcmd_for_a_jvm`, `jcmd_backend_reads_heap_threads_and_nmt` and `jcmd_without_native_memory_tracking_is_a_limitation` failed. Parallel, ZGC and Shenandoah read the same on both | The parser reads both layouts. `jvm.heap_committed` is the heap's committed size in both (`total` up to JDK 21, `committed` on JDK 25). Where there is no Metaspace line, `jvm.nonheap_used` comes from `VM.metaspace basic`: its `Metaspace used` line on JDK 25, its `Both: ... used.` line on JDK 21; when neither is there the limitations say so. Tests: `jcmd::tests::heap_info_of_jdk_25_reads_committed_not_reserved` and `heap_info_of_the_other_collectors_on_jdk_25` (captured JDK 25 output), `metaspace_comes_from_either_command`, `poll_reads_the_metaspace_of_a_jdk_25_heap_info_from_the_second_command` and `poll_says_so_when_no_command_gives_the_metaspace` (a stand-in `jcmd` answering with captured output), all seen failing without the change, and the three `sample` tests above, which pass with JDK 25 first on PATH (`jcmd_backend_reads_heap_threads_and_nmt` now also asserts `jvm.nonheap_used`). The JVM-dependent tests of dla-sample, dla-parsers, dla-backends, dla-cli and dla-workload passed with JDK 25 first on PATH, except the Kotlin Gradle ones in the next row and `harness_capture::a_jni_library_gets_the_shim_and_its_native_stack_resolves_to_the_rust_line`: the Kotlin 2.0 compiler it runs from the local Maven cache throws on JDK 25 (see the next row); it passed on the runner, whose log does not show which compiler it used. The test now compiles a one-line Kotlin file with the compiler its `build.sh` would pick (`kotlinc`, else the embeddable 2.0.21 compiler of the local Maven cache) and skips, printing the reason, when that does not run on the JDK in use: seen failing with JDK 25 first on PATH before the change (`IllegalArgumentException: 25.0.5.1`) and skipping after it, and it still runs and passes on JDK 21 here. `ok_run` of that test file prints the lines that name the error, then the tail of each harness log. The Epsilon layout is still not read on any JDK |
| Kotlin Gradle test projects failed on JDK 25 | corrected, to be confirmed in CI | On the runner the online retry of the Kotlin Gradle build stopped with `Inconsistent JVM-target compatibility detected for tasks compileJava (25) and compileKotlin (21)`; the offline attempt failing first with `Plugin [id` was expected, not the cause, and the product behaves as designed (offline first, then online). That mismatch is only the first error: the Kotlin compilers of the Kotlin Gradle plugin 1.9.24 and 1.9.25 and of Kotlin 2.0.21 fail in `JavaVersion.parse` (`IllegalArgumentException`) on every JDK 25 (`25`, `25.0.1` and `25.0.5.1` alike) while the four-part `21.0.12.1` and `24.0.2.1` parse, and Gradle 8.14 runs that compiler on its own JDK | The test projects pin Java and Kotlin to 17. That the pin removes the mismatch on JDK 22 to 24 is judged by reading only: no JDK 22 to 24 is installed here and no runner uses one, no test fails without the pin on an installed JDK (on JDK 21 the three tests pass with and without it, on JDK 25 they skip), and the inference rests on the runner log, which shows compileJava 25 against compileKotlin 21. The three tests `harness_driver_build::a_gradle_kotlin_driver_runs_on_the_resolved_runtime_class_path`, `harness_junit::kotlin_gradle_project_series_grows_with_the_repeated_tests` and `a_kotlin_spring_boot_test_run_exercises_the_entry_points_of_the_classes_it_loads` pass on JDK 21 here with the pin. When Gradle runs on JDK 25 or newer they print `skipped: the Kotlin compiler of this test's Kotlin Gradle plugin cannot run on JDK 25 (JDK 24 or older needed)` and return, as the other tool-dependent tests do; seen here with JDK 25 first on PATH. They run again when the plugin is moved to a Kotlin release whose compiler runs on JDK 25, which was not tested here (Maven Central refused the downloads) |

Seen failing here: `cargo clippy --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu -p dla-probe --all-targets -- -D warnings` reported the lint before the change and passes after it (no new test: the behaviour is unchanged, and the existing `dla-probe` registry tests pass). The whole workspace was checked with clippy 1.100 for `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` and `aarch64-apple-darwin` (the C parsers of the tree-sitter crates were replaced by stand-in compiler and archiver scripts, since no foreign C compiler is installed; only type and lint checking is affected) and is clean; `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` was checked the same way once the stand-in archiver created the file named by its `-out:` argument, and the whole workspace is clean on it too. Doc leftovers fixed in the same change: doc comment lines over the wrap width in `gate.rs`, `harness.rs` and `timebudget.rs`; this table's rows for a harness that must be run (the step for a run refused for time is `dla harness run <H>`, not the build; the count of failing tests no longer quotes a total), the planning tags in the titles of the rows of this table, an unescaped pipe inside a code span in the benchmark follow-up table; a stray line in `harness/jvm/README.md`; two over-long lines of `skills/detect-leak-anywhere/references/jvm.md`; and the statement that a class leak of 8 or fewer classes reads inconclusive, never a leak, which holds only while the class count does not read continues | Lint run on the Windows and macOS targets here; the Windows runner confirms the Windows job passes. The text changes have no test |

| The thread total of a short JVM series counted the flight recorder's own threads (`jvm-audit-window` failed the dynamic run once in twelve) | corrected | Dumps taken during a capture caught `JFR Recorder Thread` and `JFR Shutdown Hook` in some of four points (9, 10, 9, 11 threads); `dla trend` read the total as steady and the final gate asked for something that accounts for it. | JVM coverage read a project given through a link as test output when a directory above it is named like one | corrected, to be confirmed in CI | `DlaDump.isTestLocation` judged the part below the project only when the location lay under the project by its written name; a location that lay under it only after links were resolved (the project given by a link, the JVM reporting the real path) was judged by its whole path, so a project below a directory such as `java/test` read as test output. A link kept inside the project (`out/test`) still counts by its own name.
