The link spellings on the macOS runner are to be confirmed in CI |
| The purge test read a few KB of live growth as retained (`go-gc-tuned-batch` failed the dynamic run once with no rule-out) | corrected | On the runner `go.heap_live` and `go.heap_objects` both passed the first trend by chance. They hold the same bytes after a forced collection, but only `go.heap_objects` is in the purge record of `free-os-memory`, so only it got a purge verdict: `go.heap_live non-leak (plateau, purge not-run)` and `go.heap_objects inconclusive (plateau, same slope and rise, purge retained)`. One inconclusive vote makes the overall verdict inconclusive. A local run of the fixture shows the sizes: the column stands a few KB higher before the purge than at the end of the warm-up, live objects that a purge cannot give back. The plateau test of the same column treats a rise of at most 32 KiB as a plateau whatever the noise reads (`MIN_BYTE_RISE`), but the purge test called any growth above one billionth of the level growth, so the two tests of one column disagreed on what growth is. A larger limit (the 3 noise units of the plateau test) was tried and left out: it read slow real leaks under the scatter of the deciding column as non-leak (a bound non-leak refutes), which costs more than an inconclusive flat run. Other columns with the first shape (`go.heap_objects` of `go-ticker-modern`, read inconclusive with purge retained in an earlier run) have the same cause | `judge::purge` takes the column's smallest growth: the minimum rise the plateau test uses (32 KiB for a byte column, none for other columns). A growth at or below it is `no-growth`; growth above it reads released, partial or retained as before, and `judge::combine` is unchanged (a plateau over real growth with a retained purge is still inconclusive, a retained purge still turns an undecided plateau into leak). Tests: `judge::tests::a_growth_at_or_below_the_columns_minimum_rise_is_no_growth_for_the_purge` (below, at and above the minimum for a byte column, and unchanged for a column with no minimum), `alloc_cmd::tests::a_flat_go_column_with_a_few_kb_of_live_growth_reads_non_leak_not_retained` (a series of the Go shape: a few KB since the warm-up, purge before equal to the last point, after slightly higher; reads plateau, purge `no-growth`, non-leak) and `alloc_cmd::tests::a_growth_inside_the_columns_own_scatter_but_above_its_minimum_is_still_growth_for_the_purge` (the leak side: a noisy column whose last reading is above the warm-up point by more than the minimum but inside its scatter reads purge `retained` and inconclusive, never non-leak; a step far above the scatter reads the same). The first and second were seen failing with the minimum taken out of the comparison, the third with a noise limit added to the minimum. The existing purge tests pass unchanged. The margin on the Go shape is thin: the column rose by a few KB over the default window against a minimum of 32 KiB, so a much longer extra window could cross it. Open: a flat Python run in which any column of memory in use passed the first trend can still read inconclusive, because `os.rss` then votes (it votes whenever any such column passed, not only when it passed itself) and its purge measures growth against the single point at the end of the warm-up, which in real runs stands below the later points (the allocator warming up) by more than the minimum, while the trim returns nothing; some command-level runs read inconclusive this way. That is the cost of keeping the purge rule as decided, and the rule was not widened for it. `dla-bench dynamic --parallel 1` on `go-gc-tuned-batch`, `go-buffer-pool`, `go-ticker-modern`, `python-graph-churn`, `python-pool-fragmentation`, `python-report-memo-bounded`, `node-price-board-flat`, `node-template-cache`, `rust-tree-parent-links-weak`, `rust-batch-buffers`, `native-arena-reuse` and `jvm-order-cache-bounded` passed all twelve with the final binaries, which were built after the last code change of this row (`jni-order-codec` was run with them and also passed); the refuted and passed-column counts differ from the earlier acceptance run only where a different set of columns passed the first trend by chance. The two failures cannot be made to happen by rerunning (the first trend has to pass the extra column by chance), so the proof is the prepared-series tests, not a loop. Judged by reading: the rule is the same for every runtime's byte column, and a column that grew by more than the minimum is judged as before |
| `os.rss_file` read retained on a flat Python run, and its retained purge made the verdict inconclusive (`python-graph-churn` failed the dynamic run once under load) | corrected | In a full local run under load `os.rss_file` passed the first trend as a stair on a flat, noisy series over 17 points. Its purge record read above every point of the series before the purge and higher again after it. Cause, read from the agent: the Python `trim` purge imported ctypes and loaded the C library after the checkpoint had read its values, inside the measurement, and took `before` from a new reading. Measured in a bare interpreter, that import adds resident memory (mostly anonymous, some file-backed) and the first `malloc_trim` call a little more, and a later `import platform` between the two readings adds anonymous memory too; in fixture runs `before` stood above the last point of the series for `os.rss`, and by a few hundred KB for `os.rss_file`, so a flat run read "growth, retained" on both columns. The purge is asked only of the process of the last point, so that last point also stood above the others once the load moved. Per purge kind, whether its own step can move `os.rss_file` (read in each harness): Python `trim`, yes, measured as above; Python `gc`, no (maps nothing); JVM `trim-native-heap` and `gc`, run code the program has not run before, judged by reading only; C driver, allocator shim and Rust `trim`, `before` is the checkpoint's own reading; Go and Node purge records do not carry the column, so its purge is never read there (not-run). No purge kind gives file-backed pages back | The Python agent loads ctypes, libc and `platform` and calls `malloc_trim` once at start-up in every process on Linux, so nothing is imported between the two readings of the purge, and the purge takes `before` from the checkpoint's own reading. `os.rss_file` stays a voting column, but a retained purge reading of it never contradicts a plateau (`pick::PURGE_CANNOT_CONTRADICT`; the reading stays in the result), because no purge gives file pages back; against an undecided or continuing plateau a retained reading counts as for any column, so a leak of mapped files still reads leak. Tests: `harness_capture_python::the_trim_purge_reads_before_from_the_checkpoints_own_reading` and `harness_capture_python::every_process_loads_the_trim_library_at_start_up_not_only_the_purged_one` (the first asserts `before` equal to the checkpoint's reading and `after` at most 32 KiB above `before`; both seen failing with the old agent, the first also with only the `platform` import moved back into the purge step); `alloc_cmd::tests::a_flat_file_backed_column_with_a_retained_purge_reads_non_leak_and_keeps_the_reading` (seen failing with the exemption removed) and `alloc_cmd::tests::a_retained_purge_still_contradicts_the_plateau_of_an_ordinary_column` (the same series under another column name stays inconclusive); `alloc_cmd::tests::growing_file_backed_memory_beside_growing_rss_reads_leak_and_names_both` (`os.rss` and `os.rss_file` both keep growing: leak, both columns named); `pick::tests::a_file_backed_column_that_passed_the_gate_votes_beside_os_rss`; and the command-level `alloc_test::a_flat_file_backed_column_that_passed_the_gate_reads_non_leak_on_a_flat_run` (a real Python harness run of `python-report-memo-bounded`, whose first trend is edited so that only `os.rss_file` passes; the column votes and reads non-leak; it skips off Linux). In the runs made the purge of the column read `no-growth` in most and `retained` in some, so that test reaches the exemption only in some runs; the exemption is pinned exactly by the unit tests above. The overall verdict is not asserted there, because `os.rss` votes too and its purge measures growth against a warm-up point that stands below the later points of a real run, so a flat run reads inconclusive through it in some runs; only the `os.rss_file` part of this row is corrected. Other columns with a purge retained and an inconclusive verdict in the acceptance runs: `os.vm_hwm`, `os.vm_data` and `native.peak_bytes` do not vote; `os.rss` votes and its retained is real where the growth is megabytes that a trim or collection did not return (`node-price-board-flat` and `node-template-cache` in some runs, where another column passed the first trend by chance, so `os.rss` voted); `os.rss_anon` of `rust-tree-parent-links-weak` (a few tens of KB since the warm-up, trim returned nothing) is above the minimum and no mechanism was found; `node.heap_used` of `node-socket-heartbeat-cleared` (a few hundred KB of live growth since the warm-up) is real retained growth; `go.total`, `node.heap_total` and `node.heap_used` of the leak fixtures are real. `dla-bench dynamic` on the twelve fixtures named in the row above passed all twelve with the same final binaries, `python-graph-churn` among them (it failed the earlier run) |
| The shim reader read another process's record (`jni-order-codec` flake) | corrected | The fixture's command starts two JVMs with the allocator shim preloaded, the project's test program and then the program, and each writes a shim exit record with stacks. The harness split kept both, and `shim-stack-groups` (`type-histogram-diff` and `alloc-stack-diff` on `shim-dump.jsonl`) kept the one with most live bytes. Totals of a local run's records: the test program 41858379 and the program 47769802 at the first point; the test program's figure moves by tens of MB between runs (JIT arenas alive at exit). When it was the larger one, capture 1 was the test program, which has no live block at the leak's stack: `holder absent from dump 1`, both summaries failed their gate and L-HANDLE was refused `label-evidence-missing` (twice in about six CI runs, never locally) | The record of a capture is chosen by identity. The harness writes the shim record of the process it measured for the point (the one whose shim figures the series carries) and nothing when that process has none, with a limitation line naming the process. The reader drops tool processes, prefers the process whose argv carries the capture's iteration count (the same helpers as the harness's choice of the measured process), and uses the size only among the processes still left, with a limitation line that names how many processes wrote a record, how many were left after the tool and identity steps and which was kept; where the number chose between several programs, a line says so (one line per evidence record, the same text for all capture files of the summary). Other readers of dump files were looked at; none else picks by size. `dla-bench dynamic` now prints, for a label refused for missing evidence and for a refused confirmation, the limitation lines of each failed summary (the two lines every such summary carries are left out, at most three are shown) and, for the holder its gate names, the bytes and blocks per capture with the pid and last argv words of the first capture and of the first captures the holder is absent from; each argv word passes the redactor before it is cut to its last characters, in the limitation line as in the raw file; a refused confirmation also prints the reasons of every failed summary first (a refused label prints them in its own failure line), then at most eight detail lines. A harness without the shim (Python, Node, a JVM without a JNI library) gets no limitation for the missing shim record, and a run without `--capture` leaves the `shim-dump.jsonl` files and capture directories of an earlier capturing run in place. Tests `the_split_keeps_the_measured_process_record_not_the_larger_one`, `a_measured_process_without_a_shim_record_gets_no_file_and_never_another_one`, `shim_records_of_a_jvm_dump_are_split_out_for_the_command`, `a_missing_shim_record_is_noted_only_where_the_shim_is_preloaded`, `the_shim_counts_as_preloaded_only_with_the_shim_library_in_the_preload_variable`, `a_managed_dump_without_a_shim_harness_gets_no_limitation_and_a_run_without_capture_keeps_the_file`, `the_record_of_the_process_with_the_iteration_count_wins_over_a_larger_one` (also reads the raw file's `pid` and `argv_tail`), `with_no_iteration_count_in_any_argv_the_largest_is_kept_and_the_limitation_says_so`, `the_process_with_the_iteration_count_wins_over_a_larger_one_and_a_tool_is_dropped`, `size_decides_only_among_equals_and_the_limitation_names_the_choice`, `a_lone_process_and_a_lone_tool_are_read_without_a_limitation`, `a_long_argv_word_keeps_its_end`, `the_limitation_shapes_secret_looking_argv_words_and_omits_empty_brackets`, `a_failed_gate_shows_its_limitations_and_the_holder_per_capture_with_its_processes`, `a_refused_confirmation_prints_the_detail_of_the_failed_summaries`, `the_detail_of_failed_summaries_is_filtered_by_operation_and_capped`, `the_reasons_of_every_failed_summary_come_first_and_are_not_cut`, `stale_capture_files_are_removed_at_the_start_of_a_capturing_run_only` and `a_label_refused_for_missing_evidence_prints_the_steps_that_could_have_produced_it`. Each hunk was reverted in turn, the tests run and the hunk put back, for the split tests, the two reader tests on files, the note test and the capture test of the harness run, the redaction test (also with a value longer than the cut of a word), the one-line note for a choice among programs, the failed-gate test, the refused-confirmation test (the printing call removed) and the stale-file test (the guard removed), and each failed; the bench test of the label also failed with the printing call removed. The reasons-first test and the filter-and-cap test both fail on the function body that cut the reasons and the detail lines together (the first was run against that body in the tree, both were replayed against it outside the tree). The unit tests of the choice by identity and of the argv words and the test of the preloaded decision test new functions with no old counterpart and were not run against old code. Replayed on the 17 shim files of a real run with the test program's figures in the first file raised above the program's: a release binary built before this change, run on a copy with a fresh run, fails `type-histogram-diff` with `nothing grows strictly across all 17 captures` and `stack:Java_shop_codec_Native_open: holder absent from dump 1`; the current binary passes the gate on the same files. The failure in CI itself did not reproduce locally, so only this replay and the tests show that the choice is by identity now |
| A registration leak on Go could not be labelled `L-REGISTER` | corrected | A leaked `time.Ticker` before Go 1.23 lives in the runtime timer heap. A Go run has no timer-count column, no heap graph in command mode and no goroutine for it, so none of the three evidence alternatives of the `L-REGISTER` row could be met and `dla ledger label F --as L-REGISTER` was refused with `label-evidence-missing`, although the leak was confirmed with holder `registration-table` and the heap profile named the allocating line. The test that accepts a Go finding without a note on the known-leaks route appended a synthetic row that needs a retention path, so it did not show this | `data/archetypes.toml`: `L-REGISTER` accepts one more alternative, `growth-series` + `alloc-stack`, last in its `requires` list (`L-HANDLE` and `L-COLLECT` accept the same pair), so a label keeps the kinds of the first alternative that is met and existing labels do not change. The holder rule and the sibling rule (every `N-*` hypothesis that exists must be refuted) still apply. With archetypes off the leak stays unconfirmed, because its holder comes from the known-leaks row; the label step is not reached. `go-archetypes.md` says that a timer or ticker alone before Go 1.23 is the case of that known-leaks row. Tests: `ledger_m6::the_shipped_l_register_row_labels_a_go_finding_on_growth_series_and_alloc_stack` (shipped tables, evidence named explicitly, kinds `growth-series` and `alloc-stack`), `ledger_m6::growth_alone_does_not_label_a_go_finding_l_register`, `archetypes_data::l_register_ends_with_the_growth_and_alloc_stack_alternative` (pins the order); `the_shipped_go_ticker_row_makes_l_register_acceptable_on_a_go_finding_without_a_note` stays and its comment says it covers the known-leaks route only. The first and the third were seen failing with the table line reverted (the first with `label-evidence-missing`); the second passes both ways by design (it guards the refusal). `dla-bench dynamic --parallel 1` on a scratch copy of `go-ticker-legacy` with its partner, the six Go fixtures and the four fixtures that label `L-REGISTER` (`node-socket-heartbeat`, `node-socket-heartbeat-cleared`, `node-gateway-merge`, `python-ingest-two-leaks`): 11 of 11 pass, the label kinds of the Node and Python fixtures are the ones they had; the pair with archetypes off: the ticker leak is not confirmed, the partner passes. `dla-bench static` on and off: 60 of 60 pass. Not done: the known-leaks route counts a row for every Go finding whatever the probe lists, so on Go `L-REGISTER` is now accepted without a note on a heap finding with holder `static/global` or `registration-table` and a heap profile |
| A Go slice parameter kept un-copied while callers pass views of a larger buffer had no static rule (`go-header-index`, `go-feed-merge` L2 flagged by nothing) | corrected, static only | `dla/go-slice-view-retained` (archetype none, prior 0.3, row in `data/detectors/go.toml`) reports the store line of a slice parameter put into receiver or package state (`x.f[k] = p`, `x.f = append(x.f, p)`, or an insert call on a field whose argument is `p` or a literal holding it) when a call site passes a sub-slice, a `bytes.Cut`, `bytes.SplitN`, `bytes.Fields`, `bytes.Trim*` result, a `Scanner.Bytes()` window, or a struct field set from one (up to two calls away). It is quiet for `bytes.Clone`, `slices.Clone`, `append([]byte(nil), p...)`, `string(p)`, a copy into a fresh slice, a `string` parameter, a plain field overwrite, a slice local to the call, a map owned by the caller, a window of a package-level table, and a pool `Put` fed only by buffers from `Get` or `make`. Tests: `view_stored_in_a_container_call_is_flagged_at_the_store`, `copies_and_other_stores_are_quiet`, `a_store_needs_a_caller_that_passes_a_view`, `ring_slot_assignment_with_a_struct_field_view`, `a_pool_put_fed_by_its_own_buffers_is_quiet`, `conversions_and_static_tables_are_not_stores_of_a_view`, `calls_of_another_shape_do_not_count_as_the_feeding_call` and `scanner_windows_fire_and_other_shapes_a_real_project_writes_stay_quiet` in `dla_rules/go.rs`. Checked: the tests were run, and with the rule's call removed from the Go checks five of them failed (the quiet-shape tests cannot fail without the rule and were judged by reading); `dla-bench static` on copies of the three parked Go fixtures with no Go analyzer on PATH flags `go-header-index` at the store and `go-feed-merge` L2 at the ring store (both archetype modes); the candidates of the six Go fixtures of `bench/fixtures` are the same as before, and a hand-written project with six more shapes of each kind gave the expected hits and none | Static detection only: the dynamic stages still measure after the workload returned in command mode, so the rule's candidate is not confirmed by a run yet. A view whose owner does not outlive the stored entry is not told apart from one that does (prior 0.3 reflects both) |
| `go-slice-view-retained` paired a call with a method by name alone, so two types with a method of one name were mixed up (the call that feeds `Sessions.Remember` was quoted for `Safe.Remember`) | corrected | A call now feeds a method only when its receiver can be the method's receiver type. The type is read from the source where it is stated: a parameter or receiver of the enclosing function, `var x T`, `x := T{...}`, `&T{...}`, `new(T)`, a constructor `NewT(...)` whose declared result is in the project, a package variable, and a field of a known struct (`h.idx.Note(...)` with `idx *Index`, also through an embedded struct). A known type that differs drops the call; an interface value or a call result of unknown type keeps the name match and the message says the receiver's type was not resolved, and a call with a resolved receiver is preferred over such a one. A plain function is fed by a bare call in its own directory or through the qualifier of an import whose path ends in its directory; a bare call of a name the calling package does not define (a dot import) is paired by name and says the package was not resolved. Tests in `dla_rules/go.rs`: `two_types_with_one_method_name_are_not_mixed_up`, `a_field_of_the_receiver_is_resolved_through_its_declared_type`, `an_unresolved_receiver_keeps_the_name_match_and_says_so`, `a_function_of_the_same_name_in_another_package_is_not_mixed_up`; the eight earlier tests of the rule pass unchanged. Checked: the four new tests were run and, with the pairing check made to accept every call, all four failed; `dla-bench static` on copies of the parked Go fixtures and their partners (eleven fixtures, both archetype modes) passes with `go-feed-merge` L2 and `go-header-index` L1 still flagged by this rule, the six Go fixtures of `bench/fixtures` have the same candidates as before, and a sweep of the hand-written project that showed the defect now reports only `Sessions.Remember` and the scanner case | Receiver types are read from declarations in the source, not from a type checker: a type reached through a method result, a map or slice element, a closure or a generic type argument is not resolved and falls back to the name match (stated in the message). Two types of one name in different packages are not told apart, and the package of a bare call is its directory |
| A static hit could not name a holder, an owner or a region, so a rule candidate could not be bound or confirmed | corrected | `StaticHit` carries optional `holder`, `owner` and `region` (absent in the raw sweep file and in the schema of older runs; external backends write none), and the sweep copies them into the candidate. The Go rules `go-slice-view-retained` (`container-field` or `static/global`, region `heap`) and `go-chain-head-growth` set them. Tests: `holder_owner_and_region_are_optional_in_both_directions` in `static_hit.rs` and `a_dla_rule_hit_gives_the_candidate_its_holder_owner_and_region` in `tests/sweep.rs`. Checked: the sweep test was run and failed with the copy of the three fields removed from the sweep; the serialization test was judged by reading to need the new fields to compile; the finding written by a real `dla sweep --lang go` on a hand-written project shows holder, owner and region on the three view findings | The hit type gains three optional fields, so its schema file is regenerated; no hit written before the change needs to change |
| A Go field wrapped around its own previous value (`p.ctx = trace.Start(p.ctx, ...)`) was not reported (`go-trace-context` L1) | corrected, static only | Go joins the chain-growth family as `dla/go-chain-head-growth` (archetype none, prior 0.3): a field of the receiver or a package variable is reassigned from a call that takes the field's own value, its type can hold what it replaces (an interface such as `context.Context`, a project interface, a func, or a pointer to a self-referencing type), and nothing in the project resets it (`nil`, `context.Background()`) or steps it back (`p.ctx = p.ctx.Parent()`). It is quiet for a local, `context.Background()`, `max`, `append`, string, scalar and struct fields, an `error` wrapped around itself and a context assigned together with its cancel function. The census lists such a field as `container-field` (region `heap`) with the write as its writer. Tests: `go_field_wrapped_around_its_own_value` and `go_chain_is_quiet_for_locals_resets_and_other_types` in `dla_rules/chain.rs`, `a_field_wrapped_around_its_own_value_is_listed` in the Go census and `go_field_wrapped_from_a_call_is_a_writer` in `usage.rs`. Checked: the rule and census tests were run, and failed with the rule's call (one test) and with the census branch (one test) removed; the writer test passed before any change (the usage code already counted the write), so it pins existing behaviour; `dla-bench static` flags `go-trace-context` at the assignment by the rule and the census in both archetype modes | Static detection only: the heap series of a Go command-mode run is read after the workload returned, so `go-trace-context` does not confirm in a dynamic run yet. A wrap through an interface type that is neither `context.Context` nor declared in the project (an imported `http.Handler`) is not reported |
| A `gc.freeze()` call inside a function was not listed by the Python census (`python-frozen-gc`, static) | corrected | `gc.freeze()` moves every object the collector tracks, including cyclic garbage it has not reached yet, into the permanent generation, which no later collection looks at: called after every batch it keeps that garbage for good. The census now lists a `gc.freeze` call that has a function (or method, or async function) around it as kind `gc-freeze`, holder `cycle-only`, region heap. A module-level call (the documented use before a fork), `gc.collect()`, `gc.set_threshold()`, `gc.unfreeze()`, `gc.disable()` and a `freeze` method of another object are not listed, and the call does not depend on a function name. Test `gc_freeze_in_a_function_is_listed_and_other_gc_calls_are_not` in `lang/python.rs`. Checked: run with the new branch disabled it failed, run with it restored it passed. On a scratch copy of `python-frozen-gc`, `dla-bench static` flags `journeys/worker.py:25` by the census with archetype data present and removed; on `bench/fixtures` (60 fixtures, archetype data removed) no fixture except the `python-zstd-options-3145` decoy (next rows) changed its candidates or their origins, and `python-graph-churn`, which calls `gc.set_threshold`, gained none | The removers (`gc.unfreeze`) are not recorded: the tracked name `gc` finds no writers or removers in a project that only freezes. A freeze imported by name (`from gc import freeze`) is not matched by the census, only by the agent |
| The Python agent could not say which call site froze garbage, and its object counts left the frozen objects out | corrected | `harness/python/sitecustomize.py` wraps `gc.freeze` and `gc.unfreeze` (the real call and its errors reach the target unchanged; the bookkeeping is guarded and uses its own lock, never the dump lock), attributes the rise of `gc.get_freeze_count()` to the innermost project frame of the caller, and writes the group `py.gc_frozen` with each site's share of the current count (in proportion to what each site froze, so the shares sum to the count; frozen objects freed by reference counting leave the count, a cumulative sum would not). `gc.unfreeze()` sets the shares to zero and keeps the sites; nothing is written when `freeze` was never called; a limitation line says that `py.gc_objects` and the type counts exclude the permanent generation whenever the freeze count is above zero. Tests in `crates/dla-workload/tests/harness_capture_python.rs`: `a_freeze_in_a_loop_is_attributed_to_its_call_site_and_rises`, `the_shares_of_several_sites_sum_to_the_current_freeze_count`, `one_freeze_stays_flat_and_an_unfreeze_with_a_collection_empties_the_group`, `without_a_freeze_there_is_no_group_and_a_failing_freeze_reaches_the_target`. Checked: with the wrapper not installed three of the four failed; with the shares replaced by cumulative totals the sum test failed; the no-freeze test passes by design in both | Only the Python of this machine was run (3.13 for the tests and the fixture); 3.12 and 3.14 were not, nor an interpreter without `gc.freeze` (the wrapper is skipped there by reading). Checked: with the catalog line removed both failed, the second with `favors non-leak` and the column read `plateau`. `python-frozen-gc` copied to a scratch directory passes `dla-bench dynamic --parallel 1 --only python-frozen-gc` (a claim and a rule-out, label `UNCLASSIFIED`) | The bytes growth (`py.traced`) stays unattributed in the residual rows because the tied column counts objects |

| A user could not tell what the static stages left out, or bring it back | corrected | `dla probe` prints `left_out` (one line per directory with its file count, one line for the generated files with the markers seen, one line per runtime whose whole code base lies in left-out paths) and `left_out_note`; the report's `Scope:` line names `left out by default` and `brought back`; `dla run init --include <path>` (repeatable, relative to the project root, must exist) brings a path back for every static stage, and a vendored directory nested below an included path stays out | Tests: `include_brings_a_left_out_path_back`, `include_must_name_an_existing_path_of_the_project`, `a_project_made_of_a_vendor_tree_says_so_and_still_works`, `a_project_whose_root_is_named_vendor_is_analysed`, `included_paths_come_back_into_the_walk`, `one_included_file_does_not_bring_back_its_neighbours`, `scope_line_names_what_was_left_out_by_default_and_what_was_brought_back` |
| Which names and markers count as code the project did not write | decided | `external` and `extern` are also ordinary package names, so they are left out only when the directory is not a package of the project's own language (a Python package with `__init__.py`, a Go package of that name, a directory under a JVM source root). A marker counts only on a comment line in the first 20 lines and, apart from the tool names, only at the start of the comment, so prose that mentions `@generated` or "automatically generated" does not. Two things beyond the listed markers were added because a real project's generated C file says "The file is autogenerated from ..." and its header says nothing: the same sentence in other words, and a C or C++ header next to a generated source file of the same name. A prebuilt `.node` addon in a vendored directory stays visible to the boundary detection | Tests: `external_packages_of_the_project_are_kept`, `packages_that_happen_to_be_called_external_are_kept`, `markers_survive_real_file_headers_and_prose_does_not_trigger_them`, `names_that_only_look_like_vendored_ones_are_kept`, `gitmodules_in_the_shapes_git_writes_them`. | The capture series of Go heap profiles has the bytes allocated as its axis | corrected | `dla summarize alloc-stack-diff` on the Go heap backend put the profiles on the capture number, as if each were taken at the end of its point, but the in-run profile of a command-mode point is the last sample of its run, taken between about two thirds and the whole of the work. A linear leak then read with a last step half of the first (0.476 in the failing run of `go-ticker-legacy`, against a bar of 0.5) and nothing was confirmed. Measured on 6 kept capture sets of `go-ticker-legacy` and 4 of `go-header-index`: the half-split ratio of the leak's stack was 0.87 to 1.82 (`go-ticker-legacy`) and 0.81 to 1.18 (`go-header-index`) on the capture number, and 1.05 to 1.19 and 0.90 to 1.11 on the bytes allocated, the sum of `alloc_space` of each profile. With the change the axis of the series is that sum (`allocated-bytes`) when every profile has a positive total that increases in capture order, the gate compares rates per allocated byte, the stacks listed as growing in the stack evidence use the same positions, and a slope shown is bytes kept per byte allocated; the profiles are put in the order of those totals (each point is its own process, so the order they were taken in carries nothing) and a limitation line names the order when it differs; when a total is missing or two are equal the axis stays the capture number and a limitation line says why. Dynamic runs with the change: 3 runs of the 8 Go fixtures, 5 of `go-ticker-legacy` and 5 of `go-header-index`; all pass but one run under heavy host load. Tests: `a_linear_leak_profiled_at_different_shares_of_its_runs_passes_on_the_allocated_bytes_axis`, `without_alloc_totals_the_axis_is_the_capture_number_and_a_limitation_says_why` and `a_flat_stack_and_a_plateau_in_x_still_fail_the_gate` (the first and the last fail without the change), `unevenly_spaced_dumps_are_judged_by_their_rate_over_x`, `a_flat_series_and_a_plateau_in_x_still_fail_at_any_spacing` and `half_split_and_slopes_use_x_not_the_index` | A first version judged the growing stacks of the stack evidence on the capture number while the series used the new axis, so the evidence named no tie and the label step refused for lack of evidence in 2 of the first 7 runs; the stack evidence now reads the same positions (the first test above checks the tie). One run of `go-ticker-legacy` under heavy host load still failed: its sample of the 6-unit point was taken early in the run, so its total allocated (84.5 MB) was below that of the 4-unit point (95.9 MB), the totals did not increase in capture order, the axis fell back to the capture number and the stack's in-use bytes fell from the second point to the third. The profiles are now ordered by their totals, which reads that run's figures as a linear leak (test `captures_taken_out_of_work_order_are_put_in_the_order_of_the_bytes_allocated`, which fails without the ordering) |
| A candidate whose harness target was measured is closed `not_exercised` | corrected | `dla trend` now writes the verdicts of the declared targets (`finding`, `verdict`, `declared`) into the result of its growth evidence, and the ledger's rule for a measurement tied to the finding accepts a declared target with verdict `constant`, `changed` or `executed` for the finding or a member of its family; `not_executed`, `not_loaded`, `unknown` and a verdict of a candidate an automatic probe stood for do not count, and older records without the list stay readable. `dla ledger close-rest` takes the reason from that rule: a measured candidate becomes `unresolved` with `inconclusive` (also at the budget floor, and whatever static evidence it has), the others keep `not_exercised` or `time_budget`; its output and record count `inconclusive`, and the remedy for `unknown` no longer says the code ran. Tests: `a_declared_target_verdict_that_read_the_finding_makes_it_measured`, `a_verdict_of_a_candidate_an_automatic_probe_covered_is_not_a_measurement`, `a_verdict_of_a_sibling_in_the_family_counts_and_one_of_another_finding_does_not` (inconclusive.rs), `close_rest_says_inconclusive_for_a_measured_candidate_and_not_exercised_for_the_rest`, `at_the_budget_floor_a_measured_candidate_is_inconclusive_not_time_budget`, `verdicts_settle_candidates_and_close_rest_closes_the_rest` (queue.rs); each failed with its hunk removed | Verdicts of a series with no growth column at all are not stored in the ledger (no growth record is written for it), so such a target is not read as a measurement |
| A harness rerun without the dependency paths of its first run fails at import | corrected | `dla harness run` records the values of `PYTHONPATH`, `NODE_PATH`, `CLASSPATH`, `GOFLAGS`, `GOWORK`, `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, `DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH` and `VIRTUAL_ENV` it ran with in its run record (nothing else); a later `dla harness run`, `dla alloc-test` or `dla intervene` of the harness (an edited clone takes its source harness) sets the unset ones from the latest run for every process it starts, merging with the manifest value as for a set variable, prints `env_reused` and writes one line at the top of the process log; a variable set in the current environment wins. Tests: `a_rerun_without_the_variable_gets_the_value_of_the_last_run_and_says_so`, `alloc_test_reuses_the_environment_of_the_last_run_too` (env_reuse.rs), `only_listed_unset_variables_of_the_latest_run_come_back` and `an_edited_clone_takes_the_variables_of_the_harness_it_was_cloned_from` (dependency_env.rs); the first two failed with the reuse switched off | `dla intervene` shares the code path of `dla alloc-test` and was not run end to end with a reused variable |
| A site on the last line of a function was tied to no finding | corrected | The function lookup of `dla trend` asked tree-sitter for a range that ended past the end of the line, so a function whose last line was the site (a Python method ending on a statement, a Go closing brace, a one-line JavaScript or Java function, a trailing space or comment) was missed and its parent named. One helper now looks up the node at the first character of the line and both the candidate tie and the verdict lookup use it. Tests: `a_site_on_the_last_line_of_a_function_belongs_to_that_function`, `a_column_on_the_last_line_of_a_method_names_the_finding_written_in_it` (the first failed with the old range, also when it ended at the end of the line) and `the_function_around_the_last_line_is_found_with_a_trailing_comment_too` | A JavaScript arrow function is not a function kind of the lookup, so a site in one is tied through the function that contains it |
| A Go driver run records which functions its hooks reach | corrected | A Go driver run (`go test -run ^TestDLADriver$`) passed no coverage flags, so `executed-<S>.json` had no data, every target without a probe read `unknown` and a driver that reached none of its targets settled them after one run. Command mode instruments every point with `-coverprofile` and `-coverpkg=./...`; nothing there keeps the instrumentation from the measured points (they are instrumented). A driver run now keeps its measured process uninstrumented and runs the driver once more afterwards (one round of `DLA_STEP` units, no warm-up, no captures, no sampler) with `-coverprofile` in `cov/` and `-coverpkg` naming every module that `go list -m` gives (one pattern per module of a `go.work` workspace, `./...` when the go command cannot say), so the driver package need not be the package of the target; `go tool cover -func` no longer gets `GOFLAGS=-mod=mod` in a workspace, which Go refuses. Tests: `a_go_driver_run_records_the_functions_its_hooks_reach` and `a_go_driver_run_in_a_workspace_covers_every_module` (both fail without the change: the driver package and the target package differ, and in the workspace the target is in the other module); `go_driver_mode_records_carry_no_in_run_readings` still passes. On a copy of the Go RPC library (go 1.25, 8 targets, hook calling `RegisterLoggerBuilder` with a name derived from the key) the same go test command with `-coverpkg=google.golang.org/grpc/...` and one round wrote a profile in which `go tool cover -func` shows `RegisterLoggerBuilder` at 100.0% and `GetLoggerBuilder` at 0.0%, in 57 s with the dependencies fetched. Dynamic runs: `--only go-` twice, 8 of 8 each | The full chain on that copy (`dla harness run H1 --mode both`, `dla trend`, `dla run status`) was not run: it needs the run's consent, which only the user gives, so the `executed` and `not_executed` verdicts and the queue standing are shown by the unit tests of the verdicts and the queue and not on that tree. The bench's Go fixtures run in command mode, so no bench fixture exercises the driver coverage point. A module that needs a newer toolchain than the default one is built by the go command's own switch (the run and `go list -m` inherit the manifest environment) and was not tried |
| The in-run reading of a Go point stands at one share of the run | corrected | The reading was the lowest sample of the last third of the run, so each point stood at the share of its run where the geometric schedule happened to put a sample: 40 to 96 % over 72 points of 8 runs of `go-header-index` (mean 75 %, 3 points with no sample in the last third, a collection costing 39 ms at the median and 232 ms at most while other jobs held the load average between 8 and 34). A series over n whose points stand at different shares of their work is not linear: the in-run column read 20, 39, 28, 54, 86, 91, 128, 121, 129 MB in one run and failed the half-split gate there (ratio 0.47), the earlier failure had a last sample at 919 ms of a 2317 ms run, and 6 of 8 runs had a point below the one before it. The reading is now the floor envelope of the samples (the lowest reading at or after each) read at three quarters of the run time by interpolation, and where no sample ended after that time the floor of the last four samples is continued to it (slope not below zero, at most four times the last sample's time) with a limitation line; the record carries the times and readings of the last 48 samples. Ten runs of `go-header-index` with the change (load average 8 to 24 at their start): all pass, the in-run column passes the gate in all ten with half-split ratios 0.88 to 1.52 (8 runs before: 0.47 to 2.3, one failure), the points rise at every step in 8 of 10 runs (2 of 8 before), 6 of 90 points were continued. The flat fixtures `go-gc-tuned-batch`, `go-buffer-pool` and `go-ticker-modern` kept their in-run column failing the gate in every run. Test: `the_go_in_run_reading_stands_at_one_share_of_the_run` (a leak read within 1.5 % at last samples at 67, 87, 100 and 82 % of runs of 1, 4, 6 and 9 units, a flat heap and a transient left flat, the 919 ms of 2317 ms run continued; it fails with the former rule). `--only go-` ran 4 times with the change: 3 runs of 8 pass and one failed on `go-buffer-pool` | That failure (run under load average 18) is not the sampler: the in-run columns failed the gate as usual, but `os.vm_data`, `go.total` and `go.released` passed the first trend as a step (address space reserved under load), the alloc-test took them as its basis, read the workload inconclusive and the bench found no rule-out. One run was killed from outside (exit 143) and repeated. `go_command_mode_reads_state_owned_by_locals_while_the_workload_runs` failed once when four Go tests ran at once on a loaded machine (one sample only, at 42 % of a 263 ms run, which no rule can place) and passed in 4 of 4 alone |
| An entry-path harness shows whether a caller can make new keys reach a holder | corrected | A real run of the plugin on a Python HTTP library confirmed a cache that grew when the driver injected a new key per unit, and the blind verifier then called it a leak real use would not trigger: nothing asked who feeds the keys. `dla harness gen --lang <rt> --driver --entry F[,F2,...]` (at most 8 findings, each a leak-side finding that grew in some harness; refused with the fix as `entry-not-grown`, `too-many-targets` or `invalid-value` for no `--driver`, or `--probe` or `--next` with it) writes a hook driver like `--probe`, marks the manifest `purpose: entry-path` (a new optional field, schema regenerated), and writes the rules of an entry-path unit and, per finding, the callers chain and the key provenance above the first hook. The driver directory gets `VARIES.txt`; `dla harness run` refuses `varies-missing` while it is empty or the placeholder, and the run record stores the line. Tests: `the_trigger_is_reproduced_for_one_holder_and_not_found_for_the_other` (each refusal, the block, the refusal without a varies line, the run record), `with_the_queue_off_the_trigger_is_not_asked_and_the_report_says_so`, `an_entry_path_block_has_the_callers_the_key_and_the_rules_and_no_general_line`, `a_runtime_without_a_reader_of_calls_says_not_computed`, `varies_is_the_first_real_line_of_the_file`, `the_purpose_is_optional_and_named_entry_path`. Each of these failed with its hunk reverted: the growth check, the limit of 8, the `--probe` exclusion, the varies refusal, the stored line, the purpose, the callers block | A runtime other than Python, Go, Java, JavaScript and TypeScript prints `callers: not computed`; a Kotlin-only JVM project does too |
| Callers chains and the key provenance of a writer function | corrected | New syntax-tree reader of calls (Python, Go, Java, JavaScript, TypeScript): the callers of a function matched by name, up to three hops, at most five per hop, public ones first and marked; the parameter of the writer the stored key is built from; the expression each first-hop call site passes; the assignments that set a passed local name; the `if` conditions the call happens under; and, for a function nothing calls by name (a callback, a class a framework builds), the places that mention it. Tests: `python_chain_has_three_hops_public_first`, `go_chain_has_three_hops`, `java_chain_has_three_hops`, `javascript_chain_has_three_hops`, `typescript_arrow_functions_take_the_name_they_are_assigned_to`, `public_callers_come_first_and_a_hop_holds_five`, `a_function_is_listed_once_and_recursion_ends_the_chain`, `calls_match_by_name_alone`, `a_python_constructor_is_called_by_the_class_name`, `a_decorator_line_belongs_to_the_decorated_function`, `test_files_are_not_callers`, `the_key_comes_from_the_parameters_the_writing_statement_uses`, `a_keyword_argument_is_passed_for_its_own_parameter`, `a_key_that_is_no_parameter_has_none`, `a_passed_local_name_shows_the_assignments_that_set_it`, `a_function_nothing_calls_is_followed_by_where_it_is_passed`, `a_call_under_an_if_says_what_must_hold_for_it_to_happen`, `other_languages_have_no_chain`. Each failed with its hunk reverted (the public-first order, the cap of five, the third hop, the constructor name, keyword arguments, attribute names not counting as values, the origins, the references, the guards, the test-file exclusion) | Matching is by name only: two functions with one name are not told apart, and a call through a variable is seen only as a mention |
| The trigger of a finding is derived from the run directory | corrected | New pure function beside the queue code: `reproduced` when the finding has verdict `grew` in the trend of an entry-path harness or of a harness that runs the project's own workload (a fixed command or a JUnit repeat), the trigger text being the varies line or the workload id; `not_found` when two entry-path harnesses with different drivers targeted it, ran, were read with `dla trend`, and it grew in neither (a `repeated` series and an unread trend do not count; the same driver twice does not); `unchecked` otherwise. Shown in `dla run status` (a `triggers` object for confirmed and strong findings), in the rows of `dla ledger list`, and in the verdict output of `dla trend` for an entry-path series (`trigger` per target and the next command). Tests: `an_entry_path_harness_that_grew_it_reproduces_the_trigger`, `a_harness_that_runs_the_projects_own_workload_reproduces_it`, `growth_in_a_hook_driver_alone_is_unchecked`, `two_entry_path_harnesses_with_different_drivers_and_no_growth_find_no_trigger`, `the_same_driver_twice_does_not_count`, `a_run_whose_trend_was_not_read_does_not_count`, `a_repeated_series_says_nothing_about_new_keys`, `growth_in_a_later_entry_path_harness_reproduces_after_a_miss`, `an_entry_harness_not_run_yet_is_in_flight`, and the status, list and trend parts of `the_trigger_is_reproduced_for_one_holder_and_not_found_for_the_other`. Each of the first set failed with its condition reverted (entry-path, fixed command, distinct drivers, distinct harnesses, fresh series, read trend) | The trend of a finding is read from the latest report of each series; a verdict stored before this change has no entry-path purpose and reads as unchecked |
| A confirmed leak with no checked trigger is an item of the gate | corrected | A leak-side finding in state confirmed or strong whose trigger is unchecked and that grew in some harness gives one item of kind `trigger` in `dla run status`, in the final gate and in the stop hook, right after the queue items: its fix is the `--entry` command with every unchecked finding of the runtime, then fill, run, trend; after one entry-path harness without growth it asks for a second, different one; an entry-path harness generated and not run names its run. Not raised with `--queue off`, at or under the budget floor, or with a workload blocker recorded; `dla ledger confirm` is not blocked. Tests: `a_confirmed_leak_that_grew_under_a_hook_driver_is_asked_for_its_trigger`, `after_one_entry_path_harness_without_growth_the_fix_asks_for_a_second_one`, `after_two_different_entry_path_harnesses_without_growth_the_item_is_satisfied`, `an_entry_path_harness_that_grew_it_satisfies_the_item`, `with_the_queue_off_nothing_is_asked`, `at_the_floor_nothing_is_asked`, `a_workload_blocker_asks_nothing`, `a_refuted_or_unconfirmed_finding_is_not_asked`, and the status, gate and stop hook parts of `the_trigger_is_reproduced_for_one_holder_and_not_found_for_the_other`. Each failed with its rule reverted (the queue, the floor, the blocker, the growth requirement, the second harness fix, the in-flight fix, the position in the final gate, the item in status) | The benchmark's dynamic policy runs with the queue off, so its outcomes are unchanged |
| The report, the bundle and the verifier state the trigger | corrected | Each confirmed or strong leak has a line `Trigger: reproduced through the public API in H5 (varies: ...)`, `Trigger: not found: it grows when the harness passes new keys to <writer function>; two workloads through the public API did not grow it (H5: ...; H6: ...)` or `Trigger: not checked (<why>)`; the `Findings:` line ends with the counts by trigger; reproduced leaks are listed first; a view without a run directory says nothing. `dla bundle` of a finding with an entry-path run holds the driver (without the generated target block, scrubbed like every record), its `VARIES.txt` and the trend verdict under `entry-path/`; the verifier text says to judge the trigger from them and that growth under direct injection alone is no reason to call a finding a leak or a non-leak. Tests: `a_reproduced_trigger_names_the_harness_and_what_a_caller_varies`, `no_trigger_found_after_two_different_entry_path_harnesses_says_what_was_tried`, `reproduced_leaks_are_listed_first`, `a_trigger_that_was_not_checked_says_why`, `a_trigger_with_one_entry_path_harness_without_growth_says_what_is_missing`, `a_view_without_a_run_directory_says_nothing_about_triggers`, `the_generated_target_block_is_cut_out_of_a_driver`, and the report and bundle parts of `the_trigger_is_reproduced_for_one_holder_and_not_found_for_the_other`. Each failed with its hunk reverted (the counts, the line, the order, the not-found text, the unchecked reason, the bundle files, the cut block) | |
| The skill names leaks by their trigger | corrected | `SKILL.md` (checklist and Before you stop), `references/stages.md`, `recovery.md`, `evidence-and-report.md` and `agents/worker.md` teach the entry-path step after a target grew and was confirmed, the two new refusals, the item, and the wording of the final answer: a reproduced leak is a leak with its trigger, one without a trigger is an unbounded holder with no trigger found, never a leak and never disputed for that reason alone. The skill stays inside its limits: `the_skill_stays_inside_its_size_budget`, `five_rules_then_the_checklist_and_both_blocks_come_first`, `nothing_fits_and_before_you_stop_say_what_they_must` | |
| Real check: a Python HTTP library, the two top targets, then the entry-path step | corrected | A copy of the measured library with its runtime dependencies on the Python path: the first batch ranked the documented unbounded cache first and a class-level format cache second; direct drivers grew both and both were confirmed. Following only the generated block and the tool's next commands: the block named the writer function, its callers (one private hop, then public server-building functions through places that mention it), the parameters the key is built from, the expression passed, and, after a first attempt that did not grow because the application had no middleware, the line that the call happens only when the application has middlewares (this line, and the places that mention a function nothing calls, were missing from the first block and were added). The unit that sent client requests for paths that match no route to a server whose application had a middleware grew the first holder: its trigger reads reproduced, with the varies line. The second holder, keyed by a configuration string, stayed flat under two different entry-path drivers (one client, one raw socket with malformed and unsupported requests): not found. The report prints both lines and the counts | The units were written by the builder of the change, who knew the cause: that the block led there is shown only for what it printed |
| The order of candidates in the measurement queue is a score of named signals, not the tier | corrected | With the tiers alone the documented leak of a Go RPC library was rank 159 of 298 and that of a Java data-access library rank 325 of 479, so a run with time for two to four batches of 8 never measured either. `dla_core::rank::score` is the sum of the weights of one table (`WEIGHTS`, one name per signal) over signals of the finding and of the census facts on its annotation; the queue sorts the eligible set by descending score, then tier, then more than one origin, then ledger order. Tests: `rank::tests::every_signal_has_one_named_weight_and_the_score_is_their_sum`, `each_signal_moves_the_score_the_way_its_sign_says`, `a_static_keyed_cache_with_parameter_keys_outranks_a_rule_hit_on_a_register_only_field`, `an_outer_map_with_element_level_deletes_only_outranks_a_map_with_a_real_remover`, `a_package_variable_assigned_once_in_init_ranks_below_both`, `a_rule_hit_without_census_facts_keeps_a_neutral_score_and_old_annotations_still_count`, `a_writer_added_by_hand_next_to_census_sites_counts_as_an_unclassified_insert`, `test_support_example_and_demo_paths_are_demoted_and_look_alikes_are_not`; `queue::tests::the_order_inside_the_eligible_set_is_the_score_not_the_ledger`; `rank_score::ledger_list_prints_the_score_and_why_in_descending_order` | The weights are those of a research pass over four real trees and the 64 fixtures with three changes (`rule_dla` 15, `rule_strong` 45, `life_process` 20); none was fitted to the measurements below. `dla ledger list` prints `score` and `why` (the three largest contributions by name), the report's note describes the order in two sentences, and the one existing report test that asserted the old note's closing words (`ranking::static_only_findings_are_ordered_by_tier_then_ledger_order`) asserts the new wording instead. Test, example, mock, fake, stub, e2e, fixture, interop, sample, demo and bench path words are demoted whole-word (so a name such as `contest` or `sampler` is not) |
| The census reads each writer and remover site in the syntax tree | corrected | Writers and removers were name matches: a site in another file that only matched the name of a private holder counted, `x[k] = make(..)` and a constructor's `this.x = new T()` counted as removers, and nothing said what a site did. `dla-census` now reads every site of a Go, Java, Python, JavaScript or TypeScript holder in its syntax tree (function, parameters, initialiser, start-up reach through callers up to three calls up, public surface) and the statement with comments and string literals blanked (insert, reassign, reset or delete, depth 0 for the holder and 1 or more for an element, key from a parameter or a constant), and records per holder the container class, bound, lifetime, privacy, literal table, pool, key type, registration receiver and owner creation (`CensusEntry` and `Annotation` gain optional fields; older files and ledgers load and read as neutral). Tests: `sites_tests::go_element_writes_and_deletes_carry_their_depth`, `go_a_site_knows_its_function_its_initialiser_and_its_parameters`, `go_an_unexported_holder_is_not_written_from_another_package_by_name`, `go_another_struct_s_field_of_the_same_name_is_not_a_writer`, `go_a_pointer_that_is_only_assigned_as_a_whole_is_not_a_container_and_has_no_writers`, `go_bounds_literal_tables_pools_and_owner_creation`, `go_the_owner_type_is_made_per_call_or_only_at_start_up`, `java_private_holders_ignore_a_same_named_variable_in_another_file`, `java_a_constructor_assignment_is_initialisation_not_removal`, `java_weak_and_bounded_types_and_owner_creation`, `java_a_head_assigned_a_local_built_from_the_old_head_is_a_chain_that_grows`, `python_module_container_sites_and_bounds`, `python_init_writers_and_a_private_name_in_another_module`, `python_a_name_in_a_string_or_a_comment_is_not_a_site`, `javascript_sites_params_constants_and_module_level`, `registrations_say_where_their_receiver_comes_from`; `events::tests::element_writes_and_deletes_have_a_depth`, `a_made_container_stored_under_a_key_is_an_insert`, `a_value_built_from_the_holder_is_a_chain_and_a_value_read_from_it_is_not`; `usage::tests::an_element_assigned_a_made_container_is_a_writer_and_the_holder_reset_is_a_remover` | Rust, C and Kotlin keep the name matches and carry no facts. The statement is read as text (a port of the research reader) with the tree supplying everything around it, not as a tree walk of the expression. A holder that is not a container and is only ever assigned as a whole stays a candidate (heap-graph ties name it) but has no writers, so no tier takes it unless a rule names it; a field wrapped around its own value (`x = wrap(x)`, `latest = context` where `context` was built from `latest`) keeps its writer |
| Holders of one owner are measured in one batch | corrected | A driver that exercises a class reaches all of its state, but `--next` took the next 8 by rank, so one field of a class could be measured and its neighbours left for a batch that never came. `dla harness gen --next [N]` now declares the next N (at most 8) pending candidates by rank and then every other pending candidate of the same owner (same file and owner path up to the last segment, for fields of one struct or class; module-level and static holders only when they share a writer function), up to 16 in all; the added ones carry `sibling_of` in the summary, `harness.json` and the driver's target block, and the queue's next batch, `dla run status` and the queue item list the same batch. `--probe` keeps its limit of 8. Tests: `queue::tests::a_batch_brings_the_holders_of_one_owner_with_it`, `module_level_holders_are_siblings_only_through_a_shared_writer_function`, `a_batch_with_siblings_never_passes_sixteen`; `rank_score::next_brings_the_fields_of_one_struct_into_the_same_batch`, `next_with_a_count_takes_that_many_by_rank_and_the_siblings_of_them` | On a Go RPC library the first batch declares the health server's status map and brings its update map with it |
| The score puts the documented leaks of four real projects near the front | verified | With the tiers the documented site of a Python HTTP library, a Go RPC library, a Java data-access library and a JavaScript HTTP client was rank 1, 159 of 298, 325 of 479, and 29 and 30 of 38. With the score it is 1 of 68, 10 of 128, 23 of 384, and 23 and 24 of 31. Two hold-outs that did not choose the weights: an async Rust utility crate (the three candidates of its cancellation token are ranks 4, 5 and 6 before and after: the census gives Rust no facts) and a Java web server (its leak is a native handle that no candidate stands for in either run; the nearest candidate in the same package went from rank 333 to 377) | The Java and JavaScript sites are outside the target of 16 and no weight was changed to bring them in. The Java site is in a package-private class, so the public-surface signal that the research regex gave it (6 points) does not apply, and it sits in a group of 127-point ties. The JavaScript site registers on a local (`let req;` assigned in the function), which the census reads as -20 where the research regex read the receiver as outer (0). The first batch of each of the four trees declares its first 8 by rank and the siblings of their owners (a Go RPC library: the status map brings its update map) |
| The score keeps every true leak of the 64 fixtures within the first eight, and the acceptance runs are unchanged | verified | 38 of the 39 key sites are candidates. Within rank 1, 3 and 8 before the change: 21, 31 and 37; after: 15, 31 and 37. No site that was within 8 left it and none moved by more than 2 (eleven moved by 1 or 2, first places that fell behind a holder with a higher score). The candidate set of every fixture is the same before and after (283 findings; 168 eligible before, 160 after). `dla-bench static`: 64 of 64, archetypes on and off; the candidates that differ from the older baselines belong to analyzers installed after those baselines (eslint, ruff, pylint, rapx) and appear equally in a run of the binary without this change. `dla-bench dynamic --parallel 2` on twelve fixtures (three each of Python, Go, JVM and Node): all pass; confirmed, refuted, claims, leaks found and rule-outs equal those of the earlier run | The count of columns that pass the gate moves from run to run (resource columns on a loaded machine). Where a holder is a non-container that is only reassigned, or a ticker's channel is only read, the policy's `adds` and `remover` details now say "never removed" or name the declaration site instead of a name-matched line. Weights were not tuned on these fixtures |
| The `Queue:` line left out a targeted finding once it was confirmed | corrected | The header counted only findings still in the candidate universe, so two targeted findings that were confirmed fell out of both the total and the `grew` count. It now counts every leak-side, non-sibling, non-duplicate finding of an eligible tier in any state, with its best verdict; the rule is in the doc comment of the report facts. Test: `the_queue_line_counts_a_finding_that_left_the_candidate_state` (failed before) | A finding that left the candidate state before it was ever ranked cannot be told from one never ranked, and counts as not targeted if its tier is eligible |
| The `Residual:` header line was one clause per series with unrounded numbers | corrected | With more than three rows the header names the rows above their threshold (with the open item), counts the others ("N series below threshold") and points to a new section `Residual by series`, which lists every row; numbers use the report's own number formatter and a row names its series. Up to three rows print as before. Test: `many_residual_rows_are_bounded_in_the_header_and_complete_in_a_section` (failed before) | The threshold of 3 rows for the long form is a choice |
| `Coverage:` read `0 of 0 entry points exercised` when no entry point was listed | corrected | A runtime with no discovered entry point (`dla workload discover` did not run, or found none for a library) now reads "entry points not enumerated (none listed for this runtime)"; the counts are unchanged when entry points are listed. The harness already notes this as a limitation. Test: `a_run_with_no_listed_entry_points_does_not_read_as_zero_of_zero` (failed before) | The report cannot tell "discovery did not run" from "found none" |
| A verifier's site that is a writer of the holder counted as a site disagreement | corrected | `dla ledger verify` compares the verifier's site with the finding's site and, with the same three-line tolerance, with the writers and removers the ledger records for the finding; the bundle is unchanged. Test: `a_verifier_naming_a_writer_or_remover_of_the_holder_agrees_on_the_site` (failed before) | At the time "the same function" was not compared; it is now (see the row on a verifier's site in the same function as a recorded writer) |
| The verdicts of the declared targets of a flat series | verified | Reproduced with a driver harness whose unit touches no target: the flat series still has columns, so `dla trend` writes its growth record with the target verdicts, and `ledger list` and `close-rest` read them. Test: `a_flat_series_still_records_the_verdicts_of_its_declared_targets` (passes without a code change) | A series with no column at all writes no growth record; none was produced |
| The trigger outcome "no trigger found" rested on runs that did not measure the holder | corrected | `not_found` now needs two entry-path runs (different harnesses and driver digests, fresh keys, read) whose verdict for the finding is `constant`, a measured flat holder; two runs of which at least one has another verdict that is not `grew` give the new outcome `unmeasured`, reported as "Trigger: not checked: the entry-path harnesses H7 and H9 ran, but nothing measured the holder there (verdicts: executed, unknown)" and counted under "trigger not checked". The gate item is closed by it, and after one such run its fix says the harness ran but did not measure the finding and asks for a size reading and a rerun before a second harness. `run status`, `ledger list`, the output of `dla trend` and the bundle's verdict file use the outcome names and say an unmeasured run is no evidence either way; the verifier text says so too. Tests: `two_entry_path_harnesses_with_different_drivers_and_no_growth_find_no_trigger`, `runs_that_did_not_measure_the_holder_are_not_checked_whatever_the_verdict` (trigger_tests.rs), `after_one_entry_path_run_that_did_not_measure_the_holder_the_fix_says_what_is_missing` (trigger_items.rs), `two_entry_path_runs_that_did_not_measure_the_holder_are_not_checked_not_not_found` (report trigger tests); each failed with the old rule | Whether a Go holder gets a size reading is the harness builder's part; this only stops the report from claiming a flat holder |
| `dla ledger close-rest` took minutes on a ledger of several hundred findings | corrected | Each close ran the refusals of `ledger resolve`, and three of them loaded the whole run (ledger replay, series, trends, manifests) and recomputed the ranking and the queues for every finding. `close-rest` now loads the view, the ranking and the queues once and the refusals read them, with the same rules and refusals per finding (closing a candidate leaves every other finding's runtime, rank and pending standing as it was). Measured on a copy of the large real run (744 findings, 111 closed, debug build): 82.6 s before, 6.0 s after, with an identical ledger apart from timestamps. Test: `closing_hundreds_of_candidates_in_one_pass_equals_closing_them_one_by_one` (200 candidates, same states and reasons as one `ledger resolve` each); it is a regression test for equality and did not fail before, the timing is the evidence | No time bound is asserted (timing depends on the machine) |
| One `dla gap <S> --residual` per series | corrected | `dla gap --residual --all` writes the residual row of every non-gap series that has none, through the code of the single command (rows, evidence, open items), skips and names a series with no byte column, and prints one bounded summary (count written, series above their threshold with their open items). The final gate prints it in place of `dla gap <S> --residual` when more than three series lack a row; what counts as covered is unchanged. Tests: `all_writes_the_rows_one_by_one_would_and_says_so_in_one_summary` (coverage rows equal to those of four single calls), `a_run_with_no_series_writes_nothing`, `all_needs_residual_and_no_series` (gap_all.rs), `more_than_three_series_without_a_residual_row_name_the_one_command` (gate unit test; failed with the limit raised) | The agent still resolves the open items of the series above their threshold one by one |
| A Go `--next` batch is one package | corrected | A real run on a Go RPC library generated the driver of an 11-target batch of 7 packages in the package of the discovered workload (a `main` package): a Go test file reads the unexported names of its own package only, so the driver reached and probed almost none of its targets, and a target was settled by the two-drivers rule without its code having run. The batch of a Go queue is now the best-ranked pending candidate and the other pending candidates whose site is in its package directory (8 at most, then the same-owner siblings, 16 in all); the driver is written into that package directory and `harness.json` runs `go test ... ./<dir>`; `dla run status` (`queue.go.package`, the queue item) and the `--next` output (`summary.package`) name the package, and the target block says which package the driver is in. `--next --package <dir>` takes that directory (refused with the packages that have pending candidates when none is). A directory with no non-test Go file, or where `go list` fails or lists no buildable file, is named in `summary.next_note` and the next package by rank is taken; its candidates stay pending. Tests: `a_next_batch_is_the_candidates_of_one_package_and_the_driver_is_in_it`, `next_with_package_takes_the_candidates_of_that_package`, `a_package_that_cannot_hold_the_driver_is_skipped_and_its_candidates_stay_pending` (`go_batch.rs`; all failed with the old batch) | Other runtimes are unchanged (a Java driver likewise reads package-private state of its own package only; not changed). `go list` runs with `GOPROXY=off` in the package directory; when `go` cannot be run the package is not skipped |
| Go struct fields had no size probe | corrected | The output said a field holder "is not a package variable, so it is recorded as a target without a probe", and most real holders are fields. A `container-field` or `registry-field` target in the driver's package now gets a `go-field` probe in `probes.json` (package directory, type, field); the driver engine (identical in both Go templates) has `dlaTrack(v any)`, the model calls it with each long-lived object it builds, and at every checkpoint the engine sums `Len()` of the field over the tracked objects of the type by reflection (map, slice, array, chan, string; a pointer or interface followed one level; no `unsafe`). The probe column feeds the growth gate like a package variable: tracked fields of a synthetic server read `grew` in `dla trend`. A `sync.Map` field and a type with no length give no size and the target reads `not_loaded` with the reason; with nothing tracked for the type the record says "no tracked object of type T: call dlaTrack(<your T>) in setup" and the target reads `not_loaded`, never `executed`. The entry-path harness (`--entry`) carries the same probes. Tests: `tracked_fields_read_grew_an_untracked_one_says_so_and_alloc_test_does_not_read_a_plateau`, `an_entry_path_harness_carries_the_same_field_probes` (`go_field_probe.rs`; both failed without the probe derivation) | Held objects stay alive for the whole process (`dlaTrack` says so); the field is read by the type's name, not its package path, so two types of one name in the driver's package are summed together |
| A rising size probe is not a plateau (`dla alloc-test`) | corrected | A real case read `plateau` on `go.heap_live` for a `map[string]int32` that gained one entry per unit (a Go map grows in steps), and the finding was refuted; the finding had no probe. With a probe, `dla alloc-test --finding F` already took the finding's probe column as its deciding column in every runtime; now a size probe also leads the other columns tied to the finding, the output names the deciding column (`decided_by`, also in `alloc/A<n>.json`), and a Go field probe of the finding that was never read keeps the result from favouring non-leak (it is inconclusive, with the probe and the reason in the limitations). Flat probe: non-leak as before. Python and JVM probes already decided this way (their probe column is the deciding one), so the rule is the same in every runtime; only the unread-probe cap is Go-field only. Tests: `a_size_probe_of_the_finding_decides_before_the_columns_that_passed_the_trend` (failed before), `an_unread_size_probe_turns_non_leak_inconclusive_and_leaves_a_leak`, `a_go_field_probe_without_readings_bars_a_non_leak_verdict_and_says_why`, and the `alloc-test` steps of `tracked_fields_read_grew_an_untracked_one_says_so_and_alloc_test_does_not_read_a_plateau` | The synthetic project's heap does not plateau (the entries are heavier than the real case), so the unread-probe cap is checked on its function and on the note, not on a plateau read of the whole process |
| The Node agent and driver load in any package type | corrected | A run on a library whose package.json says `"type": "module"` died at once with `require is not defined in ES module scope`: `dla-preload.js` and `dla-harness.js` (CommonJS) sat inside the project's scratch copy, so Node loaded them as ES modules, and nothing was measured. | The files are written as `dla-preload.cjs` and `dla-harness.cjs` (CommonJS in any package; `NODE_OPTIONS=--require` and the driver's `DLA_AGENT` use the new names). A harness directory of an older version keeps its recorded names. The driver has `projectImport(file)` beside `projectRequire(file)`, and the target block says to use it when the package is `"type": "module"` or the workload file is `.mjs`. Tests: `harness_node_esm::es_module_project_in_command_mode_is_measured_by_probes_listeners_and_coverage`, `harness_node_esm::a_harness_with_the_old_js_agent_name_still_runs`, `harness_node_esm::commonjs_project_is_measured_unchanged`, `node_esm::driver_mode_in_an_es_module_package_measures_the_probes_and_the_listener_site`, `node_esm::command_mode_on_an_es_module_entry_file_measures_the_probes_and_the_listener_site`, `node_esm::driver_mode_in_a_commonjs_package_is_unchanged`, `gen::node_module_tests::a_module_package_is_found_from_the_workload_directory_upwards`. |
| Probes of module-level bindings of ES modules | corrected | The agent exposed top-level bindings only through `Module.prototype._compile`, which never runs for an ES module: every probe of one read `module has no probe accessor`. | With `module.registerHooks()` (Node 22.15+ and 23.5+) a `load` hook appends one statement after the last line of a probed ES module that hands the agent a closure reading the live bindings; no line moves, so the `file:line` of listener and timer registrations is unchanged, and `NODE_V8_COVERAGE` names the ES-module files by URL as before. On a Node without `registerHooks` the probe has no column and a limitation line names the versions needed. Tests: `harness_node_esm::es_module_project_in_command_mode_is_measured_by_probes_listeners_and_coverage` (module-level `Map` grows, bounded control and static `Map` stay constant, listener column names `src/bus.js:8`, coverage names `src/store.js::remember`), `harness_node_esm::es_module_probe_without_register_hooks_says_which_node_is_needed`. Not run: Windows and macOS (file URLs and path comparison are the platform-sensitive parts). |
| `dla trend` took 40 to 125 s per series on a ledger of 602 findings | corrected | A sampling profile (debugger stack samples of the running command) put the time in the owner lookup of the census join: for every column and finding it normalized the owner of every census entry again, which is quadratic in entries times lookups. The resolver now computes the canonical owner of each entry once and each lookup compares strings, with the same exact-then-unique-suffix rule. Measured on a copy of the run directory (debug build): `dla trend` of four series 38.9, 5.5, 125 and 124 s before and 0.7, 0.7, 1.6 and 1.4 s after; the stdout and every file of the run directory (the ledger without timestamps) are identical for those four series, only the ledger seal differs because it hashes the timestamps. Every other command of the measurement loop was under 5 s before (the longest, `dla harness gen --next`, is the build of the agent and the copy of the project, in external tools). Tests: `the_cached_lookup_finds_what_normalizing_every_owner_each_time_finds` pins the result against the old per-entry normalization; no time bound is asserted | The timings come from one machine shared with other work; the profile is by stack sampling, not a profiler |
| A target a batch missed gets a harness of its own | corrected | In two real runs the documented leak was a declared target of two batches of 9 to 16 targets; the model wrote code for two or three targets per batch, the others read `not_loaded` or `not_executed`, and the two-drivers rule settled them as not reached after the second batch without an attempt. Now a hook-driver run MISSES a target when its verdict is `constant`, `not_loaded`, `not_executed` or `unknown`; a missed candidate stays pending (MISSED, as opposed to FRESH); it is settled as not reached (`settled_by: not_reached`, was `two_drivers`) only when two different drivers missed it and one of the harnesses declared at most 3 targets (focused). `--next`: when the best-ranked pending candidate was missed the batch is a focus batch (it and up to two more missed ones: same owner, then same file, then next by rank, in one package for Go; no siblings, no fresh candidates; `--next N` is capped at 3 and the output says so); otherwise the broad batch holds fresh candidates only. The harness records `purpose: focus` and `focus.json` (the earlier harness and trend reason per target); the driver block says "Focus harness: ..." with the writer functions, a broad block one line about the small harness to come; `dla trend` names the missed targets first in `next`; `run status`, `ledger list`, `close-rest`, the refusal (`target-pending` now names the focus batch for a missed candidate; it still fires only for `not_exercised`, `no_harness`, `time_budget`, and `env_unavailable`, `tool_missing`, `build_fails`, `privilege_declined` are accepted as before) and the report's `Queue:` and `Targeted:` lines follow ("not reached" is only what the rule settled; the rest is "missed once, no focus harness"). Entry-path harnesses, JUnit repeats and command mode keep their rules. Tests: `two_big_batches_that_missed_a_target_leave_it_pending_and_missed`, `a_focus_batch_is_chosen_when_the_best_ranked_pending_candidate_was_missed`, `the_focus_batch_prefers_the_same_owner_then_the_same_file_then_the_rank`, `a_broad_batch_holds_fresh_candidates_only`, `one_focused_miss_after_a_broad_miss_settles_and_the_same_driver_twice_does_not`, `an_entry_path_miss_is_not_a_miss_of_the_queue_and_a_reached_target_is_never_missed` (`queue.rs`), `a_missed_target_gets_a_focus_harness_and_is_given_up_only_by_the_rule` (`focus.rs`), `a_focus_batch_stays_inside_one_package` (`go_batch.rs`); the first three failed with the old rule. Changed existing tests: `the_queue_line_counts_a_finding_that_left_the_candidate_state` (a miss with no focus harness reads "missed once, no focus harness", not "not reached"), `a_batch_with_siblings_never_passes_sixteen` (`batch_for` returns a `Batch`), `verdicts_settle_candidates_and_close_rest_closes_the_rest` (the `Targeted:` line names the rule) | `unknown` is a miss (it settles nothing, as before) |
| `dla census --lang go` took two minutes of CPU on a repository of 1 083 Go files | corrected | Stack samples and phase timings put the time in the writer and remover search (every holder scanned every line of every file with up to seven regexes, all compiled for every holder name) and in unoptimized regex and parser code. The search now reads an index of the words of each line (a line without the holder's name cannot match, a non-ASCII line is always read, a name that is not a plain word is searched on every line), compiles each regex when a line first needs it and runs it only on a line that holds the literal the pattern requires; a source parsed again gets the tree of its first parse (a cache of at most 32 MB of source per thread); and the regex and tree-sitter crates are optimized in the development profile. CPU on the Go tree: 134 s before, 15 s after; Python 26 to 2.6 s, JVM 115 to 16 s, Node 3.9 to 0.6 s, Rust 3.3 to 0.5 s.
