The census JSON and the ledger without timestamps are identical before and after for all five trees, and the ledger of three sweeps is too. Tests: `the_word_index_finds_what_a_scan_of_every_line_finds` (usage), `a_source_parsed_again_gets_the_same_tree_and_a_changed_source_its_own` (parse); no time bound | The sweep of the JVM tree still takes 38 s of CPU, which is the external analyzers it starts |
| The stop hook stamped a run incomplete while a long `dla` command was still running | corrected | The commands that can take long (census, sweep, harness run, trend, alloc-test, report, gap, intervene) register themselves in the run state for their lifetime as an attached process of the new kind `command` (never killed by an abort) and mark themselves stopped when they end. The stop hook blocks the stop while a registered command is alive (the process exists and is the one recorded), names it and its start, says to wait, and neither counts the attempt nor stamps; a record whose process is gone, or whose command has run for more than 30 minutes, is ignored. Tests: `a_running_dla_command_blocks_the_stop_without_counting_or_stamping`, `a_registered_command_whose_process_is_gone_counts_as_not_running`, `a_command_running_past_the_limit_is_taken_for_hung`, `a_long_command_registers_itself_while_it_runs_and_marks_itself_stopped` (gate hooks); the first failed with the check removed | Only the commands of the list are registered |
| A stamped run that went on had its queue closed and its candidates closed with the reason `time_budget` or `not_exercised` | corrected | A stop-hook stamp does not close the measurement queue: the queue and the budget rule read the time left as for a going run, and `over` is only for an aborted run or one a merge made incomplete. `dla run status` on a stamped run shows the same queue and next command as a going run and a `stop_stamp` field. A command that works on the run (every command except the status and the gate, the report, the bundle, `ledger list` and the commands that handle the run itself) lifts the stamp before it does its work, whether or not it then writes to the ledger; the state keeps the stamp and the time it was lifted, and the report says so in a `History:` line, not in its status. `close-rest` on a stamped run therefore lifts it and leaves what a queue holds back pending; at the floor it closes with `time_budget`; an aborted run stays aborted and over. Tests: `a_stamped_run_has_its_stamp_lifted_by_the_next_working_command_and_keeps_it_in_its_history`, `an_aborted_run_stays_aborted_whatever_runs_after` (gate hooks), `a_stamped_run_with_time_left_keeps_its_queue_and_close_rest_lifts_the_stamp`, `a_stamped_run_at_the_floor_closes_with_time_budget`, `an_aborted_run_keeps_its_queue_over_and_nothing_lifts_it` (queue), `floor_is_the_reason_only_when_the_time_left_is_at_the_floor` (unit), `a_stamp_the_run_went_on_from_stays_in_its_history_not_its_status` (report); the queue, the lift and the unit tests failed with their part reversed | The refusal of a manual `ledger resolve --reason time_budget` follows the same time rule now |
| An `auto` budget stayed unsettled in a run that never passed a gate check | corrected | Every command that works on the run (not the hooks, `run init`, `run abort`, consent, schema and install) sizes an `auto` budget before it runs once sweep and census have run for a runtime, with the formula and the state change of the first passing gate check; `dla gate check` sizes it itself whether it passes or not and prints the budget line. Tests: `nothing_settles_before_sweep_and_census_have_run`, `each_command_that_needs_the_budget_settles_it_once_after_sweep_and_census` (run status, close-rest, harness gen, gate check; formula, once); the second failed with the settling removed | A worker's share keeps its own rule |
| A series with too few points is not a quiet series (`dla trend`) | corrected | A real run measured the documented leak (`node.listeners@<site>` +1 per unit, `node.heap_used` +15 KB per unit) in series of 4 to 7 points, and `dla trend` printed `passed: []` and settled the targets whose code ran. | Columns that fail the gate only for their own point count (`n < 8`: a short series, or a column that entered the histogram late) are listed in `short` (at most 12, site columns first) and `next[0]` names the strongest three with slope per iteration and findings, and the command that measures long enough (`--iterations` = measured points + what the column lacks). A declared target whose verdict would be `executed` is `unknown` ("series too short") on a short series. Tests: `trend::short::tests::*`, `trend::verdicts::tests::a_short_series_never_settles_a_target_as_executed`, `node_short::a_short_run_speaks_and_the_full_run_ties_the_site_column_to_its_finding`. Not changed: the gate itself, and how nulls are counted. |
| The Node driver ends by itself and fits its points into the time limit | corrected | A driver whose setup left an HTTP server open kept the process alive until the kill (no coverage data); a unit of two requests with 440 units per point did not fit 300 s (8 of 9 points). | `dla-harness.cjs` calls `process.exit` after the last checkpoint and the flush; it lowers the step of the rounds left (floor 10 units, limit from `DLA_TIME_LIMIT_SECS`, which `dla harness run` sets for every driver) and names it in a limitation. Tests: `node_short::a_driver_with_an_open_server_ends_by_itself_and_writes_coverage`, `node_short::a_slow_unit_lowers_the_step_and_all_points_arrive`. Python, Go, JVM and Rust drivers have the same fixed loop and are not changed. |
| A `MaxListenersExceededWarning` carries the registration site and `dla harness run` and `dla trend` print it first | corrected | The detector hit of a real run had `site: null` and was never read. | The agent records the sites that hold the listeners of the warned event (`site`, `sites`); `dla harness run` prints an `_attention` array (first key) with the hit, a short or cut series, a lowered step and missing coverage data; `dla trend` puts the hit in `next` with the finding at the site and the command that targets or adds it. Tests: `harness::run::tests::the_attention_lines_*`, `node_short::a_short_run_speaks_*`. |
| A registration on an object handed in from outside is a candidate | corrected | A census registration whose own call is the only insert (a listener on a callback parameter) had `w_none`, tier 5 and was never ranked. | `rank.rs`: such a finding is its own writer (no `w_none`, not tier 5); the receiver weights are unchanged (`outer` ranks above `local`); the driver's to-do line for an outer receiver says the units must share the object. Tests: `rank::tests::a_registration_on_an_outer_object_*`, `node_short::a_registration_on_an_object_from_outside_is_eligible`. |
| A residual names the capture that attributes it | corrected | The open item offered only a hypothesis or `unresolved`; 30 rows were closed that way. | The open item and `next` of `dla gap --residual` name `dla harness run <H> --mode <m> --capture <kinds>` for node and jvm harnesses (text only, no gate rule). Test: `gap::residual::tests::a_residual_names_the_capture_that_can_attribute_it`. |
| `go.released` passed the first trend as a stair and made the unbound alloc-test inconclusive (`go-buffer-pool`, CI run: "no rule-out on a non-leak fixture"; the same in a local run of `go-gc-tuned-batch`) | corrected | `go.released` is `/memory/classes/heap/released:bytes`: heap the runtime handed back to the system. It rises when the scavenger or `debug.FreeOSMemory()` returns idle memory and falls when the runtime reuses it; a leak keeps memory in use, so a rise of it is the opposite of held memory. On a flat Go run it swings by tens of megabytes (a local run, in order: 27.4, 23.5, 22.7, 23.1, 12.5, 19.4, 23.1, 20.8 MB). Chance alone passes the gate: a simulation of 200000 series of 8 independent readings passes it in 0.96 % of them (the gate needs `p < 0.05` over 8 points, a Sen interval above zero, no decaying half and a rise over 3 noise units), and `go.released` passed 1 of 45 trends of a local Go sweep. In the CI log the column read slope 1737.697 and noise 1018194 over 8 points at x spacing 440 (read from a local run of the fixture; the extra window of E12 spans 3080 = 7 x 440, and its main slope equals the trend slope): rise 5.35 MB, 5.26 noise units, so it passed the gate by the documented rule (the log shows a slope per x unit next to a noise in bytes; the rise is not the size of the noise). The unbound alloc-test then voted by it. E12: the extra rise -4.004 MB is below the 3 noise units limit and below the 32 KiB minimum, so the plateau test read plateau; the purge read `retained`, because `FreeOSMemory` moves bytes into `go.released` (after above before: a negative drop) so no purge can ever release it; plateau with a retained purge is the contradiction rule and reads inconclusive. E13: the extra window rose 5.625 MB (more than 3 noise units, slope above half of the main slope): growth continues, leak; os.rss and os.vm_hwm non-leak; a leak vote beside non-leak votes is inconclusive | `series::not_a_growth_signal` names `go.released`; `dla trend` (`trend/analysis.rs`) and the trend the alloc-test computes (`alloc_cmd::trend_of`) fail its gate with the reason `memory returned to the OS` whatever its shape; the column is still recorded, analysed and shown, and the gap split still reads it. No finding is raised on it and it is no basis of the alloc-test. Test: `trend_cmd::analysis::tests::memory_returned_to_the_os_is_analysed_but_never_passes_as_growth` (a stair that passes as `go.total`; seen failing with the exemption turned off). Local runs: `dla-bench dynamic --only go-buffer-pool`, 10 runs as the machine was (load 10 to 33 from other work) and 10 with two extra busy loops, none failed before; 10 runs with the new binary, none failed. The failure did not recur locally, so the loops show no change in rate; the proof is the rule and the test. `--only go-` before and after: all 8 pass; confirmed 4/4 equal, leaks found equal, rule-outs equal. `go.released` passed once in the before sweep (`go-subscription-workers`, a leak fixture: stair, rise 24.1 MB, noise 7.1 MB) and the leak is found without it. Judged by reading: the rule is the meaning of the column, the same for every Go program |
| A retained purge made a plateau of `go.total` inconclusive (a local run of a Go fixture: `go.total inconclusive (plateau, purge retained)`, extra slope 0, noise 1.54 MB) | corrected | `go.total` is `/memory/classes/total:bytes`, everything the runtime has mapped (released pages included); it only steps up, so no purge returns bytes of it: any growth over 32 KiB since the warm-up reads `retained`, and the contradiction rule then turns every plateau of it into inconclusive. `os.vm_hwm` (a peak), `native.peak_bytes` and `os.vm_data` (reservations) are the same, and are listed in `pick::NO_RELEASE` for the same reason | `pick::purge_cannot_release` (those four names): a retained purge reading of them never contradicts a plateau, as for `os.rss_file`; the reading stays in the result and a continuing plateau still reads leak. Tests: `alloc_cmd::tests::a_retained_purge_does_not_contradict_the_plateau_of_a_column_no_purge_can_release` (`go.total`, `os.vm_data`, `os.vm_hwm`; seen failing with the function returning false) and `alloc_cmd::tests::growth_that_continues_in_a_column_no_purge_can_release_still_reads_leak`; the existing `a_retained_purge_still_contradicts_the_plateau_of_an_ordinary_column` is unchanged. Not shown by a loop: `go.total` passed no trend of 69 in the local `go-buffer-pool` runs (it passed 18 of 45 in the Go sweep, all in the three leak fixtures `go-header-index`, `go-ticker-legacy` and `go-subscription-workers`). One consequence read from `judge::overall`: a Go run whose `go.heap_live` leaks while `go.total` plateaus now reads inconclusive instead of leak (a non-leak vote beside a leak vote); no fixture showed it (`--only go-`: leaks found equal before and after) |
| Whole-process `os.rss` and `os.rss_anon` that pass the first trend on a JVM warm-up read inconclusive by the size of one trim (`jvm-pool-warmup`, CI: stairs slope 6913 and 7044, noise 9.0 and 8.8 MB, `os.vm_hwm` too) | unverified | Local run `base-load-jvm-pool-warmup-5` has the same first trend (`os.rss` stair, slope 6571, noise 3.04 MB, rise 51 MB; `os.rss_anon`, `os.vm_hwm` too; p 0.00087). Its first unbound alloc-test read non-leak (both plateau; `trim-native-heap` returned 5.1 of 20.4 MB, 25 %: partial), its second inconclusive (plateau; 3.7 of 26.3 MB, 14 %: retained). The ratio crosses the 20 % cut by chance: what a native trim returns of a JVM does not depend on whether the growth is a warm-up or a leak (the growth is mostly the committed Java heap). In 4 other local runs `os.rss` did not pass, and reported alone it read inconclusive (plateau, retained, 36 to 43 MB of growth, 3 to 5 MB returned) every time | none. Letting a stair that levels off in the extra rounds stand against a retained purge would also refute stair-shaped leaks in whole-process columns that today read inconclusive; no leak fixture was run to measure that. Open: decide with a loop of the leak fixtures that have a whole-process stair (`jvm-extension-reload`, `jvm-invoice-render`, node and Python ones) listing the plateau and purge readings of `os.rss` before any such rule |
| A retained purge made every plateau of a whole-process column inconclusive (the JVM flake above; exemptions had grown to `os.vm_hwm`, `native.peak_bytes`, `os.vm_data`, `os.rss_file`, `go.total`, `go.released`) | corrected | A purge that kept the growth says the growth is not allocator or collector headroom; it does not say it continues. What the old unconditional rule guarded is a slow stair that rose once or twice and shows no step in a short plateau window. Logs (156 plateau-with-partial/retained column readings of the earlier sweeps and loops, listed in `u43/report.md`): no leak fixture's outcome depended on such a vote (every such column in a leak fixture was a non-voting, reported one); the voting ones were all in non-leak fixtures (`jvm-pool-warmup` os.rss and os.rss_anon with 2 to 4 main-window rises, `go-buffer-pool`, `go-subscription-workers-closed`, `jvm-invoice-render-trimmed`, `python-report-memo-bounded` with 0 to 2) | `judge::combine` takes the expected rises: main-window rises (points that set a new maximum over the noise unit of the plateau test, from the end of the warm-up) x extra span / main span; plateau + retained stands (non-leak, N-WARM and N-HWM) at `judge::MIN_EXPECTED_RISES` (3) or more and the column result's `note` says the growth is held; below 3, or with no count, it stays inconclusive, and the `next` line says how many extra points would reach 3 (the doubled `--extra` converges). Counters keep their rule. The exemptions stay: a peak, a size or a mapped total cannot be lowered, so their retained reading carries no information whatever the count says (redundant: none). Tests: `judge::tests::the_rises_of_a_window_are_the_new_maxima_over_the_noise`, `judge::tests::a_retained_purge_holds_a_plateau_back_only_while_its_window_is_short`, `alloc_cmd::tests::a_ramp_then_flat_with_a_retained_purge_reads_non_leak_and_says_the_growth_is_held`, `a_stair_that_rose_twice_reads_inconclusive_until_the_extra_window_doubles`, `continuing_growth_with_a_retained_purge_still_reads_leak`; the rule mutated back to the unconditional contradiction failed three of them, the rise count mutated to zero failed three. Note: a stair with one rise needs 16 extra points of the default spacing, so the bench's single mechanical alloc-test still reads inconclusive for a fixture whose main window rose twice or less |
| The step is fitted inside the first round, in every driver template | corrected | The Node driver looked at the clock only after a whole round: a unit of one second made round 1 (440 units) outlast the 300 s limit before the first check. | Node, Python, Go, JVM and Rust drivers read `DLA_TIME_LIMIT_SECS` and, once 10 units of a round have run and after every round, lower the step of the round in progress and of the rounds left (floor 10, margin 15 percent and 5 s), noting it as a limitation that `dla harness run` prints as `DRIVER STEP`. Differences: Python and the JVM end the process explicitly after the last checkpoint (`os._exit`, `System.exit`; Go ends with the test and Rust when `main` returns); the time per unit is measured from the start of the loop in all five, the budget from the process start (`process.uptime`, a monotonic clock at import, `dlaStart`, `Instant` at start, JVM uptime). Tests: `node_short::a_unit_so_slow_that_one_round_exceeds_the_limit_*`, `driver_fit::the_{python,go,jvm,rust}_driver_is_fitted_inside_its_first_round*` (300 units of 100 ms against a 16 s limit: 5 of 5 points, first round cut). Not run through `dla harness run`: the four non-Node templates were run standalone with their hooks filled. |
| A probe of a plain object read a constant 1 while the object grew (ES modules and CommonJS) | corrected | In a real run a module-level `const deprecatedWarnings = {}` that gained a key per unit read `stayed 1`: the probe read the right object, but the walk counts objects and every value was `true`, and `size` was only set for Map, Set and arrays. | `sizeOf` gives a plain object (or null-prototype object) its key count: column `<name>.size` rises one per unit. Test: `node_esm::a_module_level_object_table_and_a_map_rise_one_per_unit_in_driver_mode` (failed before the fix). |
| Residual capture advice covers every runtime | corrected | Python and Go harnesses have `--capture` kinds, but the advice named none for them. | `capture_kinds` rows: node alloc,heap / nonheap; jvm histogram,jfr / nonheap; go alloc / nonheap; python alloc,histogram / nonheap; rust and native none (`--tool`). Test: `gap::residual::tests::a_residual_names_the_capture_that_can_attribute_it` checks every row and that the runtime's agent writes each kind. |
| One flat run of one arbitrary unit does not end a run that has budget left | corrected | A real run on a Rust async utility crate ended after 78 seconds with 76 of 77 budget minutes unused: one driver whose unit touched all 7 candidates once read every target `executed`, the queue emptied and `close-rest` closed them; the leak needs a grandchild token cancelled through its parent, which the unit never built. Now, once no first-pass candidate is pending, the run is going and budget is over the floor, the candidates one hook driver read `executed` (nothing grew, nothing missed) get a second look: a focus harness of at most 3 targets (`purpose: second-look`, best-ranked first, at most `SECOND_LOOK_MAX` = 3 such harnesses per runtime, one look per candidate). `queue.pending`, `run status` (`queue.<rt>.second_look`), `ledger list` (`queue: pending`, `second_look`), `close-rest` (leaves them, names the batch command) and the final gate item all say so; the report's Queue line ends "second look N (grew M)". The block lists what the first driver did not vary (other orders, derived-from-derived three deep, cancelling or failing parent first, overlapping lifetimes, a long-lived object next to a fresh one) and the earlier harness; `dla harness run` refuses a second look with fewer `VARIES.txt` lines than targets (`varies-missing`, same file and wording as an entry-path harness) and one whose hook code (comments and whitespace aside) equals that of an earlier harness of the same targets (`invalid-value`). A candidate is settled for good by flat readings of two different drivers, the second a focused harness. At the floor nothing is asked (`time_budget` as before); with `--queue off` nothing changes, so the bench's fixed policy is unaffected. Tests: `one_flat_run_leaves_the_candidates_for_a_second_look_when_the_first_pass_is_over`, `a_second_look_ends_and_settles_after_a_different_driver`, `the_second_look_is_bounded_and_skips_grew_missed_and_floor` (`queue.rs`), `one_flat_run_gets_a_second_look_with_a_different_driver_while_budget_lasts` (`second_look.rs`); each failed with the second look off | Constant readings stay under the miss rule; the same-driver check compares code, not behaviour |
| A measured growth could be closed unmeasured (a workload blocker with harnesses run, a finding a passed column points at closed `inconclusive`, a printed `ledger test` refused as printed) | corrected | `intake.workload` as `unresolved` with a blocker reason is refused (`workload-ran`) when a harness ran and wrote a series for every runtime that has candidates. A finding a passed growth column points at (the tie, or the first candidate of a passed site or probe column) or that a trend gave `grew`, with no alloc-test evidence bound to its family, is left by `close-rest` with the full commands in order (sibling, `ledger test --evidence`, `alloc-test`), refused by `ledger resolve` as `unresolved` or `suspected_static_only` (`growth-not-measured`; `time_budget` at the floor and an aborted run keep their own rules), and listed by the final gate (a `finding` item with those commands, or a new `growth` item for one already closed); a detector hit with a site and no measurement verdict is listed too, and the DETECTOR HIT line of `dla trend` prints the direct steps when the column at the site passed. After the alloc-test evidence the existing rules decide. On a copy of the real axios run the printed steps ran for real: alloc-test favored leak, the sibling was refuted and the documented finding confirmed, and its item went. Tests: `a_workload_blocker_is_refused_while_a_harness_ran_for_every_runtime_with_candidates`, `a_workload_blocker_is_accepted_when_no_harness_ran`, `the_chain_from_a_passed_column_to_an_alloc_test_cannot_be_walked_around`, `a_detector_hit_that_nothing_measured_is_listed_by_the_final_gate_until_it_is`, `the_detector_line_of_dla_trend_prints_the_direct_steps_when_the_column_at_the_site_passed`, `an_aborted_run_is_not_held_to_it_and_the_floor_keeps_its_own_rule` (grown_chain.rs); `verdicts_settle_candidates_and_close_rest_closes_the_rest` now decides the grown finding by an alloc-test evidence; each failed with its part reversed | The chain's confirm step is shown on the real run, not in the synthetic test; a worker's slice is not held to the rule |
| A second look takes its sequences from the key | corrected | In a real run the second-look unit built a three-level token chain and cancelled it in one fixed sequence for every unit, so one case ran 3520 times and the leak, another order of the same operations, never showed. Every driver template (Python, Node, Go, JVM, Rust, C) now has a small helper that takes independent choices from the key: `dla_choice(key, slot, n)` (Python, C), `dlaChoice` (Node, Go), `choice` (JVM, Rust). Choice `slot` of `n` options is the digit of the key in mixed radix (the divisor is the product of the `n` of the lower slots, kept in an 8-entry array: no allocation), so every combination of the slots occurs within n0*n1*... units and repeats evenly; the driver notes at its last checkpoint "the units took N combinations of K choices (of P possible)", which `dla harness run` prints (`summary.choices`) and `dla trend` carries (`choices`). The second-look block says to write the unit as choices and gives the recipe (depth of derivation, which object gets the ending call, when intermediates are released, the order of releases, success or failure) and why; the first-pass block gets one sentence about the helper. `dla harness run` refuses a second look whose driver code holds fewer than two calls of the helper (non-comment lines that are not a definition, name followed by `(` and not part of a longer name; `invalid-value` with the recipe as the fix). Tests: one per template (`the_<lang>_choice_helper_walks_every_combination_of_two_slots_in_n1_times_n2_keys`, run with the language's own toolchain; the Python and Go ones failed with a helper that ignores the slot), `a_choice_call_is_counted_outside_comments_and_definitions_only`, and `second_look.rs` (a unit without the helper is refused with the fix line, with two choices it runs and the line appears; failed with the check off and with the note off) | The owner type's own operations are not listed in the block (the census has no cheap list of methods that return the same type or end the object; not started). The count is the product of the `n` seen, capped by the units done, not a count of distinct combinations |
| A per-site count column ties only to the findings at its own line | corrected | In a real run six findings were confirmed on listener counts; two (sites `:393`, `:730`) had no column of their own and were tied through `node.listeners@lib/adapters/http.js:717`, the line of `transport.request(...)`, which the census lists as a writer of every registration on `req` (20 candidates) and where the 7 listeners counted per unit are the runtime's own. The Growth line of a finding also quoted that column instead of its own. | `node.listeners@`, `node.timers@` and `go.goroutines@` columns (not the native allocation-site columns) list as candidates only findings whose own site is that line: no same-function neighbour, no census writer line. The report's Growth line quotes the passing column at the finding's own site before another tied column (`alloc-test` already tried the columns at the finding's site first). On the evidence copy this leaves `:717`, `:787` and `:971` with no candidate and F14 and F18 unconfirmable by a column; F6, F25, F26, F27, F28 (and F3, by its probe column) stand. Tests: `trend::candidates::tests::a_per_site_count_column_ties_only_to_the_findings_at_its_own_line`, `dla-report::render::the_column_at_the_findings_own_site_is_quoted_before_another_tied_column`. Fixtures read, not run: the per-site columns in the logs of earlier bench runs sit at the leak's own site (`audit.js:18`, `heartbeat.js:15`, `subscription.go:33`); `node.listeners@src/gateway.js:24` of the heartbeat fixture loses its F5/F6 tie. |
| A second look could leave out the slot it did not like | corrected | In a real run the model wrote its second-look units as choices but left out exactly one slot of the prose recipe, when the intermediate objects are released, and every token of the chain lived in a vector until after the cancel: the slot the documented leak needs. The unit hook of a second look is now generated as a skeleton: five slot variables in the language of the driver (`depth`, `ending`, `when`, `order`, `outcome`), each taken with the helper and followed by its options in a comment, above the not-filled-in marker; the block keeps one short paragraph (every slot variable must be used, and why). `dla harness run` refuses a second look in which a slot variable has no use beyond its own line in the unit body (comments stripped; `invalid-value`, names the slots and what each means, the fix shows the give-up form). A slot that truly does not apply is given up by replacing its line with `// slot <name>: not applicable: <reason>` (`#` in Python), two of five at most (a third is refused); `summary.slots_given_up` of the run and `slots_given_up` of `dla trend` list them next to the combination line. The slot lines are ignored when two units are compared for the same-code refusal. Tests: `a_slot_is_used_given_up_or_reported_unused_in_every_language` (`harness.rs`, six languages), `the_unit_skeleton_parses_unfilled_in_python_node_and_rust`, and `second_look.rs` (unused slots refused with the names, no skeleton refused for its helper count, three given up refused, two accepted and listed in the run and the trend); failed with the slot check off and with a skeleton line missing its semicolon | The use check is textual (a slot variable counts as used by any code line that names it; a use in dead code passes) |
| A holder whose key comes from the caller needs a key it has not seen, and a fresh object when the key is compared by identity | corrected | In a real run on a Java data library the documented cache (`resolved`, a map per class loader whose key is the lambda the caller passes to `TypedPropertyPaths.of`) read constant at 190 for a unit that passed the same few method references: a method reference or a lambda that captures nothing is one object per call site for the whole run, so every unit used the same keys. Experiment on a copy (unit forms tried in the library's own reader): a lambda that captures a local is REJECTED (`Cannot derive method reference ... Method calls allowed on owning type 'int' only`: the reader takes the first parameter of the implementation method, which is the captured value), a new instance of a class implementing the interface and an anonymous class are REJECTED (`Not a lambda`, no `writeReplace`), a method reference bound to a fresh receiver is REJECTED (`Method '...name()' not found`); a serializable lambda created per unit with `LambdaMetafactory.altMetafactory` (FLAG_SERIALIZABLE, implementation `Person.getName`) is accepted and is a new object each time: the probe read `grew` (reachable objects 3121 to 27065 over 9 points, slope 6.8 per unit; bytes 138632 to 1218312, 307 B per unit; the outer map `.size` stays 1: one class loader), `alloc-test` leak, sibling, refute, test, confirm all accepted. Now the target block has one `key:` line when the census says an insert at the holder takes its key from a parameter (`HolderFacts::key_from_caller`: insert at depth 0, `param`, not constant, not at start-up): the key comes from the caller, the holder grows only when each unit passes a key it has not seen (a key compared by value is built from the unit's key; a key compared by identity must be a fresh object per unit), and per runtime what makes a fresh one (JVM: `new`, a lambda that captures a local, and, when the library rejects those, a serializable lambda made per unit with `LambdaMetafactory.altMetafactory`; Node and Python: a function or object literal evaluated in the unit; Go: a new pointer or a value built from the key; Rust: a new allocation when the key is a pointer or an id taken from one). `dla trend` adds to the `constant` reason of such a holder that the unit may have passed the same keys every time; the `close-rest` remedy of such a finding adds "(the key comes from the caller: pass a key the holder has not seen; a key compared by identity needs a fresh object per unit)" (the remedy the tool prints was already free of project names). No sixth second-look slot: the line is in the block of the first pass and of the second look alike, and a slot would shift the give-up limit for one kind of holder. Tests: `a_key_the_caller_passes_gets_one_line_per_runtime_and_nothing_else_does`, `a_constant_probe_of_a_key_the_caller_passes_says_the_unit_may_have_passed_the_same_objects` (both failed with `key_from_caller` off) | On the evidence ledger of the real run 113 of 388 ranked candidates have an insert whose key derives from a parameter (the line applies), 258 have writer sites with no such key and 17 have no writer site: for those the census cannot tell and no line is printed (no guess). The JVM sentence names a JDK factory, not a project |
| The JUnit repeat failed on a project whose tests use a mocking library that attaches | corrected | In two real runs on a Java data library the JUnit repeat failed on every unit (`Could not initialize plugin: interface org.mockito.plugins.MockMaker`, root cause `Could not self-attach to current VM using external process`), so the model never used it and every candidate cost a hand-written unit. The attach is made by the mocking library's agent helper: under JDK 21 a JVM cannot attach to itself, so the helper starts a second JVM that attaches to the first through the Attach API; a sandbox or container that refuses the attach makes it fail. On this machine the attach works (the log only warns), so the failure was reproduced with `-XX:+DisableAttachMechanism` in `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS`: 18 of 18 units failed with the same message. `dla-junit-build.sh` now adds a start-up agent when the test class path holds a jar whose manifest has a `Premain-Class` (read with `unzip -p`: `mockito-core` 5.23 has `org.mockito.internal.PremainAttach`; `byte-buddy-agent` has `net.bytebuddy.agent.Installer` and is the fallback for a mockito-core without the entry): a `"-javaagent:<jar>"` line in `run.args`, `startup-agents.txt`, a `DLA-BUILD start-up agent:` line in the build log, `summary.startup_agent` in the run output and `startup-agent:<jar>` in the manifest `tools`. With it the same run (attach disabled) passed 18 of 18 units and DLA's own columns (heap, classes, probes) were all there. When every unit still fails on a self-attach the `DLA-JUNIT every unit failed` text now adds the fix. Measured on the data library: the documented cache (`TypedPropertyPaths.resolved`) under a JUnit repeat of the four test classes that name `TypedPropertyPath` (Java and Kotlin), 792 tests per run, 18 units: reachable objects 2745 to 4615, bytes 85880 to 170808 (5.3 KB per unit), `dla trend` `grew`; `alloc-test` leak decided by the probe; sibling, refute, test and confirm accepted. Tests: `a_mocking_jar_with_a_start_up_agent_entry_is_added_to_the_loops_jvm_and_none_otherwise` (the build script with a stand-in Maven and real jars: the agent in `run.args` and `startup-agents.txt`; the older mockito-core falls back to the byte-buddy jar; none otherwise; failed with the step switched off). The `key:` explanation of a target block is now printed once per driver file ("Key objects ...") with a short pointer line per target: `a_key_the_caller_passes_gets_one_line_per_runtime_and_nothing_else_does` (failed when the long text was printed per target) | No bench fixture uses a mocking library or the JUnit repeat, so the bench is unaffected and unchanged; I did not run `dla-bench`. The agent is added only when `unzip` exists |
