### Round R2 fixes (intervene, bundle, census, native labels)

| Item | Status | Evidence | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dla intervene` and a renamed control column | corrected in R3 (partial in R2) | A site-group column embeds `file:line` and a probe column embeds the owner. A patch that adds a line above the writer, or renames the owner, left the treated series without the control's exact name, and the judge read the missing column as growth removed | `--patch` refuses a hunk that adds or removes lines above a recorded writer (the line number would move). After the run, a treated column of the same kind (`alloc@`, `probe:`, `holder:`) that the control series did not have is a renamed twin: the result is invalid, with a limitation line naming it. Test `a_moved_site_or_renamed_owner_is_a_twin_not_a_removal`. The R2 fix was partial: a treated run that lacked the control column still counted as growth removed whenever any other column existed, and `os.rss` or `cgroup.*` always exist. R3: a missing column is growth removed only for `--stub-writer`, and only when the control's own source (the in-process agent or the allocator shim) still produced a non-whole-process column in the treated run (`os.*`, `cgroup.*`, `fd.*` and `gap.*` never count, since the operating system writes them whatever the edit did; the agent's own totals such as `go.heap_live` do count, because only a running agent writes them). For `--patch` a missing control column is invalid with a limitation naming the column, because a patch can rename an owner or move a site; a patch must keep the measured column to be judged. A patch hunk that rewrites a recorded writer line and drops the owner identifier is refused before the run (text based; other renames are caught by the missing column). Tests `a_missing_column_is_removal_only_when_the_same_source_still_worked`, `an_os_rss_only_control_is_never_proven_removed`, `a_patch_that_renames_the_owner_on_a_writer_line_is_refused` |
| `dla intervene` edits through a symlink | corrected | `copy_tree` kept the text of an absolute in-project link, so a clone's link still led into the original harness's `work/` | Absolute links inside the source `work/` are re-pointed into the clone, and `plan.rs` refuses any path that crosses a symlink (read and write). Tests `copy_tree_repoints_an_absolute_link_into_the_copy`, `a_path_through_a_symlink_is_refused` |
| Two diff headers for one file | corrected | `a/./x.py` and `b/x.py` were two keys; the second write replaced the first | Headers are normalized (`a/`, `b/`, leading `./`) and sections of one file merge in order; a duplicate path after normalization is refused. Test `two_headers_of_one_file_are_one_file` |
| Blind bundle and the finding's site and id | corrected in R3 (partial in R2) | Tied growth columns carried `site`, `finding`, `candidates`, `tie` and a column name `alloc@file:line`; growth frames chose the excerpt | Every JSON object that names a `column` loses the tie keys, names are cut at `@`, and in growth records the finding's id, site and writers are replaced in every string. `index.json` no longer holds `finding`. Excerpts come from allocation and retention frames only: a growth frame is the tied site, so using it would hand the verifier the answer. The directory name still holds the finding id; the verifier is given the directory path by the core. Tests `tie_keys_and_site_bearing_names_are_scrubbed`, `a_tied_growth_record_leaves_no_site_or_finding_id_in_the_bundle`. The R2 scrub was partial: only growth records were scrubbed, so discriminator, histogram and allocation records still carried the finding id, the site and top-level `finding`/`candidates`/`ties`. R3: every bundled record and raw copy is scrubbed. The keys `finding`, `candidates`, `ties`, `tie`, `tied`, `basis` go at every depth; the finding id (with and without the worker prefix, as a whole token) and the finding's site and recorded writers are replaced in every string and key, except under the structured frame keys of allocation and retention records (`frames`, `root_path`, `path`, `paths`, `retention_path(s)`, `chain`, `via`), which are raw evidence the verifier reads and the only source of excerpts. Limit: a non-JSON raw copy of an allocation or retention record keeps its site text, because its frames cannot be told from other text there (the finding id is still replaced). Tests `every_record_loses_ids_sites_and_the_drop_keys`, `frame_fields_of_allocation_and_retention_keep_the_site`, `raw_text_copies_lose_the_finding_id`, `no_bundled_record_names_the_finding_or_its_site_outside_frame_fields` |
| ThreadLocal holder attribution | corrected | A value held by two entries of one thread (both maps, or two ThreadLocals) was subtracted and credited twice; a ThreadLocal held by two static fields was credited to the alphabetically first | A value is counted once per thread. A ThreadLocal held by more than one different static field is credited to none (the values stay with the thread), since the owner is not known. Tests `a_value_in_both_thread_maps_is_counted_once`, `a_threadlocal_held_by_two_static_fields_is_not_credited_to_either` |
| Census owner of initializer stores | corrected | Kotlin had no test. A static block owned any assignment, including a local declared earlier or `obj.x`. The class-file path took the next store on the line whatever it was | Java: a bare name must not be declared in the block, a qualified one must name an enclosing class. Kotlin: `init { prop = .. }` is owned by the property unless the block declared a local of that name; companion and object property initializers are tested. Class file: the store must follow the allocation through its constructor and object-returning calls; a call that returns nothing or a primitive ends the chain and the owner stays the initializer. Tests `a_local_in_a_static_block_does_not_own_the_holder`, `companion_object_and_init_block_stores_are_owned_by_their_property`, `a_thread_local_made_in_clinit_is_owned_by_the_field_it_is_stored_in` |
| Native leak could not take `L-HANDLE` | corrected | `alloc-stack-diff` had no reader for the allocator shim's stacks. The shim stacks existed only as `type-histogram-diff` (a histogram record), and the policy's `alloc-stack-diff` call read the JFR profile of the managed side, which names no native site. `L-HANDLE` needs an allocation-stack record tied to the finding | `shim-alloc-stacks` reads the same `shim-dump.jsonl` files for `alloc-stack-diff`: same stack keys, same ties (site only, no loosening), recorded as `alloc-stack` evidence that states the finding its growing stack tied to. The harness prints the command after the histogram one. On `jni-order-codec`, `jvm/F19` takes `L-HANDLE` (was refused `label-evidence-missing`). Test `the_shim_stacks_are_an_alloc_stack_input_tied_by_site_alone` |
| Residual rows and the series (R3) | corrected | `gate::residual_covers` read `coverage.residual` for the whole run, so rows computed for one series accounted for a whole-process column of another; rows did not record their series | `ResidualRow` has a `series` field, written by `dla gap <S> --residual`; the gate counts only the rows of the series the column's trend analysed, and a row without a series (an older run) covers nothing. `set_residual` replaces rows per region and series. Tests `gate_h1::a_whole_process_column_is_never_tied_and_is_accounted_for_by_the_residual`, `coverage::residual_rows_are_replaced_per_region_and_series` |
| `dla trend` hint for whole-process columns (R3) | corrected | `next` suggested `ledger add --origin growth-series` for `os.rss` and other whole-process columns, which the gate never ties | Such a column gets `dla gap <S> --residual` instead (one hint per trend). Test `trend::a_whole_process_column_is_hinted_to_the_residual_accounting_not_ledger_add` |
| State field docs: stop attempts (R3) | corrected | `WorkerEntry.stalled` still said the gate "blocked the worker three times" | Docs and the generated schema say failing stop attempts, as the hook does |
| `dla-bench dynamic` ignored a fixture's `[toolchain]` (B0; closes the first part of item (8) of "Open repair items") | corrected | A version pair (`go-ticker-legacy` on Go 1.22.12, `node-signal-compose` on Node 22 and 24, `python-zstd-options` on 3.14.4 and 3.14.5) ran its harness on the host tools in the mechanical acceptance, so a pair could not be accepted on the tool it names | `dynamic_cmd::fixture_toolchains` calls `fixture_toolchain::provide_all` once before any fixture (the code `dla-bench run` uses; a release that cannot be provided stops the run with the fixture's name) and `command_env` gives every `dla` command of the fixture its `PATH` prefix, `GOTOOLCHAIN` and `JAVA_HOME`. Tests: `tests/dynamic_toolchain.rs` (`dynamic_runs_the_dla_commands_on_the_tool_the_toolchain_names` reads `command -v node` and `node --version` inside a stand-in `dla` and fails without the change, as the host's node answers; `dynamic_stops_before_any_command_when_a_release_cannot_be_provided`; `dynamic_leaves_a_fixture_without_a_toolchain_on_the_host_tools`), unit tests `a_fixtures_toolchain_goes_first_on_the_path_of_a_dla_command` and `a_fixture_without_a_toolchain_changes_no_variable` |
| Version pairs and UNCLASSIFIED traps in the validator and graders (B0) | decided | Two of the new pairs (`node-signal-compose`, `python-zstd-options`) have a trap with cause UNCLASSIFIED: the cause is runtime bookkeeping or a C-extension reference leak. `trap_miss_rate` counted only `archetype-leak` fixtures with a `pair`, and the validator did not check what a pair holds | Such a trap is `archetype-free` with `pair`. Both graders count `archetype-leak` and `archetype-free` fixtures with a `pair` in `trap_miss_rate` (tests `an_unclassified_trap_with_a_pair_counts_in_the_trap_miss_rate`, `test_trap_miss_rate_requires_pair_and_all_confirmable_found`; sample `testdata/free-pair-sample`, a version pair, in `graders_agree`). The `decoys` block stays keyed by archetype row: an UNCLASSIFIED trap has no row and adds none. The validator refuses a pair that is not one trap and one `decoy-control`, that differs in runtime, or whose `[toolchain]` tables name different tools (tests `an_unclassified_version_pair_is_accepted`, `a_pair_needs_one_trap_and_one_decoy`, `a_version_pair_may_not_swap_the_tool_it_names`). The `trap_false_alarm_rate` of the decoy is unchanged |
| JVM row L-STALE (B0) | decided | `node-retry-scheduler` is a chain held through an object's own fields and stays UNCLASSIFIED; L-STALE had no row and no pair (`jvm-session-index`, `jvm-session-index-pruned`, built in phase 2) | `references/jvm-archetypes.md` has an L-STALE row (index, registry or cache entry that names state removed, replaced or finished elsewhere, reachable only through the container; rule-outs N-WARM and N-INTENT; the earlier `dla trend --against` rule-out was a command that cannot compare a pruned variant, see the Pa P7 row). Sources: Bloch, Effective Java 3rd ed., Item 7 "Eliminate obsolete object references" (caches, listeners and callbacks; the book text was not fetchable, the item and its three sources of obsolete references were read in secondary summaries, which is a limit of this row), the Oracle troubleshooting guide for memory leaks (fetched: "a memory leak occurs when an application unintentionally holds references to Java objects or classes, preventing them from being garbage collected") and the `WeakHashMap` javadoc (fetched: values held by strong references must not refer to their own keys). The `data/archetypes.toml` and `agents/verifier.md` summaries say that the entry is reachable from a container the program owns and not through a chain of the objects' own fields, which separates L-STALE from `node-retry-scheduler`. `archetypes::runtime_rows("jvm")` and `policy::SHIPPED_ROWS` list L-STALE; `runtime_rows_match_the_shipped_reference_files` and `the_policy_labels_exactly_the_rows_of_the_shipped_prose_files` keep them equal to the file (both failed with the row added and the lists not). Every L-* row of `data/archetypes.toml` now has a shipped row in some runtime, so the policy test of a row without a shipped prose row uses a made-up cause |
| A Node probe on a root it cannot enter read a constant | corrected | `node-signal-compose-24`: the probe on `const stop = new AbortController()` (state in `#signal`, no own keys) read a constant 1 on every point, and the flat line refuted a true finding | The Node agent writes `size`, `reachable` and `bytes` as null with an `error` for a root it cannot enter: a function, a leaf or opaque value, or an object of a class (prototype neither `Object.prototype` nor null, not an Array, Map or Set) with no own keys. The harness then writes no `probe:` column and a limitation line. Maps, Sets, arrays (also subclasses), plain and null-prototype objects and class instances with own fields keep their column. `harness_run::node_probe_of_an_unenterable_root_writes_no_column_and_a_limitation` (saw it fail with the agent change reverted: the controller, private-field class, function and promise probes still had columns). The alloc-test needed no change: with no column for the finding it reads the region's primary column, as `dla-bench dynamic` on the pair shows. Limit: a class whose only state is private fields, with public fields added later, is measured once it has an own key |
| FinalizationRegistry cells as a registration holder | corrected | `node-signal-compose-24`: 39,960 of 87,088 cells of Node's own registry kept one process-wide signal alive and grew with the requests; heap-graph named no holder for them | The snapshot reader groups `WeakCell`s that have a live weak `target` by target (cell plus holdings object, counts are cells; cells without a target, the cleanup backlog, are not counted) and names the owner by a reverse walk of at most two property hops to a module-scope variable, not through a `Module`'s `exports` object or the wrapper parameters; exactly one owner or no holder. `summarize heap-graph` gets the view `registry:finalization:<module>.<variable>` (registration-table), prefers it over a `static:` holder of the same owner and still drops holders inside `node:` modules. Tests: `holders::tests::finalization_cells_with_a_live_target_are_a_registration_holder_of_the_owner`, `cells_without_a_live_target_make_no_holder`, `the_owner_is_found_when_the_variable_holds_the_target_itself`, `a_target_kept_by_two_variables_or_only_by_exports_has_no_owner` (two variables, exports only, three hops, function scope; the exports case saw it fail with the exclusion removed), `summarize_node::registry_cells_with_a_live_target_are_the_registration_holder_that_ties_to_the_owner`, `a_growing_backlog_of_cells_without_a_target_is_no_holder`, `node_heapsnapshot::tests::a_finalization_holder_wins_over_the_variable_of_the_same_owner` (saw the two heap-graph tests and the preference test fail with the change removed). `dla-bench dynamic --only node-signal-compose` passes both halves. Limit: only owners within two property hops of the target are found, so a target held through a deeper structure has no holder; the reverse index is built only when live-target cells exist |
| heap-graph said "take another snapshot" when only Node's own modules grew | corrected | `node-signal-compose-24` before the holder above: the strictly growing holder was a `node:` module registry, dropped from the tracked holders, and the output read that nothing grows | When no tracked holder passes the dump-series rule and a holder inside a `node:` module does, `reasons` says the only strictly growing holders are inside Node's own modules and names them, and `needed` points at the project's own registration, a probe or an allocation stack instead of more snapshots. The tracked holders and the gate are unchanged. `summarize_node::growth_only_inside_node_modules_is_said_so_and_is_not_a_call_for_more_snapshots` (saw it fail with the check removed) |
| Go command mode read the heap only after `main` returned, so state owned by locals of the workload was missing from the series and from the heap profile (`go-header-index`, `go-trace-context`, the second leak of `go-feed-merge`) | corrected | The exit record is written after `main` returned and two collections. A request loop that fills a local index reads about 0.17 to 0.18 MB at every point whatever `n` was (`go-header-index`: 170 to 184 KB over nine points while the heap held 18 to 155 MB in the run), and the heap profile taken then holds runtime stacks only. A prototype sampler in a scratch copy of the wrapper showed the in-run heap growing with `n` on both fixtures | Command mode starts a sampler goroutine (`dlaStartSampler`, never in driver mode): one collection per sample, the first at 2 ms and each next one 15 % after the last ended, never more than a quarter of the elapsed time spent in samples, a sample that ends after `main` returned dropped. The exit record adds `values_in_run` and `in_run` (samples, window samples, reading time, end time, time spent, statistic) and the series gains `go.heap_live.in_run` and `go.heap_objects.in_run` (bytes, gauge, heap, read after a collection, not a baseline): the lowest reading among the samples that ended in the last third of the run, which does not rise with the number of samples on a flat workload (the maximum does) and reads 65 to 87 % of a leak's true slope in the prototype, so it is a lower bound. Existing columns keep their meaning. `DLA_INRUN=off` switches the sampler off. Tests: `go_command_mode_reads_state_owned_by_locals_while_the_workload_runs` (a real `go test` run: exit `go.heap_live` under 1 MB at every point, the in-run column above half of what was allocated and rising, the floor line in the series limitations), `go_command_mode_says_when_no_sample_ended_and_keeps_the_base_columns` (no sample ended, and the switched-off sampler: no in-run column, the limitation line, base columns intact), `go_driver_mode_records_carry_no_in_run_readings` and `the_go_templates_share_one_engine_block_and_only_command_mode_samples` (driver mode and the shared engine block), `names_map_to_the_documented_meaning` (`dla-core`, catalog type), `in_run_readings_become_catalog_columns_and_only_where_a_point_has_them` (`build.rs`), `in_run_readings_keep_numbers_and_a_malformed_section_does_not_lose_the_measurement` (`dumps.rs`), `dump_ignores_unknown_fields_and_reads_the_full_record` (`harness.rs` round trip), `a_go_in_run_column_is_a_whole_process_heap_column_judged_by_the_heap_residual_row` (`gate.rs`: the column is judged by the heap residual row only). The Go tests, the catalog, the build, the dump parser and the gate tests were each run with the change removed and failed; the driver and engine-block tests pass without it and guard the edit. `harness-dump.schema.json` regenerated |
| The Go heap profile of a command-mode point was taken after the workload ended | corrected | `go-header-index` and `go-trace-context` with `--capture alloc`: the exit profile held runtime and harness stacks, so `dla summarize alloc-stack-diff` had nothing to tie | With alloc capture the profile of the last valid in-run sample is written as the point's `go-<n>.heap.pb.gz` (role "heap profile (in-use bytes by stack, taken in the run at <ms> ms, after GC)") instead of the exit-time one: one heap profile per point. When no sample produced a profile the exit profile is written as before and a limitation line says so; goroutine captures stay at exit. Tests: `go_command_mode_writes_the_heap_profile_taken_in_the_run_with_the_allocating_stack` (the role says it was taken in the run, one profile per point, the stack through the allocating function holds over a megabyte), `go_command_mode_without_an_in_run_heap_profile_keeps_the_exit_profile_and_says_so`, and the `go-pprof-heap` adapter limitation in `a_heap_stack_ties_by_its_allocation_site_and_a_function_row_never_ties`; the first and the last were run with the change removed and failed. On `go-header-index` the four in-run profiles pass the growth gate with stacks through `(*Index).Note` and `ReadFrame`, tied to the census finding at the index writer |
| The primary heap column of a bound alloc-test read a flat exit-time column on a leak held by locals | corrected | `go-header-index`: the bound `dla alloc-test --finding` on its heap finding, run in a scratch copy with the exit column as the primary, read `go.heap_live` (plateau, flat) and favoured non-leak, the leak held by locals of the workload | `go.heap_live.in_run` is the primary column of a heap finding only when the latest trend passed it through the gate and `go.heap_live` did not pass or rose by less than a tenth of the in-run rise (the basis names "the in-run reading"). Where both passed and the exit column carries the growth, its exact reading decides: the in-run floor read plateau on go-ticker-legacy in about 2 of 8 runs. An exit column that passed on a rise of its own does not decide: on go-header-index it passed in 7 of 90 saved trends, on about 1.5 KB beside 113 MB in the run, and the bound test then read non-leak on the leak. In the 155 saved trends of the Go leak fixtures whose state survives the exit and where both columns passed, the exit slope was 0.6 to 3.7 times the in-run slope; in those 7 it was at most 0.00002 times; otherwise the list is as before, so a series whose in-run column is flat is judged by the exit column exactly as it was. The in-run columns have no purge reading and no pre-collection twin, so the purge and gc readings are absent for them. An unbound test lets a passing in-run column vote like any passed column. Tests: `the_in_run_heap_column_is_the_heap_primary_only_when_the_trend_passed_it` (failed with the change removed), `the_exit_heap_column_leads_when_it_passed_the_trend_beside_the_in_run_one` (both passed with a like rise, both passed with a token rise of the exit column, only in-run, neither; the token case failed with the share set to zero), `a_passing_in_run_column_votes_on_an_unbound_test_and_a_flat_one_leaves_the_exit_heap`, `an_in_run_column_has_no_purge_and_no_gc_reading_and_is_judged_by_its_own_shape` (the last two pass without the change and guard the contract). Dynamic run on `go-header-index`: the bound test on both heap findings read leak with `go.heap_live.in_run` as the primary column |
| The in-run column on the Go fixtures that must stay as they are | corrected | Dynamic runs of the six Go fixtures of the bench with the change, with the sampler off, and the last full run before it: all six pass in all three. The in-run columns failed the trend gate at every trend on `go-buffer-pool`, `go-gc-tuned-batch`, `go-subscription-workers-closed` and `go-ticker-modern` (a flat workload), and passed it on `go-subscription-workers`, whose heap really grows. The base columns that pass the gate at one trend or another on a flat workload (slopes of a few bytes per unit under noise of kilobytes) differ from run to run with the sampler off as well. Summed exit time over the nine points, sampler on against off: `go-gc-tuned-batch` 87 s against 96 s, `go-buffer-pool` 34 s against 35 s (inside the noise of a shared machine); the prototype measured +2 to +3 % of CPU on runs of 8 to 100 s and +16 % on a one-second run of a pointer-heavy heap | The base `go.*` columns are read as before, after `main` returned and two collections (`go.heap_objects.pre_gc` is still the collector-headroom reading of `go-gc-tuned-batch`). The sampler's time is bounded by its budget (a quarter of the elapsed time at the moment a sample starts, one sample of overshoot). Not changed here: the heap residual rows of `dla gap --residual` still read `go.heap_live`, so growth seen only in the in-run column is covered by the residual row of its region (under its threshold) instead of being credited to a finding; on `go-header-index` the final gate passes with the two residual open items resolved as unresolved |
| Python module probes never import a module | corrected | A probe read a module by importing it, so a module the workload never loaded was run by the agent. On a copy of aiohttp 3.10.10 (Python 3.13, runtime dependencies and the project installed editable without extensions) the generated driver with the automatic probes, its hooks filled with a small cache and logger workload, was killed at its 150 s limit before the change (the example script that starts a server at import was imported by the agent) and ran to the end in 13.8 s after it. The probed modules the workload never loaded (12: example scripts and the docs configuration) have no column and one limitation line names them. Tests: `a_python_module_probe_never_imports_a_module_the_workload_did_not_load` (failed with the change removed: the point ran into its timeout), `a_probe_never_imports_and_says_so_for_shapes_a_project_really_has` (a module that leaves a mark when imported, a package whose parent only is loaded, a spec without a module and a `PyGlobal` of a module never loaded; failed with the module name left unconverted in the limitation line) | A module probe reads a module only when it is in `sys.modules` at the checkpoint (or is the running `__main__`) and never imports. Other runtimes: the Node agent reads `Module._cache` and never requires; the JVM agent reads only classes in the loaded-class list; the JVM fallback without Instrumentation loads the class without initialising it, but reading a static field then initialises it. That fallback is not reachable in a harness run (the agent is always attached) and was not changed |
| A killed point names the phase it was in | corrected | `dla harness run` appended nothing to "did not finish in N s and was killed". The Python agent now writes its current phase to a file next to its dump (the program's own code before its first hook or checkpoint, `hook setup`, `hook unit` with the round, the checkpoint reading a named probe) and the note of a point killed at its timeout ends with the last line, or says the agent recorded no phase. The same aiohttp driver with a setup hook that never returns now ends its error with `it was killed in: hook setup`. Tests: `a_python_point_killed_at_its_timeout_says_what_it_was_doing`, `a_point_of_another_runtime_that_times_out_says_it_knows_no_phase` (both failed with the change removed), `a_phase_note_keeps_the_first_line_and_bounds_it` (new function, passes) | The Node, JVM, Go, Rust and native agents write no phase file, so their notes say the agent recorded no phase; a process stuck before the Python agent starts also has none. The phase file is deleted before each process starts so a later point never reports an earlier one |
| One site is one finding | corrected | A rule or analyzer hit and a census entry at one site were two ledger findings because the candidate writer skipped only an `(origin, site)` pair already present: two origins never compared. On aiohttp the sites `aiohttp/web_app.py:82` (F8 from a rule, F102 from the census) and `aiohttp/web_log.py:61` (F9 and F117) are now one finding each, F8 and F9, which carry the census holder, owner, evidence and name-matched writers as a `finding_annotated` line with `also_origin` and `also_supports`, the other origin in the report (`Also raised by`) and the rule's message in a note (189 findings before, 187 after). The static acceptance passes all 64 fixtures with and without archetype data; against the same tools on the unchanged build the only differences are 22 fewer census candidates (321 against 299 in total), each a site that a rule also raised, and the leaks' sites, flags and confirmability are identical. Tests: `a_rule_hit_and_a_census_entry_at_one_site_are_one_finding_in_either_order`, `a_second_run_of_a_stage_does_not_join_again`, `lines_of_one_declaration_are_one_site_and_other_lines_are_not`, `a_different_owner_at_a_joined_site_is_said_in_the_note_not_refused` (all failed with the join removed), `a_worker_never_records_on_a_finding_of_the_core` (failed with the worker check removed: foreign-item refusal), `two_rules_at_one_site_stay_two_findings_and_a_census_entry_joins_the_first`, `a_growth_series_finding_is_never_joined_to_a_static_site`, `a_decorator_stack_and_its_def_are_one_anchor`, `a_decorated_class_and_a_method_inside_it_are_separate_sites`, `other_languages_and_unreadable_files_keep_their_lines`, `a_site_raised_by_a_rule_and_the_census_shows_both_origins_and_ranks_as_the_better`, and `a_rule_hit_and_the_census_entry_of_one_site_are_one_finding` through the binary (failed with the join removed). What a rule's hit declares wins over the census entry, which fills only what the rule left empty and is named in the note where it differs, in either order of arrival; the Go ticker rules and the Node timer rule declare registration-table and heap because the leaked object is the timer, which the census reads as a blocked stack in non-heap and which sent the alloc-test of go-ticker-legacy to the goroutine column. Tests: `what_a_rule_declares_wins_over_the_census_in_either_order_and_the_census_is_in_the_note` and `a_declared_owner_wins_over_the_census_owner_in_either_order` (both failed with the precedence removed), `the_ticker_rules_declare_the_registered_timer_as_holder_in_the_heap` (failed without the declaration), `a_timer_hit_declares_the_registered_timer_as_holder_in_the_heap`, `a_timer_finding_reads_the_heap_column_not_the_goroutines_of_the_census_region`, and `a_ticker_hit_and_the_census_timer_of_one_call_are_one_finding_the_rule_describes` through the binary (failed without the declaration) | Only a census entry and a rule or analyzer hit share a finding; two rules at one site stay two findings (a test of the real analyzers expects them reported separately), and a growth-series finding is never joined. The same line, or a Python decorator line and its `def` line, is one site; a decorator on Java, Kotlin or other languages is not recognised. A joined hit counts as a duplicate in the backend's coverage row so the gate accounting still adds up. Ledgers written before the change keep their pairs, which `dla ledger duplicate` still merges |
| `dla harness gen --probe F<n>` never drops the request | corrected | A finding with no probe target of its own was dropped without a message: the census kind of a module-level cached function (`cache-decorator`) had no target, so `--probe F8` on aiohttp produced no probe for the function. A module-level cached function is now a `PyGlobal` (its size is `currsize`), a finding merged into another is refused with the finding to use, a finding that no probe of its runtime can read is recorded as a target of the harness without a probe, with the reason in the manifest gaps (Go command mode has no package-variable probes: `--driver` does), and a finding probed only through its module is said in the output notes. Tests: `probe_of_the_merged_finding_reads_the_cached_function_as_a_global`, `probe_of_a_finding_merged_into_another_names_the_one_to_use` (both failed with the change removed), `probe_of_a_finding_no_probe_can_read_is_a_target_without_a_probe_and_says_why` (rewritten by the measurement queue change: the request is no longer refused) | The probe of a joined finding is found through the census entry of its site, also when a decorator line and the `def` line differ |
| A candidate is ranked by one tier function | corrected | The report ordered only the static-only findings by a private tier function, and nothing ranked the candidates a run still had to measure. One pure function of the finding and its census annotation now gives every candidate a tier (1 a DLA rule or known leak, 2 a census holder with writers and no removers in a registration table, container field or static/global, 3 another detector, 4 a census holder with writers, 5 the rest; a path in test, example, benchmark or documentation code is tier 6), tiers 1 to 4 are eligible for the queue, and the order inside a tier puts findings with more than one origin first, then ledger order. The report uses the same function and its tier note names the five tiers and the demotion. Tests: `tier_one_is_a_known_leak_or_a_dla_rule`, `tier_two_is_a_census_holder_with_writers_and_no_removers`, `tier_three_is_any_other_detector_and_beats_four`, `tier_four_and_five`, `test_example_benchmark_and_doc_sites_are_demoted_whatever_else_holds` (failed with the demotion removed), `multi_origin_sorts_first`, `ranking_orders_by_tier_then_multiple_origins_then_ledger_order` (failed with the demotion removed), and the report tests of the tier order, which pass unchanged | The tiers read the finding and its annotation only; a candidate whose holder the census could not name has no writers and stays in tier 5 |
| A harness declares the findings it was made to reach | corrected | `dla harness gen --probe F<n>` only added a probe, and a finding with no probe target of its own was refused. The finding is now also declared a target in the manifest (`targets`), a finding no probe can read is a target without a probe (the gap note stays), `--next [N]` declares the next pending candidates of the runtime in rank order, at most 8 targets in all are refused with `too-many-targets` before anything is created, and the generated driver of every runtime carries a comment block above its first hook: for each target its site, holder, owner, the rule message, up to three writers and removers with the enclosing function, what its probe reads (or that none does) and one line of instruction by holder kind, plus the general line about the public API and fresh keys. A declared target's probe column is never dropped by the cap of 64 columns. Tests: `next_declares_the_targets_prints_them_and_writes_the_block_in_the_driver`, `more_than_eight_targets_are_refused`, `the_block_goes_above_the_first_hook_with_its_indentation`, `a_c_driver_gets_comment_pairs`, `instructions_by_holder_kind`, `the_enclosing_function_is_named_with_its_class`, `the_probe_column_of_a_declared_target_is_never_dropped_by_the_cap` (failed with the priority removed) | The block is written into the generated driver files of all runtimes through one routine that finds the first hook comment; only the Python driver was run end to end here, the other templates are covered by the comment-marker rule alone |
| `dla trend` says what each targeted finding showed | corrected | A flat or never-loaded probe appeared nowhere: the trend printed a count of failed columns. For every declared target of the series' harness and every finding an automatic probe stands for, `dla trend` now stores one verdict in the trend report and prints the declared ones: `grew` (a tied column passed the gate), `changed` (a probe column moved but did not pass), `constant` (it stayed at one value, with the value and the number of points), `not_loaded` (the harness has a probe and the series no column), and without a probe `executed` or `not_executed` from the run's coverage (the function enclosing the site or a writer; the loaded class on the JVM), else `unknown` (no coverage data, as for a Go driver run, Rust, native or Python before 3.12). Tests: `grew_when_a_tied_column_passed_the_gate`, `changed_when_the_probe_moved_but_did_not_pass` (failed with the change test removed), `constant_when_the_probe_never_moved`, `not_loaded_when_the_probe_has_no_column`, `executed_when_the_function_of_a_writer_ran`, `not_executed_when_no_enclosing_function_ran`, `unknown_without_coverage_data`, `unknown_when_no_function_encloses_the_site`, `a_class_loaded_is_executed_for_a_jvm_run`, `verdicts_settle_candidates_and_close_rest_closes_the_rest` | The Node and Go coverage verdicts match a function by file and start line or name and were not run against a real Node or Go driver here |
| A candidate is settled by what a run showed, else it is pending | corrected | Whether a candidate was ever reached was never recorded: one harness of the runtime that ran satisfied every rule for every candidate. A candidate is now settled when a verdict of it is `grew` or `changed`, when a driver run that declared it saw `executed` or `unknown`, or when two driver runs with different driver files (a digest of the driver directory stored in the run record) declared it, and pending otherwise; a fixed command (command mode, JUnit repeat) settles only by growth. Tests: `candidates_are_pending_until_a_verdict_settles_them`, `a_changed_verdict_settles`, `executed_and_unknown_settle_only_a_declared_driver_target`, `a_fixed_command_settles_only_by_growth`, `a_junit_repeat_is_a_fixed_command`, `two_runs_of_one_driver_do_not_settle_two_different_drivers_do` (failed with the second-driver rule changed), `a_batch_skips_candidates_a_harness_that_has_not_run_already_declares` | A harness run before the digest existed has none, and counts as one driver per harness |
| A ranked candidate cannot be closed unmeasured while its queue is open | corrected | A run on a library with 187 candidates ended after five minutes with all of them closed `not_exercised` while most of the budget was left, because every rule asked only whether some harness of the runtime had run. `dla run init --queue on\|off` (default on) now holds a runtime's queue open while the budget is over the floor, the workload is not recorded as blocked by a blocker reason on `intake.workload` (not_exercised or any other reason does not close it) and a pending candidate is left. `dla ledger resolve` with not_exercised, no_harness or time_budget on a pending candidate is refused `target-pending` with its rank and the next batch command; the blocker reasons and everything about inconclusive, refute, test and confirm are accepted as before. `dla run status` and `dla gate check --final` lead with one `queue` item per runtime and a `queue` object, the item of a pending candidate carries the batch command, the item of any other open candidate `dla ledger close-rest`, `next_command` is the batch command, and the stop hook's reason leads with the queue item. With `--queue off` the report header says so and everything else is as before. Tests: `a_candidate_cannot_be_closed_unmeasured_and_the_gate_and_status_lead_with_the_queue` and `a_workload_answer_that_is_not_a_blocker_leaves_the_queue_open` (both failed with the refusal removed, and the second with the blocker-only rule loosened), `with_the_queue_off_everything_is_as_before`, `under_the_budget_floor_the_close_is_accepted`, `the_stop_hook_leads_with_the_queue_item`, `the_queue_is_closed_by_off_a_blocker_and_when_empty`. Existing tests that closed a candidate without a workload now start the run with the queue off or assert the new fix: `stages_fail_and_then_pass_through_a_swept_project`, `an_open_finding_names_the_harness_step_where_its_close_would_be_refused`, `the_unfinished_finding_fix_names_the_reason_and_never_time_budget`, `status_in_worker_mode_lists_only_the_workers_items_and_core_mode_reports_slices` | The coverage contradiction item now names a blocker reason for intake.workload while the runtime's queue is open, and still accepts the old answer as its own escape |
| `dla ledger list` and `dla ledger close-rest` | corrected | Agents read `ledger.jsonl` by hand and closed candidates in a shell loop. `dla ledger list [--state] [--limit] [--all]` prints one JSON line per finding in rank order (tier, rank, origins, site, holder, owner, writer and remover counts, queue status, the harnesses that targeted it) after a totals line, and `dla ledger close-rest [--remedy]` dispositions every open candidate that is not pending in an open queue through the rules of `dla ledger resolve` (suspected_static_only with static evidence, else unresolved; not_exercised, or time_budget when the budget reached its floor), with a remedy built from the finding's history, leaves a candidate whose column grew open to test, and writes one run-config record listing what it closed. Tests: `verdicts_settle_candidates_and_close_rest_closes_the_rest`, `under_the_budget_floor_the_close_is_accepted`, `a_candidate_cannot_be_closed_unmeasured_and_the_gate_and_status_lead_with_the_queue` | The full final gate was not made to pass in these tests: the whole-process trend columns of the harness series still ask for their own dispositions |
| The report says how each candidate was targeted | corrected | The report did not say which candidates a workload had reached. A header line after Coverage counts the ranked candidates that were targeted, in how many harnesses, with the verdicts (grew, changed, executed, not reached), and those not targeted; each static-only or unresolved finding says `Targeted: H2 constant, H3 not executed` or `Not targeted (rank 143)`, and the table of the other static-only findings has a column for it. A run started with the queue off says so in the header. Tests: `verdicts_settle_candidates_and_close_rest_closes_the_rest` (failed with the facts removed), `with_the_queue_off_everything_is_as_before` | A finding closed before it was ever ranked has no rank to print |
| A target whose run had no coverage data is not settled by one driver run | corrected | A run on a Rust library ended after one driver in 100 seconds: every target read `unknown` (no coverage data) and `Index::standing` settled a target on `executed` or `unknown`, so the queue was empty. Only `executed` settles it as executed now; `unknown` counts as one of the two different drivers of the two-drivers rule and nothing more. `dla ledger list` says it (`verdict_note`), the queue item of `dla run status` and the `target-pending` fix name the candidates that read `unknown`, `close-rest`'s remedy and the report's `Targeted:` line say "the run has no coverage data, so whether its code ran is not known: run a second, different driver, or one with a size reading", and the `Queue:` header counts them as `no coverage data`, not `executed`; `references/stages.md` says the same. Tests: `executed_settles_only_a_declared_driver_target_and_unknown_does_not` and `an_unknown_verdict_does_not_settle_and_is_explained` (both failed with the old rule: the candidate was settled), `an_unknown_run_counts_as_one_of_two_drivers` (failed with the old rule), `the_targeted_line_says_what_unknown_means` (failed without the text and without the count) | The CLI test gives a target the verdict `unknown` by editing the stored trend report: no run of the test project's runtime produces one there. The texts were not read from a real run that read `unknown` (the Rust coverage point now removes that case for Rust) |
| A Rust driver run records which functions its hooks reach | corrected | A Rust driver run had no coverage data, so every target without a probe read `unknown`. After the measured (uninstrumented) process `dla harness run` builds the driver again with `RUSTFLAGS=-C instrument-coverage` into `harness/H<n>/cov-build/` (the build command of the manifest, so features and the driver copy are the same), runs one round of `step` units (no warm-up, no captures, no sampler) with `LLVM_PROFILE_FILE` in `cov/`, merges the `.profraw` with `llvm-profdata` and exports with `llvm-cov export` the project functions with a count above zero into `cov/p1-<mode>-cover.rust.json` (demangled names, start lines); `executed-<S>.json` has kind `functions` and `dla trend` reads `executed` or `not_executed`, the entry-point coverage matches file and name. The tools are `<sysroot>/lib/rustlib/<host>/bin` (`rustup component add llvm-tools`), else PATH when their LLVM major version is the one `rustc -vV` reports (the PATH tools here are LLVM 18 and rustc's is 23, which cannot read each other's profiles: a limitation line, the targets stay `unknown`); a build, run or tool that fails is a limitation, never a refusal. Tests: `a_driver_run_reads_executed_and_not_executed_from_an_instrumented_process` (fails with the coverage call removed: both targets read `unknown`; skips without the llvm-tools component), `rust_export_gives_the_project_functions_that_ran_with_demangled_names`, `rust_functions_in_cov_give_entries_and_the_executed_record`. On a copy of a published utility crate (`tokio-util` 0.7.1, 8 targets, unit registering a waiter on a token and cancelling it) `harness run H1 --mode both` took 52 s with the instrumented build and `dla trend` read 3 targets `executed` and 5 `not_executed`, the queue stayed open for the rest | The limitation paths (no tools, PATH tools of another LLVM version, a failing instrumented build) are not exercised by a test: the machine has the component installed. A Rust binary-only crate has no driver and no coverage point. Inlined or generic functions are matched by the line the function starts at, a method of a trait implemented for several types by name and line only |
| Crate features reach the Rust driver build and a gated target says so | corrected | The harness build had no way to pass crate features, so three candidates in a module the crate gates behind the feature `rt` were closed `env_unavailable`. `dla harness gen --lang rust --driver --features a,b [--no-default-features]` adds them to the driver's `cargo build` (the command in `harness.json` carries them, `harness run` reuses it, so do the coverage build and every rerun; `--features` with another runtime or without `--driver` is refused `invalid-value`). A target whose module the crate declares behind `#[cfg(feature = "x")]`, or inside a `cfg_x! { .. }` block whose `macro_rules!` has that attribute, gets "behind feature x: generate with --features x" in its block and `summary.targets`, and `harness gen` prints `feature_note` for the ones the build lacks. The reader is a line scan of the `mod` declarations up to `lib.rs`; it does not see `#[path]`, modules declared in other macros or inline, a gate on the item itself, `cfg_attr`, `not(..)`, or a feature another feature enables, and `any(a, b)` names `a`. Tests: `a_gated_module_is_named_in_the_block_and_features_reach_the_build` (fails without the argument: the build has no `--features`), `features_are_for_a_rust_driver_only`, `a_module_declared_behind_a_feature_names_it_down_the_tree`, `a_module_inside_a_cfg_macro_block_is_gated_by_the_macro_features`, `a_module_file_beside_its_parent_is_found_by_the_parent_name`, `the_gate_line_says_what_to_generate_with_or_what_the_build_has` (fails without the line). On the `tokio-util` copy `harness gen --next` named `rt` for the three `spawn_pinned` targets, and with `--features rt` the driver built and ran and they read `executed` | The features are not carried to a later `harness gen` (each harness names its own); a feature enabled only through another feature's list is not named |

The block above the hooks of every runtime now says, after "In general" (and as a rule of an entry-path unit), "a unit covers the life of the objects it touches: create, derive (also from derived objects), use, cancel or close, drop or release, and not always in the same order (vary it by key)", and `references/stages.md` says it once where the hooks are explained (`SKILL.md` unchanged). Tests: `next_declares_the_targets_prints_them_and_writes_the_block_in_the_driver` and `the_block_goes_above_the_first_hook_with_its_indentation` (both failed with the line removed), `an_entry_path_block_has_the_callers_the_key_and_the_rules_and_no_general_line` | A unit written to the sentence on the `tokio-util` copy (a grandchild made through a temporary child, parent cancelled, all dropped) showed `native.live_bytes` growing 72 bytes per unit (`dla trend` column slope 72.0); whether a model reads the sentence and writes such a unit was not tried |
| A Rust harness run can say where the growth is allocated | corrected | A unit that leaked 72 bytes per call showed `native.live_bytes` at slope 72 and `dla alloc-test` favoured a leak, but a Rust harness has no size probe and `--capture alloc` wrote nothing, so nothing named the site and `ledger confirm` was refused `growth-not-tied`. `dla harness gen --driver` now also builds the allocator shim (a missing `cc` is not a refusal); `dla harness run H --mode fresh --capture alloc` runs the driver four more times (1 to 4 rounds of `step` units, no warm-up, shim preloaded and narrowed to the driver; the measured points and the build flags stay as they were) into `out/p<k>-alloc/`, splits out the shim's record (`shim-dump.jsonl`) and prints `dla summarize alloc-stack-diff` on them; in command mode the shim dumps of the points are copied the same way (`--capture alloc` now means this for Rust). The summarize op takes the first frame inside the project (the generated driver's file, whose counting allocator wraps every allocation, is skipped) with its demangled function and `file:line`; the tie rule is unchanged (a finding's site or recorded writer at that exact line); with no finding at the line the output names the findings whose site or recorded writer lies in the same Rust function as a hint (`same_function`, and `next` with `dla ledger annotate <F> --writer <file:line>`, to run only if the block allocated there is what that holder keeps, then the summarize again: the tie is computed when summarize runs), never as a tie; an untied stack keeps its site on the column, so `dla ledger add --origin growth-series --evidence <that evidence>` (no `--site`) gives the finding that site and the column ties to it at once. `dla trend` of a Rust harness series with a passing whole-process column adds `dla harness run H --mode fresh --capture alloc` to `next` (native prints none: its capture kind does not exist). A crate with its own `#[global_allocator]` is a limitation line. On a copy of a published utility crate (`tokio-util` 0.7.1) the grandchild unit gave `stack:new (src/sync/cancellation_token.rs:144)`, 1320 blocks and 95040 bytes over three steps (72 bytes per unit), tied to F6 (through the recorded writer at that line, not the function hint), and the flow `ledger add --sibling-of`, `ledger test`, `alloc-test`, `ledger refute`, `ledger confirm F6 --evidence <the stack evidence>` ended with F6 confirmed | The shim keeps 8 frames after its own: a stack whose project frame is deeper than that (std inlines several levels) has no project site and ties to nothing, and inlined std code can hide the project frame (an allocation the optimizer removes leaves no stack at all). The extra processes make the allocating unit about ten times slower, in those processes only. `dla alloc-test` now prints the newest growth evidence that `ledger confirm` accepts as tied (`growth_evidence` uses `tied_to_owner` first); the unit test `the_growth_evidence_to_confirm_with_is_the_newest_tied_one_not_a_newer_whole_process_one` fails with the old choice. The trend capture hint is not printed once the harness has `out/p*-alloc/shim-dump.jsonl` |
| The gate counted columns: 810 missing items, one open item and the next queue batch to do | corrected | A real grpc-go run's final gate listed 810 items (569 whole-process columns and 137 residual rows that wait on two open items, 64 queue candidates that wait on one batch); the model read "810", tried to stop eleven times and gave up with 146 of 180 minutes left. `gate check` (every stage and `--final`), `run status`, the report's status and the stop hook text now count actions: items that wait for the same thing are one line with a count and three example ids (columns and residual rows on their open item, candidates on the queue, findings with one fix), ordered queue, growth, triggers, open items, findings, the rest. `missing_total` is the number of actions; `missing_dependents` and `missing_items` hold the rest and the full list stays in the stored gate file (`missing`; `actions` is the grouped list). `go.released` (and any `not_a_growth_signal` column) is no longer listed as not flat. The stop-hook digest is over the actions' ids and the ledger lines that are not trend or residual records, so a trend finishing in the background does not restart the three-attempt count. On a copy of the real ledger the final gate lists 10 actions and 778 dependents (788 items); the report status reads `INCOMPLETE (10 open)`. The bench's final phase (`bench/runner/src/dynamic/policy.rs`) did: add the finding, resolve it unresolved, and nothing for a `growth` item (the arm did not exist); it now takes the route the refusal or the item prints (duplicate when offered and accepted, else the printed commands up to `dla alloc-test H --finding F`, then the `ledger` commands the alloc-test prints as `next`; a command with a placeholder is not run). No fixture, answer key, expected failure, policy or pass criterion changed. The untied growing columns of a summary series (source kind `dump`) are one `trend_column` action per evidence (count, the three largest, one `ledger add --origin growth-series --evidence E`, whose finding takes the largest column's site and is listed with the others in the report); a finding the evidence ties to that an alloc-test decided makes them context. Measured series keep today's rule; `grown.rs`, the `growth-not-measured` refusal and the duplicate route are unchanged for them (a summary has no harness, so the pass-8 rule does not apply to it). Tests: `a_start_up_registry_the_unit_feeds_directly_is_the_direct_reading`, `a_handler_no_project_code_calls_is_a_public_entry_and_is_reproduced`, `a_run_time_caller_with_a_key_from_a_parameter_is_reproduced`, `without_census_facts_on_the_writer_neither_reading_is_given` (trigger_tests.rs), `untied_stack_columns_of_one_capture_summary_are_one_action`, `the_stacks_of_a_summary_whose_finding_an_alloc_test_decided_need_nothing` (grown_chain.rs); each failed with its part reversed (the facts rule, the missing-facts arm, the grouping, the alloc-test exemption) | The run directory's series and trend files were not in the copy, so the gate's new count is derived from `final.json`, not re-run |
| The total of a capture summary stayed in the final gate with a fix that is not a command (go-subscription-workers) | corrected | Pass 11 moved the untied group columns of a summary to the capture's one item, which lists nothing when the capture is decided (an alloc-tested finding ties to the evidence) or stood for (a growth-series finding supports it); the total's own test (`group_columns_state`) still asked every non-flat group to be tied to a dispositioned finding, so a second untied non-flat group had no item and the total waited for it with "tie the group columns first (their own trend_column items)". One predicate, `capture_accounted`, now serves both; a group accounted for by its capture counts for its total. When the total is still listed its fix is the capture's own `dla ledger add --origin growth-series --evidence E` line (the same string as the capture item, so `actions` makes them one) or `dla gate check --final` with the finding whose disposition it waits for named after it (never a closing command: the finding's own item gives its route). Other readers: only `classify` reads the group state (the stage gate skips totals; the `.pre_gc` twin goes through the same predicate); `trend_cmd.rs` prints no next line for a total, as before; `close-rest`, `resolve`, `run status` and the report read gate items or columns, not the group state.
